## Environment

Decided in the first dialog as drafted, E1 to E14. Steps of an OP-01 call, referenced as
*s1* to *s4*: s1 key reduction (ν, spec 052), s2 classification of the key
against the store, s3 content transfer (the whole read's, or each read on a
stream), s4 the stream's close.

| ID | Actor | Action |
|---|---|---|
| `OP-01/E1` | O2 | Creates or replaces an item through the API, whole or streamed |
| `OP-01/E2` | O2 | Deletes an item or a folder tree, or moves an entity away from or onto a key, through the API |
| `OP-01/E3` | O3 | Replaces an item's content as one step in the backing storage (a new object version, a rename over the file) |
| `OP-01/E4` | O3 | Modifies an item in place: truncates it, overwrites a range, appends (file-system backends only) |
| `OP-01/E5` | O3 | Deletes an item or folder, or puts a folder where an item was or the reverse |
| `OP-01/E6` | O3 | Changes access rights so the caller may no longer read a key |
| `OP-01/E7` | World | The backend cannot be reached: connection refused, name resolution fails, connect never completes |
| `OP-01/E8` | World | An established connection is lost or reset mid-call, a stream's included |
| `OP-01/E9` | World | The backend stalls: the connection stays open and no data arrives |
| `OP-01/E10` | World | The backend rejects a request transiently: throttling, a server error, busy |
| `OP-01/E11` | World | Credentials are rejected or expire, possibly mid-stream |
| `OP-01/E12` | World | A transfer ends with fewer bytes than announced, or fails an integrity check. Amended by OP-01/D99 |
| `OP-01/E13` | World | A local resource fails: disk I/O error, no memory, no file descriptors |
| `OP-01/E14` | Runtime | O1's in-flight call or a read on its stream is cancelled from outside the call (task cancellation, interrupt) |
| `OP-01/E15` | O3 | Moves an item to a tier or state the backend will not read without a further step (an archive tier, a missing customer key); added by `OP-01/D19` |
| `OP-01/E16` | O3 | Changes an item's metadata or system properties without its content; added by `OP-01/D62`. Amended by OP-01/D84 |
| `OP-01/E17` | O3 | Stores objects so that one key is both an item and a folder, such as `a.txt` and `a.txt/b` (object stores); added by `OP-01/D67` |
| `OP-01/E18` | O2 | Changes an item's metadata or system properties through the API (OP-20, where offered); added by `OP-01/D86` |
| `OP-01/E19` | O3 | Creates an item at an absent key as one step (a PUT of a new object, a rename onto a free name); added by `OP-01/D87` |

## Decisions

### OP-01/D1: Forms (AX-1)

- **Scenario:** a caller reads a 2 KB config as text, and a 40 GB item chunk
  by chunk.
- **Options:** (a) whole bytes, whole text, a sequential binary stream;
  (b) (a) plus a text stream: the library owns streaming decode (an encoding
  split across chunks, newline handling) and its failure phase, more surface
  to spec and test in both forms; (c) bytes and stream only: every caller
  decodes, which narrows N-01, a need that names text explicitly.
- **Recommendation:** (a). Whole bytes and text serve N-01, the stream N-02; a
  text stream is the caller wrapping the binary one. Source: catalogue N-01,
  N-02; prior art object_store `get` (bytes or stream), OpenDAL read and
  reader, pyarrow `open_input_stream`, none with a text stream.
- **Decision:** (a). OP-01 offers three operations: whole bytes, whole text,
  and a binary stream, each in a sync and an async form. None takes an offset
  or range: reading at offsets is OP-02's.
- **Consistency check:** a question no axis raises (forms), asked under AX-1
  per the Rules; name it when the axes are revised with F2. Agrees with
  OPS-001, OPS-002 and the catalogue's OP-01/OP-02 cut. That both forms
  behave alike is this entry's to state (shared.md Lands elsewhere, Sync and
  async parity): every later decision binds both unless it says otherwise.
- **Open items:** none.
- **Amended by:** OP-01/D69

### OP-01/D2: The stream's shape (AX-1)

- **Scenario:** a caller passes the stream to `shutil.copyfileobj`, wraps it
  for line-by-line text, or hands it to pyarrow's Parquet reader, which seeks.
- **Options:** (a) a sequential binary file object: `read(n)`, `readinto`,
  `readline` and line iteration, `close`, context manager, not seekable,
  working with `copyfileobj`, `TextIOWrapper` and pandas; (b) a chunk
  iterator only, simple, but other libraries cannot read it as a file;
  (c) the file object of (a) plus an explicit chunk iterator: two
  consumption styles to keep consistent per state (AX-6). Follow-up, whether
  the file object seeks: (i) not seekable, Parquet reads through OP-02's
  reader; (ii) seekable, one object serving N-02 and N-04, which folds OP-02
  into OP-01, a catalogue reopen, and makes every pinning and resume decision
  cover random access; (iii) forward-only seek, which Parquet's footer-first
  read does not satisfy.
- **Recommendation:** (a), then (i): (a) is what other libraries read as a
  file; (i) keeps OP-01 one forward pass, pinnable as one version, and keeps
  the catalogue cut. Source: Python `io.BufferedIOBase`;
  fsspec `open`, pyarrow `NativeFile`; catalogue OP-01/OP-02 and the session
  input's "pyarrow splits sequential from random reads".
- **Decision:** (c) and (i): a sequential, non-seekable binary file object
  that also offers an explicit chunk iterator. The maintainer's reason: it
  fits every need known so far. Random access, Parquet's included, is
  OP-02's.
- **Consistency check:** keeps the catalogue cut (no reopen). Both
  consumption styles, `read` and the chunk iterator, draw from one position,
  so AX-6 states every state's cell for each.
- **Open items:** `OP-01/O1`.
- **Amended by:** OP-01/D69, OP-01/D70

### OP-01/D3: The text form's parameters (AX-1)

- **Scenario:** an item holds Latin-1 bytes; the caller's locale is cp1252.
- **Options:** (a) `encoding` default UTF-8 and `errors` default strict,
  both fixed by the library, never the locale; (b) UTF-8 strict with no
  parameters: the smallest surface, any other encoding being the caller
  decoding the bytes form; (c) `encoding` defaulting to the locale, so the
  same item decodes differently per host.
- **Recommendation:** (a): one input reads alike on every host. Source:
  PEP 686 (UTF-8 default), `bytes.decode`'s parameters; PEP 597 against (c).
- **Decision:** (a).
- **Consistency check:** consistent with D1. What the decoded value is, from
  bytes and the two parameters, is AX-2's; what a decode failure surfaces as
  is AX-10's.
- **Open items:** none.

### OP-01/D4: The root (AX-1)

- **Scenario:** `read('')` or `read('a/..')`, which ν reduces to ε, and
  Ω_S(ε) = Found(r, folder).
- **Options:** (a) no special clause: the root fails as any folder key does;
  (b) refused before any backend contact, like a refused key: cheaper, but a
  second category for one folder, and the root behaves unlike every other
  folder.
- **Recommendation:** (a): the root fails exactly as any folder key does,
  after classification, with no clause of its own. Source: KEY-013 and spec 052 Notes (The root),
  TREE-002.
- **Decision:** (a). The addressed structure: OP-01 is meaningful exactly for
  an accepted input k = ν_c(p) with Ω_S(k) = Found(e, item); every other
  pre-state is a failure whose category AX-3 and AX-10 fix.
- **Consistency check:** consistent with KEY-001 (refusal by ν is a function
  of input and configuration, so it needs no store state) and KEY-013.
- **Open items:** none.
- **Amended by:** OP-01/D88

### OP-01/D5: The text value (AX-2)

- **Scenario:** an item holds `b'\xef\xbb\xbfa\r\nb'`, read as text with the
  defaults.
- **Options:** (a) exactly `bytes.decode(encoding, errors)`: `'\ufeffa\r\nb'`;
  (b) universal newlines as `open()`: `'\ufeffa\nb'`, so the text form no
  longer round-trips with the bytes form, and a write of the result changes
  the item; (c) strip a UTF-8 BOM by default: `'a\r\nb'`, convenient for
  Windows-made files, but an implicit rewrite of content that `utf-8-sig`
  already offers on request.
- **Recommendation:** (a): a pure function of the bytes form's value and
  D3's parameters, the same on every backend and host, and round-tripping
  with the bytes form. Source: `bytes.decode`; AX-2's normative form.
- **Decision:** (a). text(k, enc, err) = decode(bytes(k), enc, err), where
  bytes(k) is what the bytes form would return for the same version (AX-8);
  no newline translation, a BOM kept unless the caller names `utf-8-sig`.
- **Consistency check:** consistent with D1 and D3. Total over every byte
  string with `errors` other than strict; under strict, a decode failure is
  AX-10's.
- **Open items:** none.

### OP-01/D6: `read(n)` on the stream (AX-2)

- **Scenario:** 10 bytes remain and the backend's next network chunk holds 3;
  the caller calls `read(8)`.
- **Options:** (a) exactly n bytes unless fewer remain, `b''` only at the
  end, `read()` or `read(-1)` the rest; (b) 1 to n bytes, raw semantics:
  every caller then loops, and code written for regular files breaks
  quietly.
- **Recommendation:** (a): what callers of a buffered file expect. Source:
  `io.BufferedIOBase.read`; fsspec `AbstractBufferedFile`.
- **Decision:** (a). With r bytes remaining, `read(n)` for n ≥ 0 returns
  min(n, r) bytes, the next ones of the content; `read(n)` for n < 0 returns
  all r. Bytes returned across all reads and chunks, in order, are a prefix of
  the content, the whole content once `b''` is returned.
- **Consistency check:** consistent with D2 (one position for `read` and
  chunks). "The content" is the one version AX-8 fixes.
- **Open items:** none.
- **Amended by:** OP-01/D157

### OP-01/D7: The chunk iterator (AX-2)

- **Scenario:** the caller iterates chunks of size 1 MiB over a 2.5 MiB item.
- **Options:** (a) every chunk non-empty and at most size bytes, their
  concatenation the remaining content; (b) exactly size, the last shorter:
  testable and predictable, at one re-buffer per chunk; (c) no size
  parameter: chunks of the backend's own size, so the caller cannot bound one
  chunk's memory.
- **Recommendation:** (a): boundaries follow the transfer, with no re-buffer.
  Source: object_store's byte stream; aiohttp `iter_chunked`.
- **Decision:** (a). The iterator takes a positive size; it yields non-empty
  chunks of at most size bytes, starting at the stream's current position,
  whose concatenation is the content from there to the end, and stops at the
  end. Exact chunk sizes are not promised (AX-14).
- **Consistency check:** consistent with D2 and D6: chunks and `read` advance
  one position. A non-positive size is a caller error, AX-10's.
- **Open items:** none.

### OP-01/D8: Missing keys (AX-3)

- **Scenario:** `a.txt` is an item; the caller reads `x` (Absent), `p/q/x`
  with no `p` (AbsentAncestor) and `a.txt/b` (BlockedByItem).
- **Options:** (a) one NotFound category for all three; (b) NotFound
  carrying kⱼ, which costs a listing or stat walk on object stores;
  (c) three distinct categories, at the same cost, so callers must catch
  three things for "not there".
- **Recommendation:** (a): a reader can act on none of the differences, and
  on S3 all three are one 404. Source: spec 052 Notes (Resolution's
  outcomes: BlockedByItem is apart for writes); object_store `NotFound`.
- **Decision:** (a).
- **Consistency check:** consistent with KEY-013; no ground clause makes the
  three outcomes observable through every operation. Category names here are
  working names; their home is the error layer (AX-10).
- **Open items:** none.
- **Amended by:** OP-01/D75

### OP-01/D9: A folder key, and the partition (AX-3)

- **Scenario:** `d/f` is an item, so `d` is a folder; on S3 a GET on `d`
  answers 404. The caller reads `d`, or the root (D4).
- **Options:** (a) its own category, not an item; object-store backends tell
  a folder from absence with one listing request on the failure path only;
  (b) NotFound, as object_store does: no extra request, but `exists` and
  `read` then disagree about one key; (c) profile-declared, not-an-item only
  where the backend tells folders natively: one caller code path behaves
  differently per backend.
- **Recommendation:** (a): it agrees with OP-09, which reports `d` as an
  existing folder. Source: TREE-001, KEY-013 Found(e, folder); session input
  pitfall "object_store returns NotFound on a folder path".
- **Decision:** (a). The complete partition, every form, by pre-state at
  classification (s2; AX-8 fixes when that instant is):

  | Pre-state | Outcome |
  |---|---|
  | ν_c(p) refused (KEY-002, KEY-004, KEY-005) | failure: key refused, the category shared with every operation |
  | Ω_S(k) = Found(e, item) | success: e's content (empty for an empty item), as bytes, as text (D5), or as a stream positioned at 0 |
  | Ω_S(k) = Found(e, folder), the root included | failure: not an item |
  | Ω_S(k) = Absent, AbsentAncestor(kⱼ) or BlockedByItem(kⱼ) | failure: not found |

  The rows are disjoint and complete: ν refuses or accepts (KEY-001), and
  KEY-013 gives exactly one Ω case, Found split by TREE-001's partition.
  Failures that depend on the environment rather than the pre-state (access,
  transport, decode) are AX-10's.
- **Consistency check:** consistent with D4 and D8. The key-refused row
  follows shared.md Lands elsewhere ("An unacceptable key refused identically
  by every operation"). The extra listing request is a request count, AX-14's.
- **Open items:** none.
- **Amended by:** OP-01/D67, OP-01/D73

### OP-01/D10: The store's frame (AX-4)

- **Scenario:** O1 reads `a.txt` (success) and `d` (not an item); on Local
  the read bumps the file's atime, on S3 it adds a GET to the access log.
- **Options:** (a) nothing any API call observes changes; effects seen only
  out of band are registered non-promises; (b) also promise no out-of-band
  effect, which needs `noatime` on Local and is impossible for S3 logs, so no
  check could pass on every backend.
- **Recommendation:** (a): it promises what every backend holds, success or
  failure, and registers the out-of-band effects no backend set can avoid.
  Source: spec 051's store tuple; RFC 9110 § 9.2.1
  (GET is safe).
- **Decision:** (a). After any OP-01 call, successful or failed, and after
  any use or close of its stream, the store's state (U, F, I, r, C, name) and
  every property OP-09 and OP-20 report are those the environment alone
  leaves. Atime, access logs and billing are AX-14's.
- **Consistency check:** consistent with D9 (failures change nothing either)
  and spec 051. Binds OP-09 and OP-20: modification time must be one that a
  read leaves alone, so neither may report atime as it.
- **Open items:** `OP-01/O2`.
- **Amended by:** OP-01/D65, OP-01/D81, OP-01/D129, OP-01/D152, OP-01/D162

### OP-01/D11: The handle's state (AX-4)

- **Scenario:** O1 reads `a.txt`, O3 replaces it (E3), O1 reads again through
  the same handle.
- **Options:** (a) the handle holds only configuration and lifetime: no
  content or metadata cache; (b) an opt-in cache per store, whose reads are
  stale by up to its TTL, a profile variation every entry's AX-8 must state;
  (c) an s3fs-style metadata cache with expiry, whose reads can classify
  against a stale view, the pitfall the research recorded.
- **Recommendation:** (a): the second read classifies and transfers afresh
  and sees the replacement, subject to AX-8 and AX-9. Source: object_store `ObjectStore`, gocloud
  `Bucket`; session input pitfall "s3fs added ETag expiry checks".
- **Decision:** (a). A handle holds, between calls, its configuration and
  its lifetime and nothing else that changes an operation's outcome;
  connection pools and credential refresh may be held but change none. Every
  call classifies and transfers afresh. The maintainer's reason: caching, if
  wanted, is a later extension that owns its own state; the store handle
  stays clean.
- **Consistency check:** binds every operation, so listed as a shared
  decision (`OP-01/O3`). Consistent with spec 053 Notes (Extensions: an
  extension adds a feature on top of the entries).
- **Open items:** `OP-01/O3`.

### OP-01/D12: The timeout model (AX-5)

- **Scenario:** E9, the backend accepts the connection and sends nothing;
  separately, a healthy 40 GB read takes 20 minutes.
- **Options:** (a) a connect timeout and an idle (no-progress) timeout, no
  whole-call deadline; (b) (a) plus a whole-call deadline T_total, so a large
  healthy read fails by its size and the caller must size the deadline per
  item; (c) each backend client's own defaults, profile-declared, so one
  caller's code waits 20 s on one backend and forever on another.
- **Recommendation:** (a): a progressing transfer never fails by its size.
  Source: botocore connect and read timeouts; Azure 20 s connect, 60 s read
  (session input, Q4 Timeouts).
- **Decision:** (a). Under a quiet environment every OP-01 call, and every
  read on its stream, returns or fails. A network backend's request fails with
  the timeout category when connecting takes longer than T_connect, or when no
  byte arrives for T_idle while the library waits on the backend; time the
  caller spends between stream reads never counts. No whole-call deadline: the
  maintainer leaves one to a later convenience extension or a
  profile-declared backend option.
- **Consistency check:** binds every operation (shared.md Lands elsewhere, A
  timeout bound: "a bound shared by every operation, OP-01's flow"), so
  listed as `OP-01/O4`. The bound is per backend request; how many requests a
  retrying call makes is AX-13's, which therefore bounds the call's total.
  Total duration is AX-14's.
- **Open items:** `OP-01/O4`.
- **Amended by:** OP-01/D57, OP-01/D58, OP-01/D59, OP-01/D128

### OP-01/D13: The timeout defaults (AX-5)

- **Scenario:** a store configured with no timeouts.
- **Options:** (a) T_connect 20 s, T_idle 60 s for every network backend;
  (b) 10 s and 30 s, failing faster on a dead backend, though a throttled S3
  or a cold Azure read sometimes needs longer; (c) each backend's own default,
  a profile row: fewer surprises for users of the native SDK, less
  portability.
- **Recommendation:** (a): one library default for every network backend,
  within the range comparables use. Source: session input, Q4 Timeouts (object_store
  30 s, OpenDAL 60 s, botocore 60 s, Azure 20 s and 60 s, GCS 60 s).
- **Decision:** (a). Both are store configuration; these are the defaults.
- **Consistency check:** consistent with D11 (configuration is handle state
  that may change an outcome). Shared with D12, `OP-01/O4`.
- **Open items:** `OP-01/O4`.

### OP-01/D14: Local I/O termination (AX-5)

- **Scenario:** a Local store on an NFS mount whose server vanished; a read
  syscall blocks in the kernel.
- **Options:** (a) a profile deviation: termination rests on the OS's I/O
  completing, no library timeout; (b) a library timeout through a helper
  thread: a uniform promise, but each local read crosses a thread, and a
  timed-out syscall leaves a blocked thread behind.
- **Recommendation:** (a): the library cannot time out a blocked syscall
  without a helper thread, and network backends keep the normal value.
  Source: AX-15's form; pyarrow `LocalFileSystem`
  and fsspec's local file system time out no read.
- **Decision:** (a). Normal value: D12's bound. Declared deviation: Local,
  and the server-side file system behind SFTP once the SFTP request is sent,
  terminate when the OS's I/O completes.
- **Consistency check:** a variation point, so recorded again under AX-15.
  An SFTP request still meets D12's T_idle on the network side; only a server
  that keeps the channel alive while its disk hangs falls under the
  deviation.
- **Open items:** none.
- **Amended by:** OP-01/D145

### OP-01/D15: The Closed state (AX-6)

- **Scenario:** the caller leaves a `with` block, then calls `read(10)` and
  `close()` again.
- **Options:** (a) Python io semantics: reads raise `ValueError`, a second
  close is a no-op; (b) a library "closed" category, which breaks code that
  expects file-object behaviour (wrappers check `ValueError`), with no gain.
- **Recommendation:** (a): nothing about the store happened, and wrappers
  expect file-object behaviour. Source: `io.IOBase.close`.
- **Decision:** (a), in both forms.
- **Consistency check:** consistent with D2 (a file object). `ValueError` is
  a caller error, not a storage category (AX-10).
- **Open items:** none.
- **Amended by:** OP-01/D117

### OP-01/D16: The Failed state (AX-6)

- **Scenario:** a stream read surfaces a transport failure the library's own
  recovery (AX-13) could not mend; the caller catches it and reads again.
- **Options:** (a) sticky: every later read raises the same category, close
  works; (b) later reads try to resume: two places that resume, inside a read
  and across reads, each needing its own pinning rule; (c) the stream becomes
  Closed, so the original failure is lost to code that reads on.
- **Recommendation:** (a): the caller never gets bytes after a gap, and
  recovery has one place, inside a read. Source: Go `bufio.Reader`'s sticky
  error.
- **Decision:** (a). A caller who wants to go on makes a new OP-01 call.
- **Consistency check:** consistent with D6 (returned bytes are a prefix of
  the content). Recovery inside a read is AX-13's.
- **Open items:** none.
- **Amended by:** OP-01/D51, OP-01/D52, OP-01/D117

### OP-01/D17: An unclosed stream (AX-6)

- **Scenario:** the caller drops the last reference to an open stream.
- **Options:** (a) released when collected, with a `ResourceWarning`;
  (b) released silently, so leaked streams in long-running code go unnoticed
  until the pool runs dry.
- **Recommendation:** (a): the connection or descriptor is released either
  way, and the warning shows a leak before the pool runs dry. Source: CPython
  file objects.
- **Decision:** (a). When collection happens is AX-14's.
- **Consistency check:** consistent with D10: release changes no store state.
- **Open items:** none.

### OP-01/D18: The handle's lifetime ends while a stream is open (AX-6)

- **Scenario:** a stream is open, its handle is closed, then
  `stream.read(10)`.
- **Options:** (a) leave the cell open for OP-21's session; (b) decide now
  that the stream outlives the handle, so OP-21 must keep the connection
  alive for open streams; (c) decide now that it fails, so OP-21 must
  invalidate every stream it handed out.
- **Recommendation:** (a): OP-21 decides how a lifetime ends for every
  handed-out resource at once. Source: shared.md § Lifetime.
- **Decision:** (a). The stream's transitions, the open cells excepted:

  | State | `read`, `readinto`, `readline`, chunks | `close` | handle's lifetime ends |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | Open (bytes remain) | next bytes (D6, D7); a failure moves to Failed | releases; Closed | open: `OP-01/O5` |
  | Exhausted (at end) | `b''`, an empty iteration | releases; Closed | open: `OP-01/O5` |
  | Failed | the same failure again (D16) | releases; Closed | open: `OP-01/O5` |
  | Closed | `ValueError` (D15) | no-op | no effect |

  A stream starts Open, or Exhausted for an empty item; dropping it unclosed
  releases it when collected (D17). Cancellation is AX-7's.
- **Consistency check:** consistent with D15 to D17. Lifetime binds every
  operation that hands out a resource, so `OP-01/O5` is shared.
- **Open items:** `OP-01/O5`.
- **Amended by:** OP-01/D50, OP-01/D51, OP-01/D70, OP-01/D100, OP-01/D117, OP-01/D132, OP-01/D158, OP-01/D170

### OP-01/D19: An item that exists but cannot be read now (AX-7)

- **Scenario:** O3 moves `cold.bin` to S3 Glacier Flexible Retrieval or Azure
  Archive; OP-09 still sees the item, but a GET answers "restore first".
- **Options:** (a) add E15, O3 makes an item unreadable in place;
  (b) fold it into E6 as permission denied, so callers try to fix
  credentials for a restore problem; (c) out of scope, the native error
  surfacing through the catch-all category, not one of its own.
- **Recommendation:** (a): no row E1 to E14 covers an item that exists but
  cannot be read now, and AX-10 then gives it a category. Source: S3 `InvalidObjectState`, Azure
  `BlobArchived`.
- **Decision:** (a): E15 joins the Environment. Exposure of OP-01's steps
  (s1 reduction, s2 classification, s3 transfer, s4 close):

  | Row | s1 | s2 | s3 | s4 | Home |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | E1, E2 (O2) | — | yes | yes | — | AX-8 |
  | E3, E4, E5 (O3) | — | yes | yes | — | AX-9 |
  | E6, E15 (O3) | — | yes | yes | — | AX-10 |
  | E7 to E13 (world) | — | yes | yes | — | AX-10 to AX-13 |
  | E14 (runtime) | yes | yes | yes | — | D20 |

  s1 is a function of input and configuration (KEY-001), so only E14 reaches
  it; s4 releases a resource and reports nothing (AX-11).
- **Consistency check:** E15 is the first Environment addition by decision
  (axes.md § Vocabulary). Its category is AX-10's. No other entry's list is
  admitted to the ground yet, so there is none to compare with.
- **Open items:** none.
- **Amended by:** OP-01/D49, OP-01/D62, OP-01/D67, OP-01/D86, OP-01/D87, OP-01/D89

### OP-01/D20: Cancellation (AX-7)

- **Scenario:** an asyncio task awaiting `stream.read(1 MiB)` is cancelled
  with bytes half-received; or `KeyboardInterrupt` in the sync form.
- **Options:** (a) the stream becomes Closed, its resources released;
  (b) Failed, sticky, which needs an "interrupted" category and holds the
  connection until close; (c) Open with
  the position unchanged, which no backend transport can promise.
- **Recommendation:** (a): nothing claims a position no one can know.
  Source: unsourced (asyncio promises no position after a cancelled read).
- **Decision:** (a). Cancellation propagates to the caller unchanged; a
  cancelled stream read releases the stream, which is then Closed (D15). A
  cancelled whole read or stream open hands out nothing and changes nothing
  (D10).
- **Consistency check:** adds a transition to D18's table: Open, any read,
  cancelled → Closed. Consistent with D16 (cancellation is no storage
  failure, so not Failed).
- **Open items:** none.
- **Amended by:** OP-01/D50, OP-01/D81

### OP-01/D21: A whole read overlapping a write (AX-8)

- **Scenario:** `a` holds v1; O2 replaces it with v2 (E1) while O1's whole
  read of `a` is in flight.
- **Options:** (a) one version, the content at one instant inside the read's
  interval; (b) one version only against OP-04 writes, a mix possible during
  an OP-03 write on file-system backends: weaker, cheaper writes on Local, and
  callers who need integrity must use OP-04.
- **Recommendation:** (a): v1 or v2, never a mix; object stores give it
  natively, Local needs temp-and-rename. Source: session input, Q4 Consistency stated
  ("atomic put (object_store)"); Herlihy and Wing, linearizability.
- **Decision:** (a). For every history of O1's whole read (bytes or text)
  with overlapping O2 calls, there is an instant t inside the read's interval
  such that the outcome is D9's row for the pre-state at t, with the content
  the item had at t. Classification and content come from that one instant.
- **Consistency check:** requires every API mutation to change an item's
  content atomically as a reader sees it, so no reader meets a state between
  two contents: a constraint on OP-03 to OP-05 and OP-12 to OP-15
  (`OP-01/O6`). O3's rights are AX-9's.
- **Open items:** `OP-01/O6`.

### OP-01/D22: A stream overlapping a write (AX-8)

- **Scenario:** O1 opens a stream on `a` (v1) and reads 1 GB; O2 replaces `a`
  with v2 or deletes it; the stream needs a new backend request for the rest.
- **Options:** (a) pinned to the version current at open, or the next read
  fails with a "changed" category; (b) pinned and bound to complete, which a
  non-versioned bucket cannot give after a reconnect; (c) an unspecified mix,
  bytes from v1 then v2 the caller cannot tell apart, the pitfall itself.
- **Recommendation:** (a): every byte comes from the version current at open,
  and bytes after a gap or from v2 are never delivered. Source: session input pitfall "the view during a
  streamed read is undefined unless pinned; s3fs added ETag expiry checks".
- **Decision:** (a). The stream is one interval operation with one output:
  the open classifies at one instant t₀ inside the open call (D9's rows), and
  every byte the stream delivers is a byte of the content at t₀, at its
  position. If the backend can no longer serve that content, the read that
  needs it fails with the changed category and the stream is Failed (D16);
  bytes from another content, or after a gap, are never delivered.
- **Consistency check:** consistent with D6, D16, D21. A stream on an empty
  item is Exhausted at open and meets nothing later (D18). Whether a backend
  can keep serving the pinned content after a reconnect is a variation
  point (AX-15); the changed category is AX-10's.
- **Open items:** none.
- **Amended by:** OP-01/D61, OP-01/D62

### OP-01/D23: A read after a completed write (AX-8)

- **Scenario:** O2's write of v2 to `a` returns; then O1 starts a read of
  `a`.
- **Options:** (a) normal value: that write's content or a later one, an
  eventually consistent backend declaring the deviation; (b) always strong,
  no deviation, so a backend without strong consistency cannot be a v1
  backend; (c) eventual for every backend, so callers must poll on every
  backend.
- **Recommendation:** (a): strong read-after-write as the normal value, with a
  backend without it (some S3-compatible stores) declaring the deviation
  (AX-15). Source: session input, Q4 Consistency stated
  ("strong read-after-write including list (S3, Azure, GCS)").
- **Decision:** (a). Normal value: the instant t of D21 and t₀ of D22 lies
  after every API mutation that completed before the read started (real-time
  order); in particular O1 reads its own completed writes. Declared
  deviation: eventual, the read may see an earlier content for a bounded or
  unbounded time, as the backend's profile states.
- **Consistency check:** binds every write and every read entry, so shared:
  `OP-01/O7`. A profile variation (AX-15).
- **Open items:** `OP-01/O7`.

### OP-01/D24: One handle, concurrent callers (AX-8)

- **Scenario:** eight threads share one Store, or eight tasks one AsyncStore
  on its event loop, each reading.
- **Options:** (a) safe for concurrent calls, each behaving as if issued
  alone under D21 to D23; (b) one caller at a time per handle: cheaper to
  implement, but connection pools multiply and misuse fails silently.
- **Recommendation:** (a): each call behaves as if issued alone, and (b)'s
  per-caller handles multiply connection pools. Source: object_store (`Send + Sync`), gocloud
  `Bucket`, boto3 clients.
- **Decision:** (a). Calls through one handle may overlap, from any threads
  (Store) or from tasks on one event loop (AsyncStore); O2 may be the same
  handle as O1. One stream serves one consumer at a time; concurrent use of
  one stream is not promised (AX-14).
- **Consistency check:** consistent with D11 (no per-call state in the
  handle). Binds every operation: `OP-01/O8`.
- **Open items:** `OP-01/O8`.
- **Amended by:** OP-01/D81, OP-01/D129

### OP-01/D25: O3 replaces or deletes in one step (AX-9)

- **Scenario:** during O1's stream on Local, O3 runs `mv new a` (E3) or
  `rm a` (E5); on S3, O3 PUTs or DELETEs `a` with the AWS CLI.
- **Options:** (a) the same rights as O2; (b) weaker, unspecified for O3:
  simpler to state, but most stores are written by other tools too, and the
  guarantee already holds for free.
- **Recommendation:** (a): it holds natively, since an open file descriptor
  keeps the old file and an S3 range read can be pinned by ETag. Source:
  POSIX `rename` and `unlink`; S3 `If-Match`.
- **Decision:** (a). D21 to D23 apply with O3's E3, E5 and E15 in place of
  O2's E1 and E2: one content per whole read, a stream pinned to its open's
  content or failing with the changed category. D23's real-time order is
  promised only for API mutations; when an O3 change becomes visible is the
  backend's.
- **Consistency check:** consistent with D21 to D23; adds no backend
  requirement beyond D22's variation point.
- **Open items:** none.
- **Amended by:** OP-01/D47, OP-01/D61, OP-01/D63, OP-01/D64, OP-01/D65, OP-01/D87

### OP-01/D26: O3 modifies in place (AX-9)

- **Scenario:** during O1's whole read or stream of a 10 GB file on Local or
  SFTP, O3 truncates it to 4 GB, or overwrites bytes 1 GB to 2 GB (E4).
- **Options:** (a) weaker rights, stated: each delivered byte is the file's
  byte at its position when it was read, no failure promised; (b) (a) plus
  best-effort size and mtime detection, failing changed on a difference:
  catches most in-place edits but not all (mtime granularity, a preserved
  mtime), so still no promise, at one extra stat per read; (c) the same
  rights as O2, which needs a snapshot or lock neither backend offers.
- **Recommendation:** (a): the library cannot pin against an in-place change,
  so it states the weaker rights rather than promise a detection it cannot
  guarantee. Source: POSIX `read(2)` gives no isolation from
  concurrent writers.
- **Decision:** (a). Under E4 during an OP-01 call or stream, D21's single
  instant and D22's pinning are deliberately not promised. What remains: every
  delivered byte is the byte the file held at that position at some instant
  between the open and that byte's delivery; the read ends at the end it
  meets; the call may complete without failing. A non-promise, scoped to E4
  on file-system backends (AX-14).
- **Consistency check:** narrows D21 and D22 for O3 only, so O3 has fewer
  rights than O2 (axes.md AX-9's open question, answered for OP-01). E4
  cannot arise on object stores, which have no in-place modification.
- **Open items:** none.
- **Amended by:** OP-01/D159

### OP-01/D27: The typing principle (AX-10)

- **Scenario:** a read fails with botocore `ClientError` 404, paramiko
  `SFTPError` or `OSError` ENOENT, depending on the backend.
- **Options:** (a) library categories under one base, each also a builtin
  where one fits, the native exception chained as `__cause__`; (b) library
  categories only: a clean hierarchy, but callers of pathlib-style code learn
  new names, and `except FileNotFoundError` no longer catches; (c) builtins
  only, fsspec style: no room for changed or unavailable, which become bare
  `OSError`.
- **Recommendation:** (a): `except FileNotFoundError` keeps working, and
  categories no builtin has (changed, unavailable) still get a type. Source:
  session input, Q4 Error taxonomy (object_store `Error`, OpenDAL
  `ErrorKind`; fsspec and pyarrow builtins).
- **Decision:** (a). Every storage failure surfaces as a library category,
  never as a backend's own exception; the native exception, where there is
  one, is its `__cause__`. Builtin parents so far: not found
  `FileNotFoundError`, not an item `IsADirectoryError`, permission denied and
  authentication failed `PermissionError`, timeout `TimeoutError`.
- **Consistency check:** the error layer's first clause: shared
  (`OP-01/O9`). Category names in this record are working names until that
  layer names them.
- **Open items:** `OP-01/O9`.

### OP-01/D28: Transport failures (AX-10)

- **Scenario:** E7 unreachable, E8 reset, E10 throttled, E12 short body, each
  after the library's own recovery (AX-13) is spent; and E9 a stall past
  T_idle.
- **Options:** (a) one unavailable category, timeout its subcategory;
  (b) separate unavailable and timeout, so callers retrying later must catch
  both; (c) one category per row: precise, but backends blur them (a reset
  under throttling).
- **Recommendation:** (a): one "try later" category. Source: OpenDAL
  `ErrorKind` (temporary vs permanent); gocloud `ErrorCode`.
- **Decision:** (a). E7, E8, E10 and E12 surface as unavailable, E9 as
  timeout (D12), which is an unavailable. A short or corrupt body is never
  returned as content.
- **Consistency check:** consistent with D12 and D16 (on a stream these move
  it to Failed). Shared: `OP-01/O9`.
- **Open items:** `OP-01/O9`.
- **Amended by:** OP-01/D56

### OP-01/D29: Access failures (AX-10)

- **Scenario:** E6, the caller lacks read rights on `a`; E11, the credentials
  are rejected or expire mid-stream.
- **Options:** (a) two categories, permission denied and authentication
  failed; (b) one, permission denied: simpler, but the cause chain tells the
  two apart for a human, not for code.
- **Recommendation:** (a): the fixes differ (grant rights, renew
  credentials), and every backend keeps them apart. Source: gocloud
  `ErrorCode` `PermissionDenied` vs `Unauthenticated` (the gRPC codes).
- **Decision:** (a), both under `PermissionError` (D27).
- **Consistency check:** consistent with D27. Shared: `OP-01/O9`.
- **Open items:** `OP-01/O9`.

### OP-01/D30: An item that cannot be read now (AX-10)

- **Scenario:** E15, an archive tier or a missing customer key.
- **Options:** (a) its own category, not readable; (b) unavailable, retried
  in vain until a restore; (c) the catch-all base: no dedicated type,
  distinguishable only through the cause chain.
- **Recommendation:** (a): not not-found (OP-09 sees the item), not
  unavailable, not access; the fix is a step on the backend. Source: S3
  `InvalidObjectState`, Azure `BlobArchived`.
- **Decision:** (a).
- **Consistency check:** consistent with D19 and D27. Shared: `OP-01/O9`.
- **Open items:** `OP-01/O9`.

### OP-01/D31: The changed category (AX-10)

- **Scenario:** O2 replaced `a` mid-stream, and the stream's resume request
  fails its ETag check (D22).
- **Options:** (a) its own category, no builtin parent; (b) under
  unavailable, so callers who retry unavailable restart the read, possibly
  getting the new content without noticing it changed.
- **Recommendation:** (a): retrying the same stream cannot help, a new call
  reads the new content, and conditional entries (OP-05) can reuse it.
  Source: OpenDAL `ErrorKind::ConditionNotMatch`; HTTP 412.
- **Decision:** (a).
- **Consistency check:** consistent with D22 and D27. Shared: `OP-01/O9`.
- **Open items:** `OP-01/O9`.
- **Amended by:** OP-01/D62

### OP-01/D32: A decode failure (AX-10)

- **Scenario:** whole text, `errors='strict'`, on bytes that are not UTF-8.
- **Options:** (a) the builtin `UnicodeDecodeError`; (b) a library category
  with `UnicodeDecodeError` as parent: catchable as a store error too, but a
  category whose cause is never the store.
- **Recommendation:** (a): decoding is D5's pure function of the content,
  not a storage failure. Source: `pathlib.Path.read_text`.
- **Decision:** (a).
- **Consistency check:** consistent with D5 and D27 (it is no storage
  failure, so the principle does not wrap it).
- **Open items:** none.

### OP-01/D33: Failures that are not the store's (AX-10)

- **Scenario:** E13 on a Local read (EIO from the disk, ENOMEM, EMFILE); a
  non-str key; `chunks(size=0)`.
- **Options:** (a) an I/O error of the backing storage is the library's base
  category; `MemoryError` and fd exhaustion propagate as raised; caller
  errors are `TypeError` or `ValueError`, before any backend contact;
  (b) everything wrapped, `MemoryError` and `TypeError` included, so `except`
  on the store's base also hides bugs in the caller's code.
- **Recommendation:** (a): `except` on the store's base then never hides a
  bug in the caller's code. Source: Python's convention, programming errors
  as `TypeError` and `ValueError`.
- **Decision:** (a).
- **Consistency check:** consistent with D15 (closed stream: `ValueError`)
  and D27. The base category is the catch-all for a storage failure no named
  category fits. Shared: `OP-01/O9`.
- **Open items:** `OP-01/O9`.
- **Amended by:** OP-01/D53, OP-01/D54, OP-01/D55

### OP-01/D34: The key-refused category (AX-10)

- **Scenario:** `read('../x')` from the root (KEY-004).
- **Options:** (a) a library category, also `ValueError`; (b) a library
  category only, which `except ValueError` around argument parsing does not
  see.
- **Recommendation:** (a): an invalid argument value, refused by the store's
  configuration (L_api). Source: Python's convention for invalid argument
  values.
- **Decision:** (a). Raised before any backend contact. The categories OP-01
  surfaces, by pre-state or Environment row:

  | Pre-state or row | Category | Builtin parent |
  |---|---|---|
  | ν refuses the input | key refused | `ValueError` |
  | Absent, AbsentAncestor, BlockedByItem | not found | `FileNotFoundError` |
  | Found(e, folder) | not an item | `IsADirectoryError` |
  | E6 | permission denied | `PermissionError` |
  | E11 | authentication failed | `PermissionError` |
  | E15 | not readable | — |
  | E7, E8, E10, E12 | unavailable | — |
  | E9 | timeout, an unavailable | `TimeoutError` |
  | E1 to E5 against a pinned stream | changed | — |
  | E13, disk I/O | base category | — |
  | E13, memory or descriptors | as raised | — |
  | decode under strict | `UnicodeDecodeError` | builtin itself |
  | caller error, closed stream | `TypeError`, `ValueError` | builtin itself |
  | E14 | cancellation, as raised (D20) | builtin itself |

- **Consistency check:** consistent with D9 (shared.md "An unacceptable key
  refused identically by every operation"; this fixes the category). Every
  row of the Environment and every failing row of D9 has a category, so none
  surfaces untyped. Shared: `OP-01/O9`.
- **Open items:** `OP-01/O9`.
- **Amended by:** OP-01/D48, OP-01/D54, OP-01/D55, OP-01/D56, OP-01/D61, OP-01/D62, OP-01/D84, OP-01/D86, OP-01/D88, OP-01/D124, OP-01/D153

### OP-01/D35: Opening a stream (AX-11)

- **Scenario:** open a stream on `missing` or on folder `d`.
- **Options:** (a) every classification failure at the open call, which
  costs the first request at open; (b) at the first read, the open lazy and
  free: every caller must expect not found from a read, and an unread stream
  never learns the key was missing.
- **Recommendation:** (a): a stream the caller holds is for an item that
  existed. Source: session input pitfall "pyarrow's `allow_delayed_open`
  defers open errors to close"; Python `open()`.
- **Decision:** (a). Key refused, not found, not an item, permission denied,
  authentication failed and not readable surface from the open call, which
  makes its first backend request there. A read on the stream surfaces only
  unavailable, timeout, changed, the base category, authentication failed
  (credentials expiring mid-stream) and the caller errors; a whole read
  surfaces everything at its one call.
- **Consistency check:** consistent with D22 (t₀ lies in the open call) and
  D34's table, whose rows this splits by phase.
- **Open items:** none.
- **Amended by:** OP-01/D45, OP-01/D46, OP-01/D96

### OP-01/D36: Closing a stream (AX-11)

- **Scenario:** a stream meets E8 while releasing its connection in
  `close()`.
- **Options:** (a) `close()` never raises a storage failure; (b) it may raise
  unavailable: in a `with` block a release failure then replaces the body's
  exception, or reports a successful read as failed.
- **Recommendation:** (a): every byte delivered was already checked, and a
  release failure must not replace a `with` body's exception. Source: Python
  read-only file objects; fsspec read-mode close.
- **Decision:** (a). A failure while releasing is swallowed (logged), not
  raised; `close()`
  raises nothing but a cancellation (E14, D20).
- **Consistency check:** consistent with D18 (close releases from every
  state) and D10 (release changes no store state).
- **Open items:** none.

### OP-01/D37: A handle's start (AX-11)

- **Scenario:** a caller creates a store for an S3 bucket while offline, then
  reads.
- **Options:** (a) creation contacts no backend; unavailable surfaces at the
  first call; (b) creation connects: a handle that exists is known reachable,
  but creation fails offline and every configuration check needs the
  network.
- **Recommendation:** (a): a handle is usable from the moment the caller
  holds it, and the first operation meets E7 like any other. Source: boto3 clients and object_store builders
  (lazy connection); gocloud `OpenBucket`.
- **Decision:** (a). A handle is usable from the moment the caller holds it;
  creating it contacts no backend, so it never fails for E7 to E12.
  Reachability is OP-24's.
- **Consistency check:** binds every operation and fills part of shared.md
  § Lifetime: `OP-01/O10`. How a handle is created and configured stays the
  API spec's (BK-496).
- **Open items:** `OP-01/O10`.

### OP-01/D38: The library's own retries (AX-13)

- **Scenario:** a whole read meets E10 (S3 503 SlowDown) twice, then
  succeeds.
- **Options:** (a) built in, bounded, configurable per store; (b) none: every
  transient failure surfaces at once, and each caller writes its own loop;
  (c) each backend client's defaults, a profile row with differing attempt
  counts and retryable sets per backend.
- **Recommendation:** (a): a read changes nothing (D10), so repeating it is
  safe. Source: session input, Q4 Retries (object_store built in, timeouts
  retried only when idempotent; s3fs an allow-list); botocore standard mode,
  3 attempts.
- **Decision:** (a). E7 to E10 and E12 are retried with exponential backoff
  and jitter, honouring a backend's retry-after hint, up to a per-store
  attempt limit, default 3 attempts per backend request; only then does
  unavailable or timeout surface. Not found, not an item, key refused,
  permission denied, authentication failed, not readable and changed are
  never retried. A retried whole read still returns one content at one
  instant of its interval (D21). Retry safety rests on no mutation (D10).
  Caller-level retry of a surfaced failure, by a new call:

  | Failure | A new call |
  |---|---|
  | key refused | repeats it: a function of input and configuration (KEY-001) |
  | not found, not an item | repeats it unless the store changes |
  | permission denied, authentication failed, not readable | repeats it until the environment changes |
  | unavailable, timeout, base category | may succeed |
  | changed | reads the content current at its own open |

- **Consistency check:** consistent with D12 (the bound is per request, so a
  call's waiting is at most the attempt limit times the bound plus the
  backoff) and D28. Retry coupled to idempotency binds every entry: shared
  `OP-01/O11`.
- **Open items:** `OP-01/O11`.
- **Amended by:** OP-01/D59, OP-01/D60, OP-01/D61, OP-01/D128, OP-01/D145, OP-01/D153

### OP-01/D39: Resuming a stream (AX-13)

- **Scenario:** after 30 GB of a 40 GB stream, E8 resets the connection.
- **Options:** (a) the next read resumes from the current position with a
  request conditioned on the pinned content, failing changed if the pin no
  longer holds, unavailable where the backend cannot pin; (b) never resume:
  any mid-stream drop fails the stream, so a 40 GB read restarts from 0 and
  long streams fail in proportion to their length.
- **Recommendation:** (a): the read continues seamlessly, or fails changed
  when the pin no longer holds, so a dropped connection never yields another
  content. Source: s3fs's ETag-checked range refetch
  (session input pitfall).
- **Decision:** (a). A resume is a retry under D38's limit; once bytes have
  flowed since the last resume, the limit starts afresh. The read continues
  without a gap or a repeated byte, or fails per D22 and D28, moving the
  stream to Failed (D16).
- **Consistency check:** consistent with D6, D16, D22. Which backends can
  pin a new request is a variation point (AX-15).
- **Open items:** none.
- **Amended by:** OP-01/D72

### OP-01/D40: The stream's memory (AX-14)

- **Scenario:** a 40 GB stream read in 1 MiB pieces.
- **Options:** (a) promised: memory bounded independently of the item's
  size, no whole copy in memory or on local disk; (b) not promised: a backend
  may stage to a temp file, N-02 holds only in spirit, and disk fills on
  large reads.
- **Recommendation:** (a): N-02 asks for a read without holding the item in
  memory, which (b) gives up. Source: catalogue N-02; session input pitfall
  "cloudpathlib downloads the whole object first".
- **Decision:** (a). An open stream holds at most a per-store buffer bound,
  independent of the item's size, plus what the caller's `read(n)` or chunk
  size asks for, and never stages the item in memory or on local disk. The
  bound's value and the read-ahead are not promised (D41, row 3).
- **Consistency check:** a promise, not a non-promise, recorded under AX-14
  because the register's row 3 bounds it. Consistent with D7 and D39 (a
  resume refetches from the position, never the whole item).
- **Open items:** none.
- **Amended by:** OP-01/D71, OP-01/D133, OP-01/D136

### OP-01/D41: The non-promise register (AX-14)

- **Scenario:** a test asserts that a successful whole read is one GET, or
  that `chunks(1 MiB)` yields exactly 1 MiB pieces.
- **Options:** (a) adopt rows 1 to 8 below; (b) adopt them but promise
  minimal requests, dropping row 2: rules out D9's listing folded into the
  success path and prefetching, and makes request counts a test subject.
- **Recommendation:** (a): each is a property a caller could infer from the
  name or from `open()` and GET prior art, which no set of backends holds
  uniformly. Source: axes.md AX-14.
- **Decision:** (a). Deliberately not promised, for every OP-01 form:

  | ID | Not promised | Scope |
  |---|---|---|
  | `OP-01/NP1` | Latency and total duration | every call and stream read; D12 bounds waiting, not duration |
  | `OP-01/NP2` | The number of backend requests: D9's listing, retries (D38), resumes (D39), prefetch | every call and stream |
  | `OP-01/NP3` | Chunk boundaries beyond D7, buffer size, read-ahead | the stream |
  | `OP-01/NP4` | When an unclosed stream is released | D17 |
  | `OP-01/NP5` | Out-of-band effects: atime, access logs, billing | D10 |
  | `OP-01/NP6` | Concurrent use of one stream by several consumers | D24 |
  | `OP-01/NP7` | The content seen under O3's in-place modification, beyond D26's per-byte rule | E4, file-system backends |
  | `OP-01/NP8` | The whole forms' memory: they hold the whole content once, plus decoding for text | whole bytes and text |

- **Consistency check:** no row contradicts a decision: each names the
  decision whose limit it marks. NP2 leaves D9's extra listing free to happen
  on the failure path only or otherwise.
- **Open items:** none.
- **Amended by:** OP-01/D71, OP-01/D97, OP-01/D107, OP-01/D118, OP-01/D123, OP-01/D126, OP-01/D136, OP-01/D152, OP-01/D162, OP-01/D166

### OP-01/D42: Pinned resume on SFTP (AX-15)

- **Scenario:** an SFTP stream's connection drops at 30 GB; the file handle
  is gone, and a reopened handle cannot prove it is the same file.
- **Options:** (a) a declared deviation: no resume, the stream fails
  unavailable; (b) resume when size and mtime match: usually right, but no
  pin (mtime granularity, a preserved mtime).
- **Recommendation:** (a): (b) would let D22's "never another content" fail
  silently. Source: SFTP v3 (draft-ietf-secsh-filexfer-02) has no content
  identity.
- **Decision:** (a).
- **Consistency check:** consistent with D22 and D39 (where a backend cannot
  pin, unavailable).
- **Open items:** none.
- **Amended by:** OP-01/D75

### OP-01/D43: Static or probed (AX-15)

- **Scenario:** an S3 backend pointed at a non-AWS S3-compatible endpoint
  whose consistency and `If-Match` support are unknown.
- **Options:** (a) static per backend; where one backend serves services
  that differ, the store's configuration selects the declaration, an
  unnamed one getting the deviations; (b) static, the normal values for
  every S3 endpoint: simplest, but an endpoint that is not strongly
  consistent then breaks D23 undeclared; (c) probed at first use, which costs
  requests and needs write access to test consistency.
- **Recommendation:** (a): an undeclared deviation is the one thing AX-15
  forbids, so the unknown case takes the weaker side. Source: unsourced.
- **Decision:** (a). OP-01's variation points:

  | Point | Normal value | Declared deviations, weakest last | Decided by |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | Termination | D12's T_connect and T_idle bound every request | Local, and SFTP's server-side disk: when the OS's I/O completes | D12, D14 |
  | Read-after-write | strong: real-time order after a completed API mutation | eventual | D23 |
  | Pinned resume | a dropped stream resumes pinned, or fails changed | no resume: fails unavailable (SFTP; an S3-compatible endpoint not declared to pin) | D39, D42 |
  | O3 in-place modification | cannot arise (object stores) | arises, with D26's weaker rights (Local, SFTP) | D26 |

  Each is static per backend, selected by the store's configuration where
  one backend serves services that differ; never probed, never requested.
- **Consistency check:** binds every entry's AX-15 (how a variation is
  fixed), so shared: `OP-01/O12`. The S3 endpoints' rows go to a profile
  table beside OP-01's spec, as spec 052's do (RFC-0024 D6).
- **Open items:** `OP-01/O12`.
- **Amended by:** OP-01/D57, OP-01/D62, OP-01/D64, OP-01/D65, OP-01/D76, OP-01/D82, OP-01/D83, OP-01/D91, OP-01/D105, OP-01/D107, OP-01/D119, OP-01/D120, OP-01/D138, OP-01/D139, OP-01/D146, OP-01/D162, OP-01/D170, OP-01/D171

### OP-01/D44: A caller who needs the stronger guarantee (AX-15)

- **Scenario:** a caller's correctness rests on read-after-write (D23) or
  pinned resume (D39).
- **Options:** (a) no request in OP-01; the caller learns the store's
  profile through OP-18; (b) a per-call "require" option that fails fast with
  an unsupported category, which every entry would then need for
  consistency.
- **Recommendation:** (a): it keeps OP-01's signature free of policy. Source:
  shared.md Lands elsewhere ("How a caller learns a store's profile: OP-18's
  flow").
- **Decision:** (a). OP-01 takes no option that requests or refuses a
  variation.
- **Consistency check:** consistent with D1 to D3 (the signatures). OP-18
  must expose D43's points: `OP-01/O13`.
- **Open items:** `OP-01/O13`.

### OP-01/D45: What a stream read surfaces (AX-11, reopen of `OP-01/O14`)

- **Scenario:** a stream's connection drops at 30 GB; before the resume
  (D39), O3 revokes the caller's read right (E6) or archives the item (E15);
  separately, the task awaiting a read is cancelled, or the read meets
  `MemoryError`.
- **Options:** (a) widen D35's read-phase list to every D34 category but
  those of classification and decoding; (b) keep D35's list and narrow D19's
  s3 cells for E6 and E15, so a resume meeting them needs another category;
  (c) widen, but leave E15 out of the read phase.
- **Recommendation:** (a): a resume is a new request, which meets E6 and E15
  as an open does, and D20 and D33 already let cancellation and as-raised
  failures out of a read. Source: `OP-01/O14`.
- **Asked:** "OP-01/O14: D35 says a stream read surfaces " → "Widen (Recommended)"
- **Decision:** (a). A read on the stream may surface every category of
  D34's table except key refused, not found and not an item (classification
  is the open's, D22) and a decode failure (the stream is binary): that is
  unavailable, timeout, changed, the base category, permission denied,
  authentication failed, not readable, memory or descriptor failures as
  raised, the caller errors, and a cancellation.
- **Amends:** OP-01/D35
- **Consistency check:** consistent with D19's s3 cells (E6, E15, E14),
  D20, D30, D33 and D39. Which category E15 surfaces mid-stream is D47's.
  Which of these move the stream to Failed is `OP-01/O36`'s.
- **Open items:** none.
- **Amended by:** OP-01/D96

### OP-01/D46: What the stream's open surfaces (AX-11, reopen of `OP-01/O26` and `OP-01/O39`)

- **Scenario:** a stream's open meets E7 past its retries, E9, a
  cancellation of the awaiting task, a caller error such as a non-str key,
  or EIO from a Local disk.
- **Options:** (a) every D34 category except changed and decode; (b) add
  unavailable and timeout only, leaving cancellation, caller errors and E13
  unlisted.
- **Recommendation:** (a): the open makes the first backend request (D35), so
  it meets everything a whole read's one call meets, except a lost pin
  (nothing is pinned before t₀) and decoding (binary). Source: `OP-01/O26`,
  `OP-01/O39`.
- **Asked:** "OP-01/O26(iii) + O39: D35's open-call list lacks" → "All but changed (Recommended)"
- **Decision:** (a). The open call may surface every category of D34's table
  except changed and a decode failure: key refused, not found, not an item,
  permission denied, authentication failed, not readable, unavailable,
  timeout, the base category, memory or descriptor failures as raised, the
  caller errors and a cancellation. A whole read surfaces every row of D34's
  table at its one call, as D35 says.
- **Amends:** OP-01/D35
- **Consistency check:** consistent with D20 (a cancelled open hands out
  nothing), D22 (t₀ lies in the open), D28, D33 and D45: the two lists
  overlap only in what any backend request can meet.
- **Open items:** none.
- **Amended by:** OP-01/D79, OP-01/D96

### OP-01/D47: E15 during a stream (AX-9, reopen of `OP-01/O15`)

- **Scenario:** O1 streams `cold.bin`; O3 moves it to an archive tier (E15);
  at 30 GB the connection drops and the stream resumes.
- **Options:** (a) E15 leaves D25; a read needing a new request fails not
  readable and the stream is Failed; (b) E15 stays in D25 and the stream
  fails changed.
- **Recommendation:** (a): the content did not change (a tier change keeps
  the ETag, not run), so changed would send the caller to re-read an item
  that will fail not readable. Source: `OP-01/O15`, D30.
- **Asked:** "OP-01/O15: D25 puts E15 (archive tier, missing customer key) under" → "Not readable (Recommended)"
- **Decision:** (a). D25's O3 actions are E3 and E5. Under E15 during a
  stream, reads go on while the transfer under way holds; a read that needs a
  new backend request fails not readable, and the stream is Failed (D16).
  Bytes delivered stay a prefix of the content pinned at open (D22).
- **Amends:** OP-01/D25
- **Consistency check:** consistent with D19 (E15 exposed at s2 and s3), D30,
  D34's E15 row and D45's read-phase list, which holds not readable.
- **Open items:** none.
- **Amended by:** OP-01/D77, OP-01/D84, OP-01/D130, OP-01/D140

### OP-01/D48: E4 and the changed category (AX-10, reopen of `OP-01/O33`)

- **Scenario:** during O1's stream on Local, O3 truncates the file (E4).
- **Options:** (a) E4 leaves D34's changed row; under E4 a read surfaces no
  category of its own; (b) E4 stays as "may fail changed", the best-effort
  detection D26 rejected.
- **Recommendation:** (a): D26 promises no detection, and a test can assert
  neither outcome under (b). Source: D26, `OP-01/O33`.
- **Asked:** "OP-01/O33: D34's changed row reads " → "E4 leaves (Recommended)"
- **Decision:** (a). D34's changed row reads "E1, E2, E3, E5 against a pinned
  stream". Under E4 a read never surfaces changed; it may end short without
  failing (D26).
- **Amends:** OP-01/D34
- **Consistency check:** consistent with D26 and D43's O3 in-place row. D47
  keeps E15 out of the row; `OP-01/O44` may add a row.
- **Open items:** none.
- **Amended by:** OP-01/D62

### OP-01/D49: Cancellation at close (AX-7, reopen of `OP-01/O16`)

- **Scenario:** an asyncio task is cancelled while awaiting a stream's
  `close()`, which is releasing its connection.
- **Options:** (a) E14 reaches s4: the cancellation propagates out of
  `close()`, as D36 says; (b) `close()` swallows it, D36 amended, so the
  task does not see its own cancellation there.
- **Recommendation:** (a): swallowing a cancellation breaks asyncio's task
  cancellation and the timeouts built on it. Source: `OP-01/O16`, D36;
  asyncio's rule that `CancelledError` propagates.
- **Asked:** "OP-01/O16: D19's table gives E14" → "Yes, propagates (Recommended)"
- **Decision:** (a). D19's table reads `yes` for E14 at s4, and its sentence
  on s4 reads: s4 releases a resource and reports nothing but a cancellation
  (D36). The stream is Closed after a cancelled `close()` (D50).
- **Amends:** OP-01/D19
- **Consistency check:** consistent with D20 and D36; D36's Decision already
  says it.
- **Open items:** none.

### OP-01/D50: The cancelled read in D18's table (AX-6, reopen of `OP-01/O45`)

- **Scenario:** a task awaiting `stream.read(1 MiB)` is cancelled, in each of
  the stream's four states.
- **Options:** (a) D18's table gains a column for a cancelled read, Closed in
  every row; (b) the transition stays in D20's sentence alone.
- **Recommendation:** (a): one table holds every transition, which the AX-6
  form asks for. Source: `OP-01/O45`, D20.
- **Asked:** "OP-01/O45: D20 makes a cancelled stream read release" → "Add a column (Recommended)"
- **Decision:** (a). D18's table gains a column "a read or `close` is
  cancelled", reading "releases; Closed" for Open, Exhausted and Failed, and
  "no effect" for Closed. D20's check reads: from every state, any cancelled
  read leads to Closed.
- **Amends:** OP-01/D18, OP-01/D20
- **Consistency check:** consistent with D15, D20, D49 (the column covers a
  cancelled `close`) and D51.
- **Open items:** none.

### OP-01/D51: Which failures move a stream to Failed (AX-6, reopen of `OP-01/O36`)

- **Scenario:** on an Open stream the caller calls `chunks(0)`; separately, a
  read meets `MemoryError` after bytes left the connection.
- **Options:** (a) every failure but a caller error and a cancellation moves
  to Failed; (b) storage failures only, so after a `MemoryError` the next read
  may return bytes after a gap; (c) storage failures to Failed, as-raised
  ones to Closed, losing the original failure.
- **Recommendation:** (a): a caller error is raised before any effect, so the
  stream is as it was; any other failure may have consumed bytes, and only
  Failed keeps both D6's prefix rule and the original failure. Source:
  `OP-01/O36`, D16, D33.
- **Asked:** "OP-01/O36: D18 says 'a failure moves to Failed'" → "All but caller/cancel (Recommended)"
- **Decision:** (a). From Open, a storage failure (D27) or a failure raised
  as is (D33) moves the stream to Failed; a caller error (D33) is raised
  before any effect and leaves the state unchanged; a cancellation moves it to
  Closed (D20, D50). From Exhausted, a caller error leaves it unchanged too.
- **Amends:** OP-01/D16, OP-01/D18
- **Consistency check:** consistent with D6, D16 (D52 fixes what is raised
  again), D20 and D45's read-phase list.
- **Open items:** none.
- **Amended by:** OP-01/D80, OP-01/D161

### OP-01/D52: The failure raised again (AX-6, reopen of `OP-01/O26`)

- **Scenario:** a Failed stream is read three times.
- **Options:** (a) a new exception of the same category each time, the
  original as its `__cause__`; (b) the same object raised again, whose
  traceback grows with every raise.
- **Recommendation:** (a): each raise gets a fresh traceback, and callers tell
  failures apart by category, never by identity. Source: PEP 3134.
- **Asked:** "OP-01/O26(ii), AX-6: D16's Failed state raises" → "New, chained (Recommended)"
- **Decision:** (a). Every read on a Failed stream raises a new exception of
  the original failure's category, whose `__cause__` is the original
  exception; for a failure raised as is (D33), the new exception is of the
  original's type.
- **Amends:** OP-01/D16
- **Consistency check:** consistent with D16 ("the same category"), D27 (the
  native exception stays reachable through the chain) and D51.
- **Open items:** none.
- **Amended by:** OP-01/D93, OP-01/D95, OP-01/D117

### OP-01/D53: Key refused and the store's base (AX-10, reopen of `OP-01/O17`)

- **Scenario:** an application passes a user-supplied name `café/x` to two
  stores; one accepts it, the other, opted into portability (KEY-008),
  refuses it.
- **Options:** (a) keep D34: key refused under the store's base and
  `ValueError`, D33's reason narrowed; (b) key refused a caller error alone,
  outside the base, so `except` on the base no longer catches it.
- **Recommendation:** (a): a refusal is a function of the store's
  configuration (KEY-001, KEY-008), so one input passes on one store and
  fails on another: the store's verdict, not only the caller's bug. Source:
  KEY-001, KEY-008, `OP-01/O17`.
- **Asked:** "OP-01/O17: D33 types so that `except` on the store's base" → "Base + ValueError (Recommended)"
- **Decision:** (a). D34's key-refused row stands. D33's reason reads:
  `except` on the store's base never hides a caller error that no store
  configuration decides (a wrong type, an invalid parameter value, a closed
  stream); key refused is decided by the store's L_api and stays under the
  base, also a `ValueError`.
- **Amends:** OP-01/D33
- **Consistency check:** consistent with D9's key-refused row, D34 and
  KEY-001 (raised before any backend contact).
- **Open items:** none.

### OP-01/D54: Descriptor exhaustion (AX-10, reopen of `OP-01/O22`)

- **Scenario:** a process at its descriptor limit reads from Local (`open`
  raises EMFILE) and from S3 (the SDK's socket creation fails, surfacing as a
  connection failure).
- **Options:** (a) *unspecified* between the two: raised as is, or
  unavailable; (b) normalised: the library finds EMFILE or ENFILE in the cause
  chain and raises the `OSError` on every backend; (c) a profile-declared
  variation per backend.
- **Recommendation:** (a): (b) rests on every SDK chaining causes, which none
  promises, and (c) varies by SDK internals, not by backend. Source:
  `OP-01/O22`, axes.md F3.
- **Asked:** "OP-01/O22: descriptor exhaustion (E13) propagates" → "Unspecified of two (Recommended)"
- **Decision:** (a). On every backend, descriptor exhaustion surfaces either
  as raised (an `OSError` with errno EMFILE or ENFILE) or as unavailable after
  D38's retries; which one is unspecified. Memory exhaustion surfaces as
  raised (D33).
- **Amends:** OP-01/D33, OP-01/D34
- **Consistency check:** consistent with D28 and D38 (retrying it changes
  nothing, D10), D45 and D46 (both lists hold both categories), D51 (either
  moves an Open stream to Failed).
- **Open items:** none.
- **Amended by:** OP-01/D145

### OP-01/D55: An unknown encoding (AX-10, reopen of `OP-01/O26`)

- **Scenario:** the text form with `encoding='utf8x'`, or `errors='bogus'`.
- **Options:** (a) `LookupError` as raised, checked before any backend
  contact; (b) wrapped as `ValueError` before contact; (c) `LookupError` when
  decoding, after the transfer.
- **Recommendation:** (a): what `bytes.decode` and `open()` raise, and a
  misspelt encoding costs no transfer. Source: `OP-01/O26`.
- **Asked:** "OP-01/O26(i): an unknown `encoding` (or `errors` handler)" → "LookupError, early (Recommended)"
- **Decision:** (a). The text form looks up `encoding` and `errors` before
  any backend contact and raises `LookupError` for either it cannot find. D33's
  caller errors are `TypeError`, `ValueError` and `LookupError`.
- **Amends:** OP-01/D33, OP-01/D34
- **Consistency check:** consistent with D3 and D5 (the decoded value is
  unchanged) and D53 (a caller error no store configuration decides).
- **Open items:** none.

### OP-01/D56: A connect past T_connect (AX-10, reopen of `OP-01/O27`)

- **Scenario:** the backend's address drops SYNs (E7, connect never
  completes); separately, it refuses the connection.
- **Options:** (a) timeout: a connect past T_connect surfaces as timeout, a
  refused or unresolvable one as unavailable; (b) unavailable for all of E7,
  D12 amended.
- **Recommendation:** (a): it keeps D12, makes `except TimeoutError` catch
  every timeout, and timeout is an unavailable, so `except` on unavailable
  still catches both. botocore and aiohttp keep the two apart (✓ in
  `OP-01/O27`). Source: `OP-01/O27`, D12.
- **Asked:** "OP-01/O27: a connect past T_connect is 'timeout' under D12" → "Timeout (Recommended)"
- **Decision:** (a). Under E7, a connect that takes longer than T_connect
  surfaces as timeout (`TimeoutError`, an unavailable); a refused connection or
  a failed name resolution surfaces as unavailable. E7 stays one Environment
  row; D34's table reads "E7, connect past T_connect; E9 | timeout" and "E7
  otherwise, E8, E10, E12 | unavailable".
- **Amends:** OP-01/D28, OP-01/D34
- **Consistency check:** consistent with D12 and D38 (timeout is retried as
  before). How long name resolution may take is `OP-01/O41`'s.
- **Open items:** none.

### OP-01/D57: Name resolution (AX-5, reopen of `OP-01/O41`)

- **Scenario:** an S3 store's endpoint name resolves through a DNS server
  that never answers; the sync form calls `getaddrinfo`.
- **Options:** (a) a declared deviation: resolution ends when the system
  resolver returns, in both forms; (b) T_connect covers resolution, through a
  helper thread or the executor, leaving a thread behind on a timeout;
  (c) the async form bounds it by T_connect and the sync form deviates.
- **Recommendation:** (a): the cost D14 rejected for a blocked syscall
  applies here too, and (c) breaks D1's parity. Source: `OP-01/O41`, D14.
- **Asked:** "OP-01/O41: name resolution (getaddrinfo) takes no timeout" → "Declared deviation (Recommended)"
- **Decision:** (a). T_connect bounds the connect after name resolution.
  D43's Termination row gains a declared deviation: every network backend,
  in both forms, ends name resolution when the system resolver returns.
- **Amends:** OP-01/D12, OP-01/D43
- **Consistency check:** consistent with D1, D13 (the defaults stand), D14
  and D56 (a failed resolution is unavailable). A resolver with its own
  timeout still terminates; the bound is then the OS's, as in D14.
- **Open items:** none.

### OP-01/D58: A caller's own deadline (AX-5, reopen of `OP-01/O23`)

- **Scenario:** a caller wraps a 40 GB stream's reads in `asyncio.timeout(60)`;
  the deadline passes during a read.
- **Options:** (a) D12 records the trade-off: a caller's deadline is a
  cancellation, and on a stream it costs the stream (D20); (b) D12 and D20
  stand unchanged, the trade-off left implicit.
- **Recommendation:** (a): AX-5 asks how a caller imposes a deadline and what
  it costs a handed-out object. Source: `OP-01/O23`, axes.md AX-5.
- **Asked:** "OP-01/O23 (AX-5's caller deadline): with no whole-call deadline" → "Record it (Recommended)"
- **Decision:** (a). A caller imposes their own deadline by cancellation
  (E14), such as `asyncio.timeout` or a signal in the sync form. A cancelled
  whole read or open hands out nothing and changes nothing; a cancelled
  stream read closes the stream (D20), so a caller who wants to go on makes a
  new OP-01 call.
- **Amends:** OP-01/D12
- **Consistency check:** consistent with D20, D49 and D50.
- **Open items:** none.
- **Amended by:** OP-01/D129

### OP-01/D59: What bounds a call's waiting (AX-13, reopen of `OP-01/O32`)

- **Scenario:** one stream `read(-1)` over 40 GB meets a reset every 5 GB;
  each resume succeeds after bytes have flowed.
- **Options:** (a) narrow D12's and D38's checks: waiting is bounded per
  request and per whole call, not per stream while bytes keep flowing;
  (b) leave both checks as they are.
- **Recommendation:** (a): D39 restarts the attempt limit once bytes flow, so
  the checks as written are false for a stream. Source: `OP-01/O32`.
- **Asked:** "OP-01/O32: D12's and D38's checks say AX-13 bounds" → "Narrow (Recommended)"
- **Decision:** (a). D12's and D38's checks read: T_connect and T_idle bound
  each backend request; a whole read's or an open's waiting is at most the
  attempt limit times the bound plus the backoff; a stream's total waiting is
  not bounded while bytes keep flowing between resumes (D39, NP1).
- **Amends:** OP-01/D12, OP-01/D38
- **Consistency check:** consistent with D12's Decision ("returns or fails"
  under a quiet environment), D39, NP1 and NP2.
- **Open items:** none.
- **Amended by:** OP-01/D97, OP-01/D128

### OP-01/D60: Caller-level retry of the remaining failures (AX-13, reopen of `OP-01/O37`)

- **Scenario:** a caller retries, by a new call, after each failure D34 lists
  that D38's table does not.
- **Options:** (a) four rows added to D38's table; (b) the table limited to
  storage failures, the others declared outside it.
- **Recommendation:** (a): every category D34 lists then has a row, and none
  needs a new rule. Source: `OP-01/O37`, D5, D20, D33.
- **Asked:** "OP-01/O37: D38's caller-level retry table lacks rows" → "Four rows (Recommended)"
- **Decision:** (a). D38's caller-level table gains:

  | Failure | A new call |
  |---|---|
  | decode under strict | repeats it unless the content changes: a pure function of it (D5) |
  | caller error | repeats it: a function of the arguments |
  | memory or descriptors, as raised | may succeed once the resource frees |
  | cancellation | may succeed: a cancelled call leaves no effect (D20) |

- **Amends:** OP-01/D38
- **Consistency check:** consistent with D5, D20, D33, D54 (descriptor
  exhaustion surfacing as unavailable already has a row) and D55 (a
  `LookupError` is a caller error).
- **Open items:** none.
- **Amended by:** OP-01/D98, OP-01/D129

### OP-01/D61: A lost pin where the backend cannot pin (AX-15, reopen of `OP-01/O28`)

- **Scenario:** an SFTP stream's connection drops at 30 GB; O2 may or may not
  have replaced the item meanwhile, and the library cannot tell.
- **Options:** (a) D22, D25 and D34's changed row state the normal value;
  under D43's no-resume deviation a dropped stream fails unavailable;
  (b) changed there too, a false positive on every dropped connection;
  (c) a third category, "cannot resume", under unavailable.
- **Recommendation:** (a): changed claims a change the library cannot know
  of, and a new call after unavailable reads the content current at its own
  open, which is a new read's due. Source: `OP-01/O28`, D42.
- **Asked:** "OP-01/O28: D22/D25/D34 say a lost pin fails 'changed'" → "Normal value only (Recommended)"
- **Decision:** (a). D22's "fails with the changed category", D25's and D34's
  changed row hold where the backend pins (D43's normal value). Where it
  declares no resume, a stream that needs a new request fails unavailable,
  whatever happened to the content, and is Failed (D16). D38's unavailable
  row adds: on a stream, a new call reads the content current at its own
  open.
- **Amends:** OP-01/D22, OP-01/D25, OP-01/D34, OP-01/D38
- **Consistency check:** consistent with D31 (changed still means a change
  the backend proved), D39, D42 and D43's Pinned resume row. Under the
  deviation, bytes delivered are still a prefix of the open's content (D22).
- **Open items:** none.
- **Amended by:** OP-01/D85

### OP-01/D62: A metadata-only change (AX-7, reopen of `OP-01/O44`)

- **Scenario:** O1 streams an Azure blob; O3 sets its metadata; the
  connection drops and the resume, conditioned on the ETag, fails although
  the content is the open's.
- **Options:** (a) an Environment row, and a declared deviation: a
  metadata-only change also fails a resume changed (Azure); (b) the row, with
  a promise that a metadata-only change never fails a resume, which Azure
  cannot keep without a content-only token.
- **Recommendation:** (a): the pin is a token the backend gives, and Azure's
  changes on any write; S3 keeps the ETag on a metadata-only change (session
  input, Q4 Identity token). Source: `OP-01/O44`.
- **Asked:** "OP-01/O44: no Environment row covers O3 changing only" → "Row + deviation (Recommended)"
- **Decision:** (a). E16 joins the Environment: O3 changes an item's
  metadata or system properties without its content. It is exposed at s3
  only, through a resume. Changed means the backend's pin token no longer
  matches; D43's Pinned resume row gains a declared deviation: a
  metadata-only change also fails a resume changed (Azure; not run). D31's
  reason reads: a new call reads the current content, which may equal the
  old.
- **Amends:** OP-01/D19, OP-01/D22, OP-01/D31, OP-01/D34, OP-01/D43, OP-01/D48
- **Consistency check:** consistent with D10 (no API call's state changes by
  OP-01), D22 (no byte from another content is delivered either way) and
  D61. E16's changed row joins D48's: "E1, E2, E3, E5, and E16 where the
  deviation is declared, against a pinned stream".
- **Open items:** none.
- **Amended by:** OP-01/D86, OP-01/D102, OP-01/D119, OP-01/D139

### OP-01/D63: When an O3 change is seen (AX-9, reopen of `OP-01/O31`)

- **Scenario:** a sync tool PUTs `a` with the AWS CLI, which returns; then O1
  reads `a`.
- **Options:** (a) D23 applies to O3: once O3's change completes, a later
  read sees it, under D23's normal value and deviations; (b) a registered
  non-promise for O3's visibility; (c) its own variation point.
- **Recommendation:** (a): it is what D25's dialog adopted, it holds natively
  on S3, Azure and Local, and a test can check it with a native writer.
  Source: `OP-01/O31`, D25's adopted option.
- **Asked:** "OP-01/O31: D25's dialog adopted 'D21 to D23 apply unchanged'" → "D23 applies to O3 (Recommended)"
- **Decision:** (a). D25's sentence "D23's real-time order is promised only
  for API mutations; when an O3 change becomes visible is the backend's" is
  withdrawn. D23's real-time order covers an O3 change of E3 or E5 that
  completed (its tool returned) before the read started, with D23's declared
  deviations.
- **Amends:** OP-01/D25
- **Consistency check:** consistent with D23, D43's Read-after-write row
  (now covering O3 too, so `OP-01/O13`'s four points stand), D47 and D64.
  E4 stays under D26.
- **Open items:** none.
- **Amended by:** OP-01/D76, OP-01/D87

### OP-01/D64: Local on a network file system (AX-15, reopen of `OP-01/O43`)

- **Scenario:** two Local stores on two hosts over one NFS export; O2 renames
  a new `a` into place on host A, then O1 on host B reads `a`; separately, O3
  on host A removes a file host B is streaming.
- **Options:** (a) a static deviation in D43's Read-after-write row, the
  removal failing changed (D22's normal value); (b) a registered non-promise
  for network file systems; (c) a configuration-selected declaration, the
  unnamed default taking the deviation.
- **Recommendation:** (a): it states what Local does without penalising local
  disks, and ESTALE is a content the backend can no longer serve, which D22
  already types. Source: `OP-01/O43`; NFS close-to-open consistency (RFC 7530,
  data caching), not run.
- **Asked:** "OP-01/O43: Local on NFS, two hosts" → "Static deviation (Recommended)"
- **Decision:** (a). D43's Read-after-write row gains a declared deviation:
  Local on a network file system, across hosts, is eventual. A Local stream
  whose file another host removes or replaces may fail changed (D22); D25's
  reason that an open descriptor keeps the old file holds on one host only.
- **Amends:** OP-01/D25, OP-01/D43
- **Consistency check:** consistent with D14 (termination on NFS), D22, D23
  and D63. Static per D43: no configuration selects it.
- **Open items:** none.
- **Amended by:** OP-01/D82

### OP-01/D65: A write overlapping an open read on Windows (AX-15, reopen of `OP-01/O19`)

- **Scenario:** on Local under Windows, O1 holds a stream on `a`; O2 replaces
  `a` (temp-and-rename) or deletes it.
- **Options:** (a) a declared deviation: the mutation may fail while a read
  holds the item open; (b) a promise, opening with `FILE_SHARE_DELETE`, which
  rests on rename and delete-pending behaviour not run here; (c) Windows
  outside Local's v1 profile.
- **Recommendation:** (a): readers keep their content either way, and the
  deviation is stated rather than resting on unrun OS behaviour. Source:
  `OP-01/O19`.
- **Asked:** "OP-01/O19: on Local/Windows an open read" → "Declared deviation (Recommended)"
- **Decision:** (a). D43's table gains a point, Overlapping mutation: normal
  value, an API replace, delete or move of an item succeeds while an OP-01
