  read of it is open; declared deviation, it may fail (Local on Windows). D10
  holds for the store's state; under the deviation an open read can change
  the outcome of an overlapping mutation, never the state it leaves. D21,
  D22 and D25 hold: the reader's content is kept because the write fails.
- **Amends:** OP-01/D10, OP-01/D25, OP-01/D43
- **Consistency check:** consistent with D21 to D25. The point brings D43's
  table to five, which OP-18 must expose (`OP-01/O13`). The category the
  failing mutation surfaces is its own entry's: `OP-01/O46`.
- **Open items:** `OP-01/O46`.
- **Amended by:** OP-01/D81

### OP-01/D66: The composed classification (AX-8, reopen of `OP-01/O20` and `OP-01/O35`)

- **Scenario:** on S3, a GET on `d` answers 404; O3 then PUTs objects; a
  listing of `d/` follows, and OP-01 reports not an item or not found.
- **Options:** (a) D21 and D22 stand, with the argument recorded; (b) an
  exception: a failed classification on object stores may come from two
  instants, unspecified between not found and not an item.
- **Recommendation:** (a): with every state meeting TREE-001 and each O3 step
  changing one object, the GET's instant and the listing's bracket an
  instant whose outcome is the one reported. Source: `OP-01/O20`,
  `OP-01/O35`, TREE-001, KEY-013.
- **Asked:** "OP-01/O20 + O35: on object stores D9 tells" → "Stand, argue (Recommended)"
- **Decision:** (a). D21's whole read and D22's open stand. The argument: a
  404 GET shows `d` no item at t₁; a listing at t₂ that finds `d/x` shows `d`
  a folder at t₂, unless `d` is both, which TREE-001 excludes; one that finds
  nothing shows `d` no folder at t₂, and a step from folder at t₁ to item at
  t₂ passes, one object at a time, through an instant where `d` is absent.
  Each case names an instant in [t₁, t₂] whose D9 row is the outcome. The one
  history that breaks it passes through a state TREE-001 excludes, which D67
  answers.
- **Consistency check:** consistent with D9, D21, D22 and KEY-013, whose four
  outcomes are disjoint by TREE-001 (as `KeyModel.lean` states). An entry
  whose classification composes requests the same way can cite this.
- **Open items:** none.

### OP-01/D67: A key both an item and a folder (AX-7, ground conflict `OP-01/O21`)

- **Scenario:** O3 PUTs objects `a.txt` and `a.txt/b` to S3; O1 reads
  `a.txt`, `a.txt/b` and `a.txt/c`.
- **Options:** (a) settled in the record by E17 with an *unspecified*
  outcome among a stated set; (b) settled by a registered non-promise with no
  admitted set; (c) a reopen of spec 051 through its own flow.
- **Recommendation:** (a): it names what a check may observe, and leaves
  spec 051 as it is. Source: `OP-01/O21`, axes.md F3.
- **Asked:** "OP-01/O21, a ground conflict (route only)" → "Settle: E17 (Recommended)"
- **Decision:** (a). E17 joins the Environment: O3 stores objects such that a
  key is both an item and a folder (object stores). It is exposed at s2 and
  s3. While a key at or above k is in that state, OP-01's outcome for k is
  *unspecified*, one of: the content of the object stored at exactly k, not
  found, not an item; never another key's content. Outside it, D9's partition
  holds unchanged.
- **Amends:** OP-01/D9, OP-01/D19
- **Consistency check:** settles the conflict without changing TREE-001: the
  store spec 051 describes is the state outside E17. Consistent with D66,
  which this answers, and D21 (whichever outcome, from one request's
  instant). That API writes never create the state is `OP-01/O6`'s sessions'
  (a BlockedByItem write).
- **Open items:** none.
- **Amended by:** OP-01/D89

### OP-01/D68: The Session's formal-model line (reopen of `OP-01/O30`)

- **Scenario:** a reader of the Session section trusts that no decision rests
  on a clause a formal model checks.
- **Options:** (a) amend the Session line; (b) leave it, the reopen line
  recording what was read.
- **Recommendation:** (a): the line is false as written. Source: `OP-01/O30`.
- **Asked:** "OP-01/O30: the Session line says" → "Correct it (Recommended)"
- **Decision:** (a). The Session's line reads: formal models not read,
  though D4 and D9 cite KEY-001, KEY-013, TREE-001 and TREE-002, which
  `KeyModel.lean` and `StoreAxioms.lean` and `.als` tag; the reopen read the
  TREE-001 and KEY-013 clauses and found them restated as specs 051 and 052
  state them.
- **Amends:** Session
- **Consistency check:** consistent with the Session's Reopen line and D66.
  A question no axis raises (the record's own provenance), asked under none;
  name it when the axes are revised with F2.
- **Open items:** none.
- **Amended by:** OP-01/D106

### OP-01/D69: The async stream's shape (AX-1, reopen of `OP-01/O24`)

- **Scenario:** a caller ports `for line in stream:` from Store to
  AsyncStore.
- **Options:** (a) an awaited mirror of the sync object, `async for`
  yielding lines; (b) the mirror, `async for` yielding chunks; (c) a minimal
  async object with `read(n)`, `chunks(size)` and `close` only.
- **Recommendation:** (a): D1's parity then holds method by method, and the
  commonest loop means the same in both forms. Source: aiofiles; aiohttp
  `StreamReader` (`async for` yields lines).
- **Asked:** "OP-01/O24: D2's file object is the sync form's" → "Awaited mirror (Recommended)"
- **Decision:** (a). The async stream offers each method of the sync one
  (D2, D70) as a coroutine with the same semantics: `read`, `readinto`,
  `readline`, `read1`, `close`; `async for` yields what sync iteration yields,
  lines; `chunks(size)` is an async iterator; `async with` closes it.
  `tell`, `readable` and `seekable` stay plain methods, as they make no
  request.
- **Amends:** OP-01/D1, OP-01/D2
- **Consistency check:** consistent with D1, D6, D7, D18 (one state table for
  both forms) and D20 (task cancellation is the async form's E14).
- **Open items:** none.
- **Amended by:** OP-01/D101

### OP-01/D70: The file object's methods (AX-1, reopen of `OP-01/O25`)

- **Scenario:** `TextIOWrapper` wraps the stream (it probes `readable` and
  uses `read1`); a progress bar calls `tell()`; pandas probes `seekable()`.
- **Options:** (a) `io.BufferedIOBase`'s contract for every method it names;
  (b) the same without `read1` and `tell`; (c) bare methods only, other names
  raising `AttributeError`.
- **Recommendation:** (a): libraries that take a binary file read it without
  surprises. Source: `io.BufferedIOBase`.
- **Asked:** "OP-01/O25: D18 lists readinto and readline" → "BufferedIOBase (Recommended)"
- **Decision:** (a). With r bytes remaining: `readinto(b)` writes min(len(b),
  r) bytes into b and returns that count, 0 at the end; `readline(limit=-1)`
  returns bytes up to and including the next `b'\n'`, at most limit of them
  when limit ≥ 0, `b''` at the end, and line iteration yields `readline()`
  until it returns `b''`; `read1(n)` returns 1 to min(n, r) bytes, making at
  most one backend request, `b''` at the end; `tell()` returns the bytes
  delivered so far; `readable()` is true and `seekable()` false; `seek`,
  `truncate` and `write` raise `io.UnsupportedOperation`. Each, like `read`,
  draws from the one position (D2), and on a Closed stream raises
  `ValueError` (D15). D18's Exhausted cell reads `b''`, 0 for `readinto`.
- **Amends:** OP-01/D2, OP-01/D18
- **Consistency check:** consistent with D6 (`read`'s exact count; `read1` is
  the one method allowed fewer, as in `io`), D7, D15 and D51. A line's memory
  is D71's.
- **Open items:** none.
- **Amended by:** OP-01/D78, OP-01/D117, OP-01/D125, OP-01/D126

### OP-01/D71: A line's memory (AX-14, reopen of `OP-01/O34`)

- **Scenario:** `for line in stream:` over a 10 GB item with no newline.
- **Options:** (a) a line excluded from D40, registered beside NP8, a bound
  available through `readline(limit)`; (b) `readline` failing past the buffer
  bound; (c) a mandatory limit.
- **Recommendation:** (a): it keeps the `io` contract that (b) and (c)
  break, and a caller who needs a bound has one. Source:
  `io.BufferedIOBase.readline`, `OP-01/O34`.
- **Asked:** "OP-01/O34: D40 bounds a stream's memory" → "Register beside NP8 (Recommended)"
- **Decision:** (a). D40's bound covers every read but `readline()` without a
  limit and line iteration. D41's register gains `OP-01/NP9`: the memory of a
  line read without a limit, which holds the whole line; scope: `readline`
  with a negative limit and line iteration, both forms.
- **Amends:** OP-01/D40, OP-01/D41
- **Consistency check:** consistent with D2, D40 (staging is still never
  done), D41 and D70.
- **Open items:** none.
- **Amended by:** OP-01/D123, OP-01/D126, OP-01/D133

### OP-01/D72: Where a resume happens (AX-13, reopen of `OP-01/O18`)

- **Recommendation:** (a): one clause for the spec, and the record still shows

- **Decision:** (a). D39's Decision reads, in addition: the resume happens
  inside the read that met the reset, which still returns its full count
  (D6) or fails; "the next read" in its option means that read.
- **Amends:** OP-01/D39
- **Consistency check:** consistent with D6, D16 and D59.
- **Open items:** none.

### OP-01/D77: E15 on a backend that does not resume (AX-15, end check)

- **Scenario:** an S3-compatible endpoint not declared to pin; O3 archives an
  item mid-stream (E15), and the connection drops.
- **Options:** (a) D61 governs there: no request is made, and the stream
  fails unavailable; (b) a probe request types the failure, not readable

- **Recommendation:** (a): no extra request, and D61 already says
  "whatever happened to the content". Source: D61, D42.

- **Decision:** (a). D47's not readable holds where the backend resumes;

### OP-01/D78: What `read1` promises (AX-2, end check)

- **Scenario:** `read1(1 MiB)` meets E10 once, which D38 retries.
- **Options:** (a) `read1` returns once at least one byte is available,
  request count left to NP2; (b) exactly one request with no retry or resume.
- **Recommendation:** (a): it is `io`'s meaning of `read1`, and D38, D39 and
  NP2 then hold for it as for `read`. Source: `io.BufferedIOBase.read1`,
  D41's NP2.
- **Asked:** "End check, D70 vs D38/NP2" → "No-wait wording (Recommended)"
- **Decision:** (a). `read1(n)` returns 1 to min(n, r) bytes as soon as at
  least one is available, without waiting to fill n, and `b''` at the end;
  D70's "at most one backend request" is withdrawn. Retries and resumes
  apply as to `read` (D38, D39), and the request count is NP2's.
- **Amends:** OP-01/D70
- **Consistency check:** consistent with D6, D38, D39, D41 and D69.
- **Open items:** none.
- **Amended by:** OP-01/D155

### OP-01/D79: What a whole read surfaces (AX-11, end check)

- **Scenario:** a whole read of 2 GB meets E8 at 1 GB, and D38 retries it.
- **Options:** (a) every D34 row except changed and a closed-stream
  `ValueError`, a retried whole read restarting; (b) changed too, a retry
  continuing by a pinned range request.
- **Recommendation:** (a): D38 already makes a retried whole read return one
  content at one instant, so no pin is needed and changed cannot arise.
  Source: D38, D46.
- **Asked:** "End check, D46: " → "All but stream rows (Recommended)"
- **Decision:** (a). A whole read surfaces every row of D34's table except
  changed and a closed stream's `ValueError`; a decode failure only in the
  text form. A retried whole read restarts the transfer.
- **Amends:** OP-01/D46
- **Consistency check:** consistent with D21, D38, D45 and D46.
- **Open items:** none.
- **Amended by:** OP-01/D96

### OP-01/D80: A caller error on a Failed stream (AX-6, end check)

- **Scenario:** a stream is Failed; the caller calls `chunks(0)`.
- **Options:** (a) the caller error first, the stream staying Failed; (b) the
  failure again, whatever the arguments.
- **Recommendation:** (a): one rule for every state, and a caller's bug is
  not hidden behind a storage failure. Source: D51, D33.
- **Asked:** "End check, D51: a caller error leaves" → "Caller error first (Recommended)"
- **Decision:** (a). In every state but Closed, argument checks come first: a
  caller error is raised and the state is unchanged. A valid call on a Failed

- **Amends:** OP-01/D51
- **Consistency check:** consistent with D15, D33, D51 and D52.
- **Open items:** none.
- **Amended by:** OP-01/D92, OP-01/D93

### OP-01/D81: The frame under Overlapping mutation (AX-4, reopen of `OP-01/O47`)

- **Scenario:** on Local under Windows, O1 holds a stream on `a`; O2's
  replace of `a` fails because of it, leaving v1 where O2 alone would have
  left v2.
- **Options:** (a) a scoped exception in D10 and D24: where D43's
  Overlapping mutation deviation is declared, an open OP-01 read may make an
  overlapping API mutation of that item fail, leaving the state as before
  that mutation; D20 and `OP-01/O8` cite it; (b) D10 unchanged, O2's failure
  counted as the environment's outcome, D24 alone gaining the exception;
  (c) D65 withdrawn, Local on Windows opening reads with
  `FILE_SHARE_DELETE`.
- **Recommendation:** (a): the frame then says what a reader of D10 must
  know, that a read can block a write, and only where the deviation is
  declared. Source: `OP-01/O47`, D65, D43; AX-15's check forbids an
  undeclared deviation.
- **Asked:** "OP-01/O47: D65 lets an O2 replace/delete fail" → "Scoped exception (Recommended)"
- **Decision:** (a). D10's Decision reads, in addition: except where D43's
  Overlapping mutation deviation is declared, where an open OP-01 read, whole
  or streamed, may make an overlapping API replace, delete or move of that
  item fail; that mutation then leaves the state as it was before it, and
  OP-01 changes nothing else. D24's Decision reads, in addition: each call
  behaves as if issued alone except for that failure. D20's "changes nothing
  (D10)" and `OP-01/O8` carry the same exception. D65's sentence "an open read
  can change the outcome of an overlapping mutation, never the state it
  leaves" is withdrawn.
- **Amends:** OP-01/D10, OP-01/D20, OP-01/D24, OP-01/D65, OP-01/O8
- **Consistency check:** consistent with D21, D22 and D25 (the reader keeps
  its content because the write fails), D43's Overlapping mutation row and
  D83 (SFTP declares it too). The failing mutation's category stays its own
  entry's (`OP-01/O46`).
- **Open items:** none.
- **Amended by:** OP-01/D152

### OP-01/D82: A declaration conditioned on the location (AX-15, reopen of `OP-01/O48`)

- **Scenario:** one Local store on a local disk, another on an NFS export
  shared with a second host; neither is configured, nothing is probed.
- **Options:** (a) D43 admits a static declaration conditioned on a stated
  property of the store's location; (b) a configuration flag, unset meaning
  strong, D43's "unnamed takes the deviations" narrowed to services;
  (c) the flag, unset meaning eventual; (d) Local eventual always.
- **Recommendation:** (a): the declaration names its condition, the caller
  knows their mount, and nothing is penalised or left undeclared. Source:
  `OP-01/O48`, D64; profile 052's per-platform Local columns and its "the
  server's file system" cells, which condition a backend's row on its
  location.
- **Asked:** "OP-01/O48: D43 has configuration select a declaration" → "Conditioned declaration (Recommended)"
- **Decision:** (a). D43's rule reads: each variation is static per backend,
  never probed, never requested; a declaration may be conditioned on a
  stated property of the store's location (its file system, the server's
  platform), and where one backend serves services that differ, the store's
  configuration selects the declaration, an unnamed service taking the
  deviations. D64's deviation is such a declaration: Local, where its file
  system is a network one shared across hosts, is eventual; elsewhere
  strong. D64's check "Static per D43: no configuration selects it" holds.
- **Amends:** OP-01/D43, OP-01/D64, OP-01/O12
- **Consistency check:** consistent with D14 (a deviation already
  conditioned on the server's file system), D43, D64 and profile 052's
  conventions. A check runs a conditioned row only where its condition
  holds. D83 applies it to SFTP.
- **Open items:** none.

- **Scenario:** an SFTP server on Windows refuses a rename over a file

- **Decision:** (a). D43's Overlapping mutation row gains a declared

- **Consistency check:** consistent with D14, D63 (O3 changes on the

### OP-01/D84: A tier move during a stream (AX-10, reopen of `OP-01/O49`)

- **Scenario:** O1 streams an Azure blob; O3 moves it to the Archive tier;
  the connection drops and the resume, conditioned on the ETag, fails.
- **Options:** (a) E16 excludes E15's moves, so the rows are disjoint; under
  E15, where D62's deviation is declared, a resume fails not readable or
  changed, which one *unspecified*, elsewhere not readable (D47); (b) the
  rows disjoint, and after a failed condition the library makes a further
  request to report not readable; (c) the overlap kept, changed reported
  wherever the pin token changes, D47 narrowed.
- **Recommendation:** (a): it names both observations a check may see and
  costs no extra request. Source: `OP-01/O49`, D47, D62, axes.md F3.
- **Asked:** "OP-01/O49 (category): an archive-tier move fits both E15" → "Disjoint, unspecified (Recommended)"
- **Decision:** (a). E16 reads: O3 changes an item's metadata or system
  properties without its content, other than an E15 move. Under E15 during a
  stream, a read that needs a new backend request fails not readable where
  the backend's pin token survives the move, and, where D62's deviation is
  declared, fails not readable or changed, which one *unspecified*; the
  stream is then Failed (D16). Where the backend declares no resume, D77
  holds: unavailable.
- **Amends:** OP-01/E16, OP-01/D34, OP-01/D47
- **Consistency check:** consistent with D30, D45 (both categories are on a
  stream read's list), D61, D62 and D77. D34's E15 row and its changed row
  each admit the other category under D62's deviation; D48's changed row
  reads "E1, E2, E3, E5, and E15 or E16 where D62's deviation is declared,
  against a pinned stream".
- **Open items:** none.

- **Open items:** none.

### OP-01/D92: A caller error on a Closed stream (AX-6, reopen of `OP-01/O51`)

- **Scenario:** a stream is Closed; the caller calls `read('x')`, then
  `read(2)`.
- **Options:** (a) argument checks first in every state, Closed included, as
  `io` does; (b) Closed first, `ValueError` whatever the arguments, as D80's
  Decision reads.
- **Recommendation:** (a): one rule for every state, which D80's adopted
  option states, and `io`'s behaviour, whose contract D70 adopts: a closed
  CPython `BufferedReader` raises `TypeError` for `read('x')` and
  `ValueError` for `read(2)` (✓ in `OP-01/O51`). Source: `OP-01/O51`, D70,
  D80.
- **Asked:** "OP-01/O51 (Closed order): D80 puts argument checks first" → "Args first, as io (Recommended)"
- **Decision:** (a). D80's Decision reads: in every state, Closed included,
  argument checks come first: a caller error is raised and the state is
  unchanged. A valid call on a Closed stream raises `ValueError` (D15).
- **Amends:** OP-01/D80
- **Consistency check:** consistent with D15 (a valid read on a Closed
  stream), D33, D51 and D70.
- **Open items:** none.

### OP-01/D93: Which calls a Failed stream raises again on (AX-6, reopen of `OP-01/O51`)

- **Scenario:** a stream is Failed; the caller calls `tell()`, `readable()`,
  `close()` and `read(10)`.
- **Options:** (a) the data reads only raise the failure again; `close`
  releases, `tell`, `readable` and `seekable` answer, `seek`, `truncate` and
  `write` raise `io.UnsupportedOperation`; (b) every call but `close` raises
  it again.
- **Recommendation:** (a): each of those answers stays true after a failure
  and makes no request, and D16's adopted option has close work. Source:
  `OP-01/O51`, D16, D69, D70.
- **Asked:** "OP-01/O51 (Failed calls): D52 says" → "Data reads only (Recommended)"
- **Decision:** (a). On a Failed stream, `read`, `read1`, `readinto`,
  `readline`, line iteration and `chunks` raise the failure again (D52);
  `close` releases and the stream is Closed (D16, D18); `tell` returns the
  bytes delivered so far, `readable` is true and `seekable` false; `seek`,
  `truncate` and `write` raise `io.UnsupportedOperation` (D70). D52's "every
  read" and D80's "a valid call on a Failed stream" mean those data reads.
- **Amends:** OP-01/D52, OP-01/D80
- **Consistency check:** consistent with D16 ("close works"), D18's Failed
  row, whose read column holds the data reads (`OP-01/O58`), D69, D70 and
  D92.
- **Open items:** none.
- **Amended by:** OP-01/D125, OP-01/D126, OP-01/D150, OP-01/D151

### OP-01/D94: A cancelled close (AX-6, reopen of `OP-01/O59`)

- **Scenario:** an asyncio task awaiting a stream's `close()` is cancelled
  while the connection is being released; the caller then calls `close()`
  again.
- **Options:** (a) Closed: the cancellation propagates, resources released or
  abandoned, a second close a no-op; (b) the state before close, the caller
  closing again.
- **Recommendation:** (a): a stream is never usable after its close began,
  and (b) holds the connection until collection after a cancelled
  `async with` exit. Source: `OP-01/O59`, D49; `io.IOBase.close` marks the
  file closed even when its flush raises.
- **Asked:** "OP-01/O59 (cancelled close): D49 and D50 leave a stream Closed" → "Closed (Recommended)"
- **Decision:** (a). A cancelled `close()` propagates the cancellation (D49)
  and leaves the stream Closed, its resources released or abandoned; a later
  `close()` is a no-op (D15).
- **Consistency check:** consistent with D15, D17, D49 and D50's column, as
  written: a question on D50's dialog, not its Decision, so no amendment.
- **Open items:** none.
- **Amended by:** OP-01/D118

### OP-01/D95: The type a Failed stream raises again (AX-6, reopen of `OP-01/O59`)

- **Scenario:** a stream read meets an `OSError` with errno EMFILE, raised as
  is (D54); the caller reads again and checks `errno`.
- **Options:** (a) a new exception of the original's type built from its
  args; (b) of the original's type with no args; (c) the store's base
  category wrapping it.
- **Recommendation:** (a): a failure raised as is has its type as its only
  category, so D52's option and Decision agree, and its args keep `errno`.
  Source: `OP-01/O59`, D52; `OSError`'s constructor takes errno from its
  args.
- **Asked:** "OP-01/O59 (re-raised type): for a failure raised as is" → "Type and args (Recommended)"
- **Decision:** (a). For a failure raised as is (D33), every raise on the
  Failed stream is a new exception of the original's type built from the
  original's args, so an `OSError` keeps its errno; its `__cause__` is the
  original.
- **Amends:** OP-01/D52
- **Consistency check:** consistent with D33, D51, D52 (a storage failure is
  raised again as a new exception of its category) and D54.
- **Open items:** none.

### OP-01/D96: One failure-phase table (AX-11, reopen of `OP-01/O52`)

- **Scenario:** a caller asks which failures a whole read, a stream's open, a
  stream read and `close()` can raise; D35, D45, D46 and D79 each answer part
  of it in prose, and D35's "a whole read surfaces everything" and D46's
  closed-stream `ValueError` survive D79.
- **Options:** (a) one table, D34's categories by phase, replacing the prose
  lists of D35, D45, D46 and D79, D46's check corrected; (b) the lists
  amended in place; (c) D46 only.
- **Recommendation:** (a): AX-11's normative form is a table row (failure,
  phase), and the prose lists drifted twice. Source: `OP-01/O52`, axes.md
  AX-11, D79's reason.
- **Asked:** "OP-01/O52 (failure phases): D79 amends D46 only" → "One phase table (Recommended)"
- **Decision:** (a). The phases each D34 category can surface in, both forms:

  | Category (D34) | Whole read | Stream open | Stream read | `close` |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | key refused | yes | yes | — | — |
  | not found, not an item | yes | yes | — | — |
  | permission denied, authentication failed | yes | yes | yes | — |
  | not readable | yes | yes | yes | — |
  | unavailable, timeout | yes | yes | yes | — |
  | changed | — | — | yes | — |
  | base category | yes | yes | yes | — |
  | memory or descriptors, as raised | yes | yes | yes | — |
  | decode under strict | text form only | — | — | — |
  | caller error | yes | yes | yes | — |
  | a closed stream's `ValueError` | — | — | yes | — |
  | cancellation | yes | yes | yes | yes |

  D35's, D45's, D46's and D79's lists read as this table. D34's E17 row
  (D88) surfaces through its not found and not an item rows, at a whole read
  and an open only (D89). D46's check reads: the open's and a stream read's
  lists share what any backend request can meet, the caller errors and a
  cancellation.
- **Amends:** OP-01/D35, OP-01/D45, OP-01/D46, OP-01/D79
- **Consistency check:** consistent with D20, D33, D36 (`close` raises
  nothing but a cancellation), D45, D46, D49, D79, D89 and D92 (argument
  checks first, Closed included). Every row of D34's table has a row here.
- **Open items:** none.
- **Amended by:** OP-01/D142, OP-01/D153

### OP-01/D97: What bounds waiting (AX-5, reopen of `OP-01/O53`)

- **Scenario:** a whole read's classification takes two requests (D73), and
  the backend then sends one byte every 59 s, so T_idle never fires.
- **Options:** (a) each wait is bounded, no total: T_connect per connect,
  T_idle per wait for the next byte, D38's limit per request, D59's check and
  NP1's scope amended; (b) a configurable minimum rate failing a request
  slower than R bytes/s over T as timeout.
- **Recommendation:** (a): D12 decided that a progressing transfer never
  fails by its size, and (b) fails a slow healthy link by its speed. Source:
  `OP-01/O53`, D12; botocore `read_timeout` (per socket read); curl
  `CURLOPT_LOW_SPEED_LIMIT` for (b).
- **Asked:** "OP-01/O53 (waiting bound): D59 says a whole read's" → "Per wait, no total (Recommended)"
- **Decision:** (a). D59's check reads: T_connect bounds each connect and
  T_idle each wait for the next byte (D12); D38's attempt limit bounds each
  request's attempts; how many requests a call makes is NP2's. No figure
  bounds a whole read's, an open's or a stream's total waiting or duration:
  a request that keeps receiving bytes is never cut. D41's NP1 scope reads:
  every call and stream read; D12 bounds each wait for a byte, not total
  waiting or duration.
- **Amends:** OP-01/D41, OP-01/D59
- **Consistency check:** consistent with D12's Decision ("returns or fails"
  under a quiet environment), which rests on every wait ending and on a call
  making finitely many requests, a count NP2 leaves unpromised; D13, D38,
  D39, D58 (a caller's own deadline is a cancellation) and D73.
- **Open items:** none.

### OP-01/D98: A closed stream in the retry table (AX-13, reopen of `OP-01/O55`)

- **Scenario:** a caller reads a Closed stream, gets `ValueError` (D15), and
  makes a new OP-01 call.
- **Options:** (a) its own row in D60's table, the caller-error row excluding
  it; (b) outside the table, as misuse of a released object.
- **Recommendation:** (a): every D34 row keeps a retry row, and the new call
  opens a new stream, which does not meet the failure. Source: `OP-01/O55`,
  D15, D60.
- **Asked:** "OP-01/O55 (closed-stream retry): D60's row" → "Own row (Recommended)"
- **Decision:** (a). D60's caller-error row reads "caller error, other than a
  closed stream | repeats it: a function of the arguments"; D60's table gains
  "a read on a closed stream | a new call opens a new stream, which does not
  meet it".
- **Amends:** OP-01/D60
- **Consistency check:** consistent with D15, D34, D53 and D92 (an invalid
  argument on a Closed stream is a caller error, which repeats).
- **Open items:** none.
- **Amended by:** OP-01/D142

### OP-01/D99: A truncated file is E4's, not E12's (AX-10, reopen of `OP-01/O60`)

- **Scenario:** during O1's read of a 10 GB file on Local, O3 truncates it to
  4 GB; the read meets the end at 4 GB.
- **Options:** (a) E4 governs: E12 is a transfer short of the length the
  backend announced for it, and a file-system read ending short because of
  E4 follows D26; (b) a file-system read ending short of the size at open
  fails unavailable.
- **Recommendation:** (a): (b) is the best-effort detection D26 rejected,
  which a truncate-then-append to the same length escapes. Source:
  `OP-01/O60`, D26; POSIX `read(2)` returns 0 at the file's current end.
- **Asked:** "OP-01/O60 (E4 vs E12): on Local or SFTP" → "E4 governs (Recommended)"
- **Decision:** (a). E12 reads: a transfer ends with fewer bytes than the
  backend announced for that transfer, or fails an integrity check. On
  file-system backends a read that ends short of the item's size at open
  because of E4 is E4's: D26 and D48 govern, and it never surfaces as
  unavailable. D28's "a short or corrupt body is never returned as content"
  holds for E12.
- **Amends:** OP-01/E12
- **Consistency check:** consistent with D26, D28, D43's O3 in-place
  modification row and D48.
- **Open items:** none.
- **Amended by:** OP-01/D172

### OP-01/D100: `read1` in D18's table (AX-6, reopen of `OP-01/O58`)

- **Scenario:** an Open stream's caller calls `read1(1 MiB)`; D18's read
  column does not list it.
- **Options:** (a) D18's column cites D93's list of data reads; (b) D18's

- **Recommendation:** (a): one place enumerates the data reads, so the two
  cannot drift. Source: `OP-01/O58`, D93.
- **Asked:** "OP-01/O58 (read1 in D18): D18's read column lists" → "Cite D93's list (Recommended)"
- **Decision:** (a). D18's first column reads "a data read (D93's list)":
  `read`, `read1`, `readinto`, `readline`, line iteration and `chunks`,
  enumerated in D93 only.
- **Amends:** OP-01/D18
- **Consistency check:** consistent with D70 (`read1` draws from the one
  position), D78 and D93.
- **Open items:** none.
- **Amended by:** OP-01/D131

### OP-01/D101: The async stream's plain methods (AX-1, reopen of `OP-01/O58`)

- **Scenario:** an AsyncStore caller calls `s.tell()` and `s.seek(0)` on a
  stream.
- **Options:** (a) the methods that make a request are coroutines, the rest
  plain; (b) every method a coroutine, as D69's option text reads.
- **Recommendation:** (a): `tell`, `readable`, `seekable`, `seek`,
  `truncate` and `write` make no request, so awaiting them gains nothing.
  Source: `OP-01/O58`, D69; `asyncio.StreamReader` keeps `at_eof` plain.
- **Asked:** "OP-01/O58 (async non-I/O methods): D69 makes" → "I/O awaited, rest plain (Recommended)"
- **Decision:** (a). On the async stream, `read`, `read1`, `readinto`,
  `readline` and `close` are coroutines, `async for` and `chunks(size)` async
  iterators; `tell`, `readable`, `seekable`, `seek`, `truncate` and `write`
  are plain methods, the last three raising `io.UnsupportedOperation` when
  called (D70).
- **Amends:** OP-01/D69
- **Consistency check:** consistent with D1's parity (each method keeps its
  sync semantics), D69's plain-methods sentence, which this asks, D70 and
  D93.
- **Open items:** none.
- **Amended by:** OP-01/D127, OP-01/D147

### OP-01/D102: E16's exposure (AX-7, reopen of `OP-01/O59`)

- **Scenario:** O1 streams an Azure blob; O3 sets its metadata (E16); the

- **Decision:** (a). D62's "exposed at s3 only, through a resume" reads:

  resume, a recycled connection, a prefetch).
- **Amends:** OP-01/D62
- **Consistency check:** consistent with D22, D47, D61, D84, D86 (E18 reads
  the same) and NP2.
- **Open items:** none.

### OP-01/D108: What each remaining item blocks (reopen of `OP-01/O64`)

- **Scenario:** the flow that writes OP-01's spec reaches D11's clause,

  follows the session) and its Lands elsewhere table.

### OP-01/D117: A caller error on a Failed or Closed stream (AX-6, reopen of `OP-01/O66`)

- **Scenario:** a stream is Failed; the caller calls `chunks(0)`.
  Separately, a stream is Closed; the caller calls `read('x')`.

  error is raised first and leaves the state as it was; a valid data read
  (D93's list) on a Failed stream raises the failure again, a valid call on
  a Closed stream raises `ValueError`, so `chunks(0)` on a Failed stream
  raises `ValueError`; (b) D52's and D16's wording kept, the Failed state's
  failure raised before the argument check, against D92.
- **Recommendation:** (a): it is `io`'s order (✓ in `OP-01/O51`) and
  `OP-01/O51`'s first answer, "Reads only". Source: `OP-01/O66`, D92, D93.

- **Decision:** (a). In every state a caller error (D33) is raised first
  and leaves the state as it was (D80 as D92 amends it). D16's "every later
  read raises the same category", D18's Failed cell and D52's "every read on
  a Failed stream" read: every valid data read (D93's list) raises the
  failure again (D52, D95). D15's "reads raise `ValueError`", D18's Closed
  cell and D70's "on a Closed stream raises `ValueError` (D15)" read: every
  valid call but `close()` raises `ValueError`, and `close()` is a no-op.
  `chunks(0)` on a Failed stream raises `ValueError` and leaves it Failed;
  `read('x')` on a Closed stream raises `TypeError`.

- **Consistency check:** consistent with D33, D51, D80, D92, D93, D95, D96

  item's question as worded here, and no other answer to it is recorded.

### OP-01/D118: A cancelled close releases locally (AX-6, reopen of `OP-01/O67`)

- **Scenario:** an asyncio task awaiting a stream's `close()` is cancelled
  while the connection is being returned to its pool.
- **Options:** (a) released locally: a cancelled `close()` still frees every
  local resource, aborting the connection instead of returning it to the
  pool, so nothing waits for garbage collection; D94's "or abandoned"
  withdrawn; what the backend still holds on its side (an SFTP file handle
  until its session ends) joins NP5's out-of-band effects; (b) D50's cell
  admits abandoning, with a register row for when an abandoned resource is
  freed.

  abort frees at once. Source: `OP-01/O67`; asyncio `Transport.abort()`
  closes at once without waiting.

- **Decision:** (a). D94's Decision reads: a cancelled `close()` propagates
  the cancellation (D49) and leaves the stream Closed with every local
  resource released, the connection aborted rather than returned to its
  pool; a later `close()` is a no-op (D15). "Or abandoned" is withdrawn.
  D41's NP5 reads: out-of-band effects: atime, access logs, billing, and
  what a backend still holds on its side after a cancelled `close()` (an
  SFTP file handle until its session ends); scope D10, D118.

- **Consistency check:** consistent with D17 (NP4 stays for an unclosed
  stream only), D20 and D50's column ("releases; Closed"), which now hold as
  written, D36 and D49. An aborted connection changes no store state (D10).

### OP-01/D126: `readinto1` and `readlines` (AX-2, reopen of `OP-01/O71`)

- **Scenario:** `TextIOWrapper` or a copy loop calls `s.readinto1(buf)`; a
  caller calls `s.readlines()` and `s.readlines(4096)`.
- **Options:** (a) both, `io` semantics: `readinto1(b)` writes 1 to
  min(len(b), r) bytes in at most one backend request, 0 at the end, as
  `read1`; `readlines(hint=-1)` returns `readline()` results until the end,
  stopping once their total reaches hint > 0; both join D93's data-read
  list, coroutines on the async form, NP9's scope gaining `readlines`
  without a positive hint; (b) other semantics or omission.
- **Recommendation:** (a): D70 adopted `io.BufferedIOBase`'s contract for
  every method it names, and both draw from the one position. Source:
  `io.BufferedIOBase.readinto1`, `io.IOBase.readlines`.
- **Asked:** "OP-01/O71 (readinto1, readlines): D70 adopts io.BufferedIOBase" → "Both, io semantics (Recommended)"
- **Decision:** (a). With r bytes remaining, `readinto1(b)` writes 1 to
  min(len(b), r) bytes into b in at most one backend request and returns
  that count, 0 at the end; `readlines(hint=-1)` returns `readline()`
  results until the end, stopping once their total length reaches hint when
  hint > 0. Both draw from the one position (D2), join D93's data-read list
  (so a Failed stream raises the failure again, D18's column), and are
  coroutines on the async stream. NP9's scope reads: `readline` with a
  negative limit, `readlines` without a positive hint, and line iteration,
  both forms (D40, D71, D126).
- **Amends:** OP-01/D41, OP-01/D70, OP-01/D71, OP-01/D93
- **Consistency check:** consistent with D6, D70 (`read1`'s one-request
  rule), D93, D100 (D18's column cites D93's list) and D117. Conflicts
  named: D100's Decision enumerates D93's list again ("`read`, `read1`,
  `readinto`, `readline`, line iteration and `chunks`") without the two;
  D18's Exhausted cell, "`b''`, 0 for `readinto`" (D70), names neither 0
  for `readinto1` nor `[]` for `readlines`; D40's bound, as D71 amends it,
  "covers every read but `readline()` without a limit and line iteration",
  so it still claims `readlines()` without a hint, which NP9 now holds.
  Asked at step 5. Answered in log `07bd66b7` (2026-11-01, 14:58Z), written
  here per BK-510; (b) is `OP-01/O71`'s alternative.
- **Open items:** none.

### OP-01/D138: A cancelled close where the SDK is synchronous (AX-15, reopen of `OP-01/O76`)

- **Scenario:** an AsyncStore task awaiting an SFTP stream's `close()` is
  cancelled; the async form runs paramiko's close in a worker thread
  (`asyncio.to_thread`), which runs on after the cancellation propagates
  (✓ run in `OP-01/O76`).
- **Options:** (a) declare a deviation: D118 stays the normal value; a
  backend whose async close runs in a worker thread (SFTP) declares that the
  stream is Closed when the cancellation propagates, a later `close()` a
  no-op, the release completing when the worker's close returns, never left
  to garbage collection; D43 gains a seventh point, Cancelled close, and
  `OP-01/O13` reads seven; (b) shield, then propagate: the cancelled close
  waits for its worker's close to return, then propagates the cancellation,
  late by the close's duration; (c) abort from the loop by closing the SDK's
  socket from the event loop's thread, not run.
- **Recommendation:** (a): when unsure, the weaker side is declared (D43's
  reason), and the cancellation stays prompt. Source: `OP-01/O76`;
  `asyncio.to_thread` (✓ run there).
- **Asked:** "OP-01/O76 (cancelled close, sync SDK): D118 says" → "Declare a deviation (Recommended)"
- **Decision:** (a). D43's table gains the row:

  | Point | Normal value | Declared deviations, weakest last | Decided by |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | Cancelled close | a cancelled `close()` releases every local resource before the cancellation propagates, the connection aborted (D118) | the release completes when the worker's close returns, after the cancellation propagates (a backend whose async close runs in a worker thread: SFTP) | D50, D94, D118, D138 |

  In both, the stream is Closed when the cancellation propagates and a later
  `close()` is a no-op (D94); the release is never left to garbage
  collection. D120's column gains the row as listed. `OP-01/O13` reads:
  D43's seven variation points (Termination, Read-after-write, Pinned
  resume, O3 in-place modification, Overlapping mutation, Dual key,
  Cancelled close).
- **Amends:** OP-01/D43, OP-01/D118, OP-01/D120, OP-01/O13
- **Consistency check:** consistent with D20, D49, D50's column ("releases;
  Closed", the release completing later under the deviation), D94 as D118
  amends it, D41's NP5 (what the backend holds on its side) and D43's static
  declaration per backend (`OP-01/O12`). Which backends declare it is the
  profile's; SFTP is named from `OP-01/O76`'s read of the `sftp` extra.

### OP-01/D152: A pending release under Overlapping mutation (AX-4, close-out of `OP-01/O85`)

- **Scenario:** on an SFTP server running Windows, a task awaiting a
  stream's `close()` is cancelled; the stream is Closed (D138) while the
  worker's close still holds the server's file handle; O2 replaces the item.
- **Options:** (a) D81's exception reaches a stream whose release a
  cancelled `close()` left pending: until it completes, where D43's
  Overlapping mutation deviation is declared, an overlapping API replace,
  delete or move of that item may fail as under an open read; NP5's SFTP
  example reads "until the backend's close returns or its session ends";
  (b) on such a backend the release completes before the cancellation
  propagates, D138 reversed.
- **Recommendation:** (a): it states what D83 has a Windows server do with
  an open handle, and keeps D138's prompt cancellation. Source: `OP-01/O85`,
  D81, D83, D118, D138; server behaviour not run.
- **Asked:** "How should OP-01 be closed? Today, RFC-0029 R2" → "Close-out rule (Recommended)"
- **Decision:** (a). D10's exception, as D81 sets it, reads: except where
  D43's Overlapping mutation deviation is declared, where an open OP-01
  read, whole or streamed, or a stream whose release a cancelled `close()`
  left pending (D118, D138), may make an overlapping API replace, delete or
  move of that item fail; that mutation then leaves the state as it was
  before it, and OP-01 changes nothing else. D41's NP5, as D118 sets it,
  reads: out-of-band effects: atime, access logs, billing, and what a
  backend still holds on its side after a cancelled `close()` (an SFTP file
  handle until the backend's close returns or its session ends); scope D10,
  D118, D138. AX-12's row reads "while the read was open or its release
  pending".
- **Amends:** OP-01/D10, OP-01/D41, OP-01/D81
- **Consistency check:** consistent with D20, D24 and `OP-01/O8`, which
  carry D10's exception by citation, D43's Overlapping mutation row, D83,
  D94, D118 and D138. A held handle that makes an API mutation fail is D10's
  exception; NP5 keeps what no API call observes. Not run, as D83. A
  close-out decision, as D151.
- **Open items:** none.

### OP-01/D153: `io.UnsupportedOperation`, `LookupError` and other calls in the tables (AX-10, close-out of `OP-01/O86`)

- **Scenario:** a caller calls `seek(0)` on an Open stream and `tell()` on
  a Closed one, and looks both up in D34's and D96's tables.
- **Options:** (a) D34 gains a row for an unsupported method, its caller

  caller error, no row of its own, though D117 raises caller errors first
  and a Closed stream's `seek` raises `ValueError`.

  and D33's caller errors stay apart from a method that never works.

  `ValueError` (✓ run in `OP-01/O86`).

- **Decision:** (a). D34's table gains the row "an unsupported method (D70,

  row reads "caller error, closed stream | `TypeError`, `ValueError`,
  `LookupError` | builtin itself". D96's table gains a column, Other stream

  caller error, a closed stream's `ValueError` and the new row "an

  every other column. D38's caller-level table gains "an unsupported method
  | repeats it: a property of the method".

- **Consistency check:** consistent with D33, D55, D70, D93 as D151 sets
  it, D101 (other calls are plain and make no request, so no storage row
  and no cancellation reaches them), D117, D125, D137 and D142 (every
  category of D34's table has a phase row and a retry row). A close-out
  decision, as D151.
- **Open items:** none.

### OP-01/D154: `write` on a Closed stream (AX-6, close-out of `OP-01/O86`)

### OP-01/D155: `read1` and `readinto1` with a size that is not positive (AX-2, close-out of `OP-01/O87`)

- **Scenario:** with r > 0 bytes remaining, a caller calls `read1(0)`,
  `read1()` and `readinto1(bytearray())`.
- **Options:** (a) as `io`: `read1(0)` returns `b''`; `read1(n)` with n < 0
  returns 1 to r bytes as soon as at least one is available, how many
  *unspecified*; `readinto1(b)` and `readinto(b)` with len(b) = 0 return 0;
  none of these moves the position or the state, and on an Open stream a
  `b''` or 0 they return is not the end; (b) a size that is not positive is
  a caller error.
- **Recommendation:** (a): `io`'s values (✓ run in `OP-01/O87`), whose
  contract D70 adopts. Source: `OP-01/O87`.
- **Asked:** "How should OP-01 be closed? Today, RFC-0029 R2" → "Close-out rule (Recommended)"
- **Decision:** (a), as stated. D78's and D134's "1 to min(n, r)" and "1 to
  min(len(b), r)" hold for a positive size. D18's Exhausted cell, as D132
  sets it, names the end values of a positive size.
- **Amends:** OP-01/D78, OP-01/D132, OP-01/D134
- **Consistency check:** consistent with D6, D7 (`chunks` takes a positive
  size; `chunks(0)` stays a caller error, D33), D18, D41's NP2, D70, D93 and
  D148. A close-out decision, as D151.
- **Open items:** none.
- **Amended by:** OP-01/D173

### OP-01/D157: `read(0)` with bytes remaining (AX-2, reopen of `OP-01/O92`)

- **Scenario:** 10 bytes remain on an Open stream; the caller calls
  `read(0)`, then `read(10)`.
- **Options:** (a) as `io` and D155: `read(0)` returns `b''` and moves
  neither the position nor the state; on an Open stream that `b''` is not
  the end, and D6's end sentence reads "once a read of nonzero size returns
  `b''`"; the following `read(10)` returns all 10 bytes; (b) a caller error:
  `read(0)` raises `ValueError` before any effect (D33, D51), the state
  unchanged, which breaks `io`-contract code that calls `read(0)` and
  differs from D155's `read1(0)` and empty-buffer `readinto`.
- **Recommendation:** (a): it is `io`'s value, whose contract D70 adopts,
  and D155's rule for the other one-shot reads. Source: `OP-01/O92`,
  `io.BufferedIOBase.read`, D155.
- **Asked:** "OP-01/O92 (AX-2, read(0)): 10 bytes remain" → "As io and D155 (Recommended)"
- **Decision:** (a). D6's Decision reads: with r bytes remaining, `read(n)`
  for n ≥ 0 returns min(n, r) bytes, the next ones of the content;
  `read(n)` for n < 0 returns all r; `read(0)` moves neither the position
  nor the state, and on an Open stream the `b''` it returns is not the end.
  Bytes returned across all reads and chunks, in order, are a prefix of the
  content, the whole content once a read of nonzero size returns `b''`.
- **Amends:** OP-01/D6
- **Consistency check:** consistent with D2, D18's Open row ("next bytes",
  none for n = 0), D70, D132's Exhausted cell (`read(0)` returns `b''`
  there too), D155 (the same rule for `read1(0)` and an empty buffer) and
  D117 (`read(0)` is a valid call: on a Failed stream it raises the failure
  again, on a Closed one `ValueError`).
- **Open items:** none.

### OP-01/D158: When a stream enters Exhausted (AX-6, reopen of `OP-01/O93`)

- **Scenario:** a stream over a 10-byte item; `read(10)` returns all ten
  bytes; no read has yet returned `b''`; the next `read(10)` meets a failure
  in D96's Stream read column, or is cancelled (E14).
- **Options:** (a) when its last byte goes out: Exhausted once the bytes
  delivered reach the content's length fixed at open (D22), so the next read
  returns `b''` (D132) with no backend contact and fails only with a caller
  error; a cancellation still closes it (D50); under E4 the end is the one a
  read meets (D26); (b) when a read reports the end: Exhausted only once a
  read returns `b''` (or 0, `[]`), a read that may need a backend request
  and so can fail (Open to Failed, D16, D51) or be cancelled (Closed) after
  every byte arrived, a `copyfileobj` then failing with the whole content
  in hand, and D18's "starts Exhausted for an empty item" withdrawn;
  (c) *unspecified*: the read after the last byte returns `b''`, raises a
  stream-read failure (Failed) or is cancelled (Closed), a check accepting
  all three.
- **Recommendation:** (a): it matches D18's row labels, "Open (bytes
  remain)" and "Exhausted (at end)", and its "starts Exhausted for an empty
  item", which no read reports. Source: `OP-01/O93`, D18, D22; HTTP
  `Content-Length`. (b): `io.RawIOBase` (0 bytes signals the end). (c): Go
  `io.Reader` (n bytes with `EOF` or `nil`).
- **Asked:** "OP-01/O93 (AX-6, entry into Exhausted): a stream over a 10-byte item" → "When its last byte goes out (Recommended)"
- **Decision:** (a). A stream enters Exhausted when the read that delivers
  its last byte returns, the bytes delivered then reaching the length of
  the content fixed at open (D22); it starts Exhausted for an empty item
  (D18). A read on an Exhausted stream returns its end value (D132) with no
  backend contact, so it fails only with a caller error (D51), and a
  cancellation moves it to Closed (D50). Under E4 the end is the one a read
  meets (D26): the stream enters Exhausted when a read meets it.
- **Amends:** OP-01/D18
- **Consistency check:** consistent with D6 (`b''` at the end), D16 and D51
  (a storage failure or one raised as is moves Open to Failed, and an
  Exhausted read meets neither), D20 and D50 (a cancellation from Exhausted
  leads to Closed), D22 (the pinned content's length), D26, D96 (its Stream
  read column lists what a read can meet, which on an Exhausted stream is a
  caller error or a cancellation only), D132, D155 and D157 (`read(0)` and
  an empty buffer do not reach the end).
- **Open items:** none.
- **Amended by:** OP-01/D159, OP-01/D160, OP-01/D170

### OP-01/D159: Exhausted on a file-system backend (AX-6, reopen of `OP-01/O94`)

- **Scenario:** a stream on Local or SFTP over a 10-byte file; `read(10)`
  returns all ten bytes; O3 then appends two bytes (E4), or does not; the
  next `read(10)`.
- **Options:** (a) stop at the length fixed at open, on every backend: the
  stream is Exhausted once the bytes delivered reach the length fixed at
  open (D22), with no backend contact after; appended bytes are never
  delivered; a truncation under E4 still ends the stream early where a read
  meets it (D99); D26 reads "the read ends at the earlier of the end it
  meets and the length fixed at open"; no new variation point; (b) read on
  to meet the end on file-system backends: appended bytes may be delivered,
  and the read after the last byte contacts the backend without E4 and on
  SFTP can fail (Failed), against D158's "with no backend contact".
- **Recommendation:** (a): it keeps D158's entry into Exhausted the same on
  every backend, and a length known at open bounds what a stream delivers.
  Source: D22, D158; HTTP `Content-Length`.
- **Asked:** "OP-01/O94 (AX-6, Exhausted on Local and SFTP): a stream over a 10-byte file" → "Stop at open length (Recommended)"
- **Decision:** (a). On every backend a stream enters Exhausted when the
  bytes delivered reach the length of the content fixed at open (D22), and
  makes no backend contact after; bytes O3 appends under E4 are never
  delivered. Under E4 a read that meets the file's end before that length
  (a truncation, D99) ends the stream there, and the stream enters Exhausted
  when that read returns its end value. D158's E4 sentence reads so. D26's
  "the read ends at the end it meets" reads "the read ends at the earlier
  of the end it meets and the length fixed at open". D43's O3 in-place
  modification row is unchanged: no new variation point.
- **Amends:** OP-01/D26, OP-01/D158
- **Consistency check:** consistent with D6 (a prefix of the content, the
  whole once the end is returned), D22 (the length fixed at open), D41's
  NP7 (the content seen under E4 beyond D26's per-byte rule, which this
  rule now bounds above), D96 (an Exhausted read meets no stream-read
  failure), D99 (a truncation is E4's and ends the read where met) and
  D132. A Local file object would return appended bytes (✓ run in
  `OP-01/O94`); the stream stops before asking for them.
- **Open items:** none.
- **Amended by:** OP-01/D165, OP-01/D166, OP-01/D168, OP-01/D170
### OP-01/D160: What D158 promises of an Exhausted read (AX-6, reopen of `OP-01/O95`)

- **Scenario:** a check reads an Exhausted stream and counts the backend
  requests the read makes.
- **Options:** (a) the outcome only: the read returns its end value (D132),
  fails only with a caller error (D51), and a cancellation moves it to
  Closed (D50); its request count is NP2's, and "no backend contact" is the
  reason, not a promise; (b) zero requests promised, NP2 narrowed by a row
  for Exhausted reads.
- **Recommendation:** (a): NP2 keeps one scope, and a check asserts what a
  caller observes. Source: D41 option (a) as D107 restores it.
- **Asked:** "OP-01/O95 (AX-6 vs NP2): D158 says a read on an Exhausted stream" → "Outcome only (Recommended)"
- **Decision:** (a). D158's sentence reads: a read on an Exhausted stream
  returns its end value (D132); it fails only with a caller error (D51),
  and a cancellation moves it to Closed (D50). The reason: it needs no
  backend contact. How many requests it makes is NP2's, as for every call.
- **Amends:** OP-01/D158
- **Consistency check:** consistent with D41's NP2 and its "a test that
  asserts one is a defect" (D107), D78 and D134 ("the request count is
  NP2's"), D50, D51, D132 and D159 (no contact after the length fixed at
  open is the reason D159 gives as well, not a count).
- **Open items:** none.

### OP-01/D161: The state after an unsupported method (AX-6, reopen of `OP-01/O96`)

- **Scenario:** on an Open stream the caller calls `seek(0)`, catches
  `io.UnsupportedOperation`, then calls `read(3)`.
- **Options:** (a) state unchanged: an unsupported method (D153's row)
  raises before any effect and leaves every state as it was, as a caller
  error does; `read(3)` returns the next bytes; D51 reads "every failure but
  a caller error, an unsupported method and a cancellation"; D153's own
  category stays apart; (b) as is: the stream is Failed and `read(3)`
  raises again (D52), against `io` and D125's "as on an Open one".
- **Recommendation:** (a): an unsupported method touches no byte, so D51's
  reason for Failed ("may have consumed bytes") does not reach it. Source:
  `io.BufferedReader` (✓ run in `OP-01/O96`), D70, D125, D151.
- **Asked:** "OP-01/O96 (AX-6): on an Open stream the caller calls seek(0)" → "State unchanged (Recommended)"
- **Decision:** (a). D51's adopted option reads "every failure but a caller
  error, an unsupported method and a cancellation moves to Failed". Its
  Decision gains: in every state, an unsupported method (D70, D125, D153)
  raises `io.UnsupportedOperation` before any effect and leaves the state
  unchanged. D153's row keeps its own category: an unsupported method is
  not counted as a caller error.
- **Amends:** OP-01/D51
- **Consistency check:** consistent with D18 (no transition is added),
  D33 (caller errors stay apart), D52 (a Failed stream raises again for the
  data reads; an unsupported method there raises `io.UnsupportedOperation`
  as D153 has it), D70, D93 as D151 sets it, D125, D153 and D154 (`write`
  on a Closed stream, Closed unchanged).
- **Open items:** none.
- **Amended by:** OP-01/D164
### OP-01/D162: A release a cancelled read leaves pending (AX-4, reopen of `OP-01/O97`)

- **Scenario:** on an SFTP server running Windows, an AsyncStore task
  awaiting a stream read is cancelled; the stream is Closed (D20) while the
  worker thread still holds the server's file handle; O2 replaces the item.
- **Options:** (a) widen both: D138's deviation covers a cancelled stream
  read as well as a cancelled `close()`, the point renamed Cancelled
  release, and `OP-01/O13` reads so; D10's exception reads "a stream whose
  release a cancellation left pending", NP5's SFTP example likewise;
  (b) a cancelled read waits for its worker before propagating, so no
  release is pending after it, late by the read's duration.
- **Recommendation:** (a): the worker thread runs on after a cancelled read
  as after a cancelled close, and the cancellation stays prompt. Source:
  D138, D152; `asyncio.to_thread` (✓ run in `OP-01/O76`); server not run.
- **Asked:** "OP-01/O97 (AX-4, gap): on an SFTP server running Windows" → "Yes, widen both (Recommended)"
- **Decision:** (a). D43's row Cancelled close is renamed Cancelled
  release and reads:

  | Point | Normal value | Declared deviations, weakest last | Decided by |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | Cancelled release | a cancelled stream read or `close()` releases every local resource before the cancellation propagates, the connection aborted (D118) | the release completes when the worker's call returns, after the cancellation propagates (a backend whose async stream calls run in a worker thread: SFTP) | D50, D94, D118, D138, D162 |

  D10's exception, as D152 sets it, reads "a stream whose release a
  cancellation left pending (D118, D138)". D41's NP5 reads "what a backend
  still holds on its side after a cancellation (an SFTP file handle until
  the backend's call returns or its session ends)". `OP-01/O13`'s list
  names Cancelled release for Cancelled close.
- **Amends:** OP-01/D10, OP-01/D41, OP-01/D43, OP-01/D138, OP-01/O13
- **Consistency check:** consistent with D20 ("a cancelled stream read
  releases the stream"), D50's column (a read or `close` cancelled:
  releases; Closed), D81, D83, D94, D118 and D152. AX-12's row reads "while
  the read was open or its release pending", which covers it. Not run, as
  D83.
- **Open items:** none.

### OP-01/D163: Notes O98 to O101 go to the spec flow (reopen)

- **Scenario:** OP-01's spec flow meets the texts `OP-01/O98` to `O101`

- **Options:** (a) route them to OP-01's spec flow (RFC-0030 C1), which

- **Recommendation:** (a): each is a list, a restatement or a reason the

- **Asked:** "OP-01/O98 to O101 (notes, block nothing)" → "Route to spec flow (Recommended)"
- **Decision:** (a). `OP-01/O98`, `O99`, `O100` and `O101` each read
  "Routed to OP-01's spec flow by OP-01/D163." and block nothing.
- **Amends:** OP-01/O98, OP-01/O99, OP-01/O100, OP-01/O101
- **Consistency check:** a question no axis raises (the record's own text),

- **Open items:** none.

### OP-01/D164: An unsupported method on a Closed stream (AX-6, close-out of `OP-01/O102`)

- **Scenario:** a stream is Closed; the caller calls `seek(0)`.
- **Options:** (a) D117 holds on Closed: D161's "in every state" reads "on
  an Open, Exhausted or Failed stream"; on a Closed one D117 as D154 sets
  it holds, `write` raising `io.UnsupportedOperation` and every other
  unsupported method `ValueError`; (b) as `io`, per method: on a Closed
  stream `seek`, `truncate`, `writelines` and `detach` raise `ValueError`,
  `write` and `fileno` `io.UnsupportedOperation`, D161 and D125's `fileno`
  amended; (c) `io.UnsupportedOperation` in every state, Closed included,
  D117's Closed rule and D125's Closed sentence amended to except them.
- **Recommendation:** (a): only D161 changes, and its dialog asked about an
  Open stream; `io` as run agrees but for `fileno`. Source: D117, D125,
  D154; a closed `io.BufferedReader` (✓ run in `OP-01/O102`).
- **Asked:** "OP-01/O102 (AX-6, close-out): a stream is Closed; the caller calls seek(0)" → "D117 holds on Closed (Recommended)"
- **Decision:** (a). D161's sentence reads: on an Open, Exhausted or Failed
  stream, an unsupported method (D70, D125, D153) raises
  `io.UnsupportedOperation` before any effect and leaves the state
  unchanged. On a Closed stream D117 as D154 sets it holds: `write` raises
  `io.UnsupportedOperation`, and every other unsupported method raises
  `ValueError`, Closed unchanged.
- **Amends:** OP-01/D161
- **Consistency check:** consistent with D51 as D161 amends it, D70, D117
  as D137 and D154 set it, D125 (on a Closed stream `fileno()`,
  `writelines()` and `detach()` raise `ValueError`, where `io` raises
  `io.UnsupportedOperation` from `fileno`, ✓ run in `OP-01/O102`) and D153
  (its option (b): "a Closed stream's `seek` raises `ValueError`"). A
  close-out decision, as D151.
- **Open items:** none.

### OP-01/D165: D159's "no backend contact" is a reason (AX-6, close-out of `OP-01/O103`)

- **Scenario:** a check counts the backend requests after the last byte of
  a stream on Local and finds one.
- **Options:** (a) a reason, as D160: D159's "and makes no backend contact
  after" reads as D160's reason, its request count NP2's; (b) zero requests
  promised, D160 amended back and NP2's scope excepting a read on an
  Exhausted stream.
- **Recommendation:** (a): NP2 keeps one scope, and D159's outcome is
  unchanged. Source: D160; D41 as D107 restores it.
- **Asked:** "OP-01/O103 (AX-6 vs NP2, close-out): D159 says a stream 'makes no backend contact after'" → "A reason, as D160 (Recommended)"
- **Decision:** (a). D159's first sentence reads: on every backend a stream
  enters Exhausted when the bytes delivered reach the length of the content
  fixed at open (D22); bytes O3 appends under E4 are never delivered. The
  reason: no read after that needs the backend. How many requests a stream
  makes is NP2's (D160).
- **Amends:** OP-01/D159
- **Consistency check:** consistent with D41's NP2 and "a test that asserts
  one is a defect" (D107), D158 as D160 sets it, and D160's check ("the
  reason D159 gives as well, not a count"), which now holds of D159's text.
  A close-out decision, as D151.
- **Open items:** none.

### OP-01/D166: The length a Local stream stops at (AX-6, close-out of `OP-01/O104`)

- **Scenario:** a Local store rooted where `/proc/self/status` (size 0 at
  open, 1417 bytes read) or a sysfs file (size 4096, 23 bytes read) is an
  item; O1 streams it, nothing changes it.
- **Options:** (a) the size at open, stated: Local's length fixed at open is
  the file's size at open; the stream ends at the earlier of the end it
  meets and that size; for a file whose content length is not its size the
  content delivered is not promised whole, a non-promise row beside NP7;
  (b) out of OP-01's scope: nothing promised for such a file, D159 reading
  "for a file whose size at open is its length".
- **Recommendation:** (a): it keeps D159's rule and states what the OS
  gives. Source: D159, D26; `fstat` (✓ run in `OP-01/O104`).
- **Asked:** "OP-01/O104 (AX-6, Local, close-out): /proc/self/status is a regular file" → "Size at open, stated (Recommended)"
- **Decision:** (a). On Local the length of the content fixed at open (D22,
  D159) is the file's size at open, and a stream ends at the earlier of the
  end a read meets and that size, with or without E4. D41's register gains:

  | ID | Not promised | Scope |
  |---|---|---|
  | `OP-01/NP10` | The whole content of a file whose content length is not its size at open (procfs, sysfs): a stream delivers at most that size, ending earlier where a read meets the end | Local streams |

- **Amends:** OP-01/D41, OP-01/D159
- **Consistency check:** consistent with D6 (a prefix of the content; the
  whole once the end is returned, which NP10 scopes out for such files as
  NP7 does under E4), D22, D26, D158 (an item of size 0 at open starts
  Exhausted) and D159 as D165 sets it. The question asked about Local; an
  SFTP server's procfs or sysfs file reports a size at open the same way
  (not run), which D159 leaves to the size the server reports and NP10 does
  not reach; the spec flow meets it, as D163's check left its lists. A
  close-out decision, as D151.
- **Open items:** none.
- **Amended by:** OP-01/D168

### OP-01/D167: Notes O105 and O106 go to the spec flow; O107's 0.33 citations are dropped (close-out)

- **Scenario:** OP-01's spec flow meets the texts `OP-01/O105` and `O106`
  name; freeze-2 meets RFC-0031 F2's sentence on `O29`, `O42`, `O76` and

- **Options:** (a) route `O105` and `O106` to OP-01's spec flow, as D163
  did; drop each 0.33 citation in `O29`, `O42`, `O76` and `O88`, each item

  staying until the spec flow.
- **Recommendation:** (a): it meets F2's "re-witnesses each against the SDK
  or OS, or drops it" for freeze-2. Source: RFC-0030 C1, C2; RFC-0031 F2.
- **Asked:** "OP-01/O105 to O107 (notes, block nothing): O105 texts D159 leaves stale" → "Route; drop O107's 0.33 (Recommended)"
- **Decision:** (a). `OP-01/O105` and `O106` each read "Routed to OP-01's
  spec flow by OP-01/D167." and block nothing. In `O29`, `O42`, `O76` and
  `O88` each citation of 0.33 code (`_azure.py`, `_sftp.py`,
  `src/remote_store/aio/_sync_adapter.py`,
  `src/remote_store/backends/_memory.py`) carries no weight and is dropped,
  each item reading "0.33 citation dropped by OP-01/D167"; O29 stands as

- **Consistency check:** a question no axis raises (the record's own text),

### OP-01/D168: The length an SFTP stream stops at (AX-6, reopen of `OP-01/O108`)

### OP-01/D170: An SFTP stream with no size reported (AX-6, reopen of `OP-01/O109`)

- **Scenario:** an SFTP server reports an item's attributes without a size
  (no size flag; paramiko's `st_size` is `None`, ✓ run in `OP-01/O109`);
  O1 streams it.
- **Options:** (a) the end a read meets: no length is fixed at open, the
  stream ends where a read meets the end and enters Exhausted when that
  read returns its end value; it starts Open even for an empty item; bytes
  O3 appends under E4 may be delivered; NP10 does not reach it; D43 gains a
  row "Length at open", normal value known at open (D159), declared
  deviation unknown (an SFTP server that reports no size); the read after
  the last byte contacts the server and can fail (Failed), as D158's option
  (b); (b) fail at open with the base category (D33): no length is ever
  guessed and D158 and D159 hold unchanged on every stream that opens, but
  such an item cannot be streamed, though it is readable; D96's Stream open
  column gains the case.
- **Recommendation:** (a): the item stays streamable, and the content is
  delivered whole where nothing changes it. Source: D158 option (b);
  `io.RawIOBase` (0 bytes signals the end); draft-ietf-secsh-filexfer-02
  § 5 (the size attribute is flagged), not read here.
- **Asked:** "OP-01/O109 (AX-6, gap in D168): an SFTP server reports" → "End a read meets (Recommended)"
- **Decision:** (a). On SFTP, where the server reports no size at open, no
  length is fixed at open: D159's and D168's rule does not apply, a stream
  ends where a read meets the end, and it enters Exhausted when that read
  returns its end value; it starts Open, an empty item included, so D18's
  and D158's "starts Exhausted for an empty item" holds where a length is
  fixed at open. Bytes O3 appends under E4 may be delivered, D159's "never
  delivered" holding where a length is fixed at open. NP10 does not reach
  such a stream. D43's table gains:

  | Point | Normal value | Declared deviations, weakest last | Decided by |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | Length at open | known at open: a stream stops at the earlier of the end a read meets and that length (D159) | unknown: a stream ends where a read meets the end, and the read that meets it can fail (an SFTP server that reports no size) | D158, D159, D166, D168, D170 |

- **Amends:** OP-01/D18, OP-01/D43, OP-01/D158, OP-01/D159, OP-01/D168
- **Consistency check:** consistent with D6 (a prefix of the content, the
  whole once a read of nonzero size returns `b''`, which here holds without
  NP10's exception, absent E4), D16 and D51 (the read that meets the end is
  a read on an Open stream, so a stream-read failure moves it to Failed),
  D22 (every byte from the content at t₀), D26 and D99 (under E4 the end
  met governs; with no size announced, a short end is never E12's), D41's
  NP2 and NP7, D96 (its Stream read column applies to that read), D132,
  D160 and D165, whose reason "no read after that needs the backend" holds
  where a length is fixed at open only; the outcome they state for an
  Exhausted stream is unchanged. D43's Pinned resume row is unchanged: an
  SFTP stream does not resume (D42). The new row's Decided-by cell follows
  D120's rule; D43's O3 in-place modification cell lacks D170 (appended
  bytes) as it lacks D159, which D163's check leaves to the spec flow
  (`OP-01/O98`). D22's check restates D18's "Exhausted at open" for an empty
  item, which D18 as amended here qualifies; a restatement as
  `OP-01/O99` records, for the spec flow. A question no axis raises beyond
  AX-6: the variation is AX-15's, named here.
- **Open items:** none.
- **Amended by:** OP-01/D171, OP-01/D174

### OP-01/D171: SFTP declares Length at open unknown (AX-15, close-out of `OP-01/O111`)

- **Scenario:** an SFTP store whose server reports a size for some items and
  none for others.
- **Options:** (a) static per backend: the Length at open row's deviation
  names SFTP, every SFTP store declares unknown, and a caller assumes the
  weaker value on every SFTP stream, learning it per store through OP-18
  (D44); a stream whose server reports a size meets the normal value, which
  the deviation admits; D82 unchanged; (b) a declaration conditioned on the
  item's own attributes reply, D82 amended; (c) the store's configuration
  states whether its server reports sizes, an unnamed server taking unknown.
- **Recommendation:** (a): D82's rule holds unchanged and D43's unknown case
  takes the weaker side. Source: D43, D82; paramiko 5.1.0 `st_size` `None`
  with no size flag (✓ run in `OP-01/O109`).
- **Asked:** "OP-01/O111 (AX-15, close-out): D170's Length at open row declares 'unknown'" → "SFTP declares unknown (Recommended)"
- **Decision:** (a). D43's Length at open row, as D170 sets it, reads in its
  deviation cell "unknown: a stream ends where a read meets the end, and the
  read that meets it can fail (SFTP)". The declaration is static per backend
  (D82): every SFTP store declares it, and a caller learns it per store
  through OP-18 (D44). D170's behaviour per stream is unchanged: a stream
  whose server reports a size at open meets the normal value (D168), which
  the deviation admits; one whose server reports none behaves as D170 says.
- **Amends:** OP-01/D43, OP-01/D170
- **Consistency check:** consistent with D43's recommendation ("the unknown
  case takes the weaker side"), D44, D82 ("static per backend, never probed,
  never requested"), D168 and D170, and with AX-15's counterexample (behaviour
  outside "the normal value plus its declared deviations"), which the normal
  value never meets. A close-out decision, as D151.
- **Open items:** none.

### OP-01/D172: A size at open is no announcement under E12 (AX-10, close-out of `OP-01/O112`)

- **Scenario:** a sysfs file, size 4096 at open, 23 bytes read (✓ run,
  CPython 3.13.18, `OP-01/O112`), streamed on Local or through an SFTP
  server, no E4.
- **Options:** (a) not an announcement: on Local and SFTP no transfer
  announces a length; a read that ends short of the size at open is never
  E12's, with or without E4; D166, D168 and D170 unchanged; (b) the size at
  open is E12's announcement: the stream fails unavailable after 23 bytes
  except under E4, D166 and D168 amended, NP10 removed.
- **Recommendation:** (a): it states what the OS and the SDK do. Source:
  POSIX `read(2)` returns 0 at the file's current end; paramiko 5.1.0's
  `SFTPFile` read ends at the server's EOF status and compares no size
  (✓ source read: `file.py` `BufferedFile.read` catches the `EOFError` that
  `sftp_client.py` `_convert_status` raises for `SFTP_EOF`; `sftp_file.py`
  `_read` reads no `st_size`).
- **Asked:** "OP-01/O112 (AX-10, close-out): a sysfs file has size 4096 at open" → "Not an announcement (Recommended)"
- **Decision:** (a). D99's second sentence reads: on file-system backends
  (Local, SFTP) no transfer announces a length, a size at open included, so
  a read that ends short of the item's size at open is never E12's, with or
  without E4: under E4 D26 and D48 govern, otherwise D166 and D168 (NP10);
  it never surfaces as unavailable. D99's E12 sentence is unchanged.
- **Amends:** OP-01/D99
- **Consistency check:** consistent with D26, D28 as D99 leaves it, D48,
  D166, D168 and D170's check ("with no size announced, a short end is never
  E12's"), which now holds of every file-system stream. paramiko's
  `SFTPClient.get` does compare `st_size` after a whole download
  (`sftp_client.py`, "size mismatch in get!", ✓ source read); it is a helper
  no decision adopts, and D99 speaks of transfers, not of a helper's check.
  A close-out decision, as D151.
- **Open items:** none.

### OP-01/D173: `read1` with n < 0 moves the position (AX-2, close-out of `OP-01/O113`)

- **Scenario:** with 10 bytes remaining, a caller calls `read1(-1)`, then
  `tell()`.
- **Options:** (a) as `io`: D155's "none of these moves the position or the
  state" covers the zero-size cases only; `read1(n)` with n < 0 draws from
  the one position (D70), `tell()` advancing by what it returns; (b) n < 0
  is a caller error, departing from `io` and D70.
- **Recommendation:** (a): `io.BufferedReader.read1(-1)` over 10 bytes
  returns 10 and `tell()` is then 10 (✓ run, CPython 3.13.18, in `OP-01/O113`
  and again in this close-out), the contract D70 adopts. Source: D70, D155.
- **Asked:** "OP-01/O113 (AX-2, close-out): D155 adopts" → "Zero-size cases only (Recommended)"
- **Decision:** (a). D155's sentence reads: neither `read1(0)` nor
  `readinto1(b)` or `readinto(b)` with len(b) = 0 moves the position or the
  state. `read1(n)` with n < 0 draws from the one position (D70), `tell()`
  advancing by the bytes it returns, and enters Exhausted as any read does
  (D158, D159; D170 where no length is fixed at open). The rest of D155 is
  unchanged.
- **Amends:** OP-01/D155
- **Consistency check:** consistent with D6, D70, D78 and D134 (positive
  sizes, unchanged). The answer named D158 and D159; D170, which decides the
  entry into Exhausted where no length is fixed at open, is added for the
  same rule. `OP-01/O99`'s r = 0 text and `O100`'s bound stay routed (D163).
  A close-out decision, as D151.
- **Open items:** none.

### OP-01/D174: `OP-01/O13` names Length at open (AX-15, close-out of `OP-01/O114`)

- **Scenario:** OP-18's session reads `OP-01/O13` to learn which variation
  points a caller must be able to learn.
- **Options:** (a) O13's list names eight points, Length at open after
  Cancelled release, as D76, D91, D138 and D162 each amended O13 on adding
  or renaming a point; D170's Amends gains `OP-01/O13`; (b) Length at open
  stays out of O13, undeclared to callers.
- **Recommendation:** (a): AX-15 admits no undeclared deviation. Source: D44,
  D138, D162, D170.
- **Asked:** "OP-01/O114 (AX-15, close-out): OP-01/O13, as D138 and D162 set it" → "O13 gains Length at open (Recommended)"
- **Decision:** (a). `OP-01/O13` reads: D43's eight variation points
  (Termination, Read-after-write, Pinned resume, O3 in-place modification,
  Overlapping mutation, Dual key, Cancelled release, Length at open). D170's
  Amends reads as naming `OP-01/O13` too.
- **Amends:** OP-01/D170, OP-01/O13
- **Consistency check:** consistent with D44 ("OP-18 must expose D43's
  points"), D138's list as D162 renames it, and D171 (SFTP declares the
  point). A close-out decision, as D151.
- **Open items:** none.
