## Open items

`OP-01/O14` to `O16` were found in PR #1188's review, after the session had
closed every axis, and `O17` to `O26` by
[audit-025](../../audits/audit-025-op-01-record-review.md) after its merge;
`O14` to `O17`, `O19`, `O20` and `O22` run as reopens and `O21` as a ground
conflict (the skill's step 4), `O24` to `O26` as gaps left explicitly open
(step 3), and `O18` and `O23` are notes, so the status stays Complete. An
axis row lists every open item that reopens one of the decisions it cites,
finds a gap in one, or names one as dependent; a note opens none.

- `OP-01/O1`: for OP-02's session. OP-01/D2 leaves positioned reads,
  Parquet's footer-first read among them, to OP-02's reader, which then has
  to be a seekable file object pyarrow accepts. Blocks nothing here; OP-02
  decides.
- `OP-01/O2`: for OP-09's and OP-20's sessions. OP-01/D10 lets no read
  change what they report, so the modification time OP-09 reports cannot be
  one a read moves (atime). Blocks nothing here.
- `OP-01/O3`: `shared: State`. OP-01/D11: a handle holds its configuration
  and lifetime and nothing else that changes an operation's outcome; caching
  is an extension's state. Where it lands is decided after this session.
  Amended by OP-01/D108.
- `OP-01/O4`: `shared: Lands elsewhere, A timeout bound`. OP-01/D12 and D13:
  T_connect and T_idle per backend request, defaults 20 s and 60 s, no
  whole-call deadline. Each entry's AX-5 still states its own claim.
  Amended by OP-01/D104. Amended by OP-01/D108.
- `OP-01/O5`: `shared: Lifetime`. What a stream OP-01 handed out does once
  its handle's lifetime ends (OP-01/D18's last column for Open, Exhausted and
  Failed). Blocked on OP-21's flow, which shared.md names for how a lifetime
  ends. Amended by OP-01/D104. Amended by OP-01/D108.
- `OP-01/O6`: for the sessions of OP-03 to OP-05 and OP-12 to OP-15.
  OP-01/D21 promises a whole read one content at one instant, which holds
  only if every API mutation changes an item's content atomically as a
  reader sees it (temp-and-rename on file-system backends). Each session
  decides it or records a reopen of D21. Amended by OP-01/D108.
- `OP-01/O7`: `shared: unhomed`. OP-01/D23: real-time read-after-write
  order across API mutations and reads, strong as the normal value, eventual
  as a declared deviation. Amended by OP-01/D104. Amended by OP-01/D108.
- `OP-01/O8`: `shared: Handle`. OP-01/D24: a handle is safe for overlapping
  calls from threads (Store) or tasks on one event loop (AsyncStore).
  Amended by OP-01/D81. Amended by OP-01/D108.
- `OP-01/O9`: `shared: Lands elsewhere, Exception typing`. The error layer
  RFC-0024 D5 grows from OP-01's flow, which names its home: OP-01/D27 to
  D31, D33 and D34 give its principle and its first categories. Where it lands is
  decided after this session. Amended by OP-01/D104. Amended by OP-01/D108.
- `OP-01/O10`: `shared: Lifetime`. OP-01/D37: a handle's lifetime starts
  when the caller holds it; creating it contacts no backend. Amended by
  OP-01/D108.
- `OP-01/O11`: `shared: Lands elsewhere, Retry coupled to idempotency`.
  OP-01/D38: the library retries transient failures of a request that
  changes nothing, bounded per store (default 3 attempts per backend
  request), and never a classification, access or changed failure. Each
  entry's AX-13 still decides its own. Amended by OP-01/D74. Amended by
  OP-01/D108.
- `OP-01/O12`: `shared: unhomed`. OP-01/D43: a variation is fixed statically
  per backend, selected by store configuration where one backend serves
  services that differ, an undeclared service taking the deviations; never
  probed. Amended by OP-01/D82. Amended by OP-01/D108.
- `OP-01/O13`: for OP-18's session (shared.md Lands elsewhere, How a caller
  learns a store's profile). A caller must be able to learn OP-01/D43's four
  variation points for a store. Blocks nothing here. Amended by OP-01/D76.
  Amended by OP-01/D91. Amended by OP-01/D138. Amended by OP-01/D162.
  Amended by OP-01/D174.
- `OP-01/O14`: a reopen of OP-01/D35 against D19, D20 and D34, found in
  PR #1188's review. D35's Decision says a stream read surfaces "only"
  unavailable, timeout, changed, the base category, authentication failed and
  the caller errors. No dialog answered that list: the chosen option fixes
  what surfaces from the open. D19's table exposes E6 and E15 at s3, which
  D34 types as permission denied and not readable; D20 propagates
  cancellation out of a stream read; D34 has E13's memory and descriptor
  failures surface as raised. None is in D35's list. To decide with the
  maintainer: widen the read-phase list, or narrow D19's s3 cells for E6 and
  E15. Linked with `OP-01/O15`: narrowing D19's s3 cell for E15 leaves O15
  no mid-stream case, and O15 typing E15 as not readable mid-stream widens
  this list. Blocks the spec clause for a stream read's failures.
  Audit-025 § 3 argues for widening: a resume (D39) is a new request, which
  after E6 meets permission denied and after E15 not readable. Decided by
  OP-01/D45.
- `OP-01/O15`: a reopen of OP-01/D25 against D30 and D34, found in PR
  #1188's review. D25's Decision adds E15 to the O3 actions under which D21
  to D23 apply, so a stream meeting E15 is pinned or fails changed; its
  dialog asked about E3 and E5 only. D30 and D34 type E15 as not readable.
  To decide with the maintainer: whether E15 belongs in D25, and which category
  it surfaces mid-stream. Dependent: D25, D34's E15 row and D35's read-phase
  list; linked with `OP-01/O14`. Blocks the spec clause for E15 during a
  stream. Audit-025 § 3: a tier change keeps the ETag as far as that audit
  knows (not run), so a pinned resume passes its pin and fails not readable,
  not changed. Decided by OP-01/D47.
- `OP-01/O16`: a reopen of OP-01/D19 against D36, found in PR #1188's
  review. D19's exposure table gives E14 no exposure at s4, and its text says
  s4 "reports nothing"; D36 says `close()` raises a cancellation (E14, D20).
  Neither the table nor the sentence was a dialog's question. To decide with the
  maintainer: whether E14's s4 cell becomes `yes`. Dependent: D19's table and
  its sentence on s4. Blocks the spec's exposure clause for a stream's
  close. Decided by OP-01/D49.
- `OP-01/O17`: a reopen of OP-01/D34 against D33 (audit-025 C1). D33 types so
  that `except` on the store's base never hides a caller's bug; D34 puts key
  refused, most often a caller's bug, under that base. Key refused, decode
  failures, closed-stream reads and caller errors are all `ValueError`. To
  decide with the maintainer: key refused under the base or a caller error
  alone, and whether D33's reason is narrowed. Dependent: D34's first row,
  D9's key-refused row. Blocks the spec clause for key refused's category.
  Decided by OP-01/D53.
- `OP-01/O18`: a note on OP-01/D39's option (a), not a reopen (audit-025
  C2). The option, which D39's Decision adopts, says "the next read resumes";
  D16 puts recovery inside a read, and D6's `read(n)` returns min(n, r)
  bytes, so the resume happens inside the read that met the reset. D39's
  Decision bullet already says so ("without a gap or a repeated byte"), so the
  spec writes D39's clause from that bullet. To decide with the maintainer:
  whether the option's wording is corrected. Decided by OP-01/D72.
- `OP-01/O19`: a reopen of OP-01/D25 and D43 against Local on Windows
  (audit-025 C3). An open Local stream's handle makes `os.replace` and
  `os.remove` fail there, so an O2 write or delete fails while a stream is
  open, against D10's "nothing any API call observes changes"; D25's open-descriptor argument
  holds on POSIX only. To decide with the maintainer: a declared deviation in
  D43's table, or a promise that needs a share-delete open. Dependent: D10,
  D21, D22, D25, D43. Blocks the spec clauses for Local's pinning and frame. Decided by
  OP-01/D65.
- `OP-01/O20`: a reopen of OP-01/D21 against D9 (audit-025 C4). On object
  stores D9's folder check is a second request after the GET, and D21 takes
  classification from one instant. With `d` and `d/f` absent at the GET (404),
  O3 creating `d` and only then `d/f` before the listing makes the read report
  not an item, while `d` was absent, then an item, then both (`O21`'s state):
  no instant in the interval has it a folder and not an item. To decide with
  the maintainer: the argument for a composed classification, or an exception
  to D21. Linked with `OP-01/O21`. Blocks D21's spec clause for a failed read. Decided by OP-01/D66.
- `OP-01/O21`: a ground conflict, TREE-001 (spec 051) against S3 (audit-025
  C5). O3 can create the objects `a.txt` and `a.txt/b` together, a state
  where an entity is both an item and a folder, which TREE-001 excludes; KEY-013
  then gives `a.txt/b` BlockedByItem, so D9 says not found while a GET returns
  content. No Environment row names the state. To decide with the
  maintainer: an Environment row with its outcome, a declared non-promise, or
  a reopen of spec 051 through its own flow. Dependent: D9, D19's table, D21.
  Blocks D9's spec clause on object stores. Decided by OP-01/D67.
- `OP-01/O22`: a reopen of OP-01/D33 against D28 and D38 (audit-025 C6).
  Descriptor exhaustion (E13) propagates as raised on Local, but surfaces
  through an SDK as a connection failure that D28 reads as E7, retried and
  typed unavailable. To decide with the maintainer: one category for the
  cause on every backend, or a declared variation. Dependent: D33, D34's E13
  rows. Blocks the spec clause for E13. Decided by OP-01/D54.
- `OP-01/O23`: a note on OP-01/D12's reason, not a reopen (audit-025 C7).
  With no whole-call deadline, a caller's own deadline is a cancellation, and
  D20 closes a stream whose read is cancelled; D12's reason does not name that
  trade-off. To decide with the maintainer: whether D12's reason records it.
  D12 and D20 stay decided, so the spec writes their clauses as they stand.
  Decided by OP-01/D58.
- `OP-01/O24`: a gap in OP-01/D1 and D2 (audit-025 G1). D2's file object is
  for the sync form; for `AsyncStore`, what `async for` yields and whether an
  async `readline` exists are undecided, so D1's parity cannot hold
  literally. To decide with the maintainer: the async stream's shape. Blocks
  the async form's stream clauses. Decided by OP-01/D69.
- `OP-01/O25`: a gap in OP-01/D2, D6 and D18 (audit-025 G2). `readinto` and
  `readline` are in D18's table without semantics; `read1`, `readline(limit)`,
  `tell()`, `readable()` and `seekable()` are undecided. To decide with the
  maintainer: each method's value, as D6 gives `read(n)`'s. Blocks the
  file object's spec clauses beyond `read`. Decided by OP-01/D70.
- `OP-01/O26`: three gaps (audit-025 G3 to G5). An unknown `encoding` raises
  `LookupError`, outside D33's caller errors; D16's "the same failure again"
  leaves open whether one exception object is re-raised; D35's open-call list
  omits unavailable and timeout, which D28 implies. To decide with the
  maintainer: each. Dependent: D3, D16, D33, D35. Blocks the clauses each
  names. Decided by OP-01/D46, OP-01/D52 and OP-01/D55.

`OP-01/O27` to `O44` were found by `/observe-review OP-01` on 2026-10-28
(RFC-0028), each naming its source: reader 1 or 2 with the finding's label,
the primed review, or a candidate listed in BK-485's dossier. `O27`, `O28`,
`O31`, `O33` to `O36`, `O41` and `O43` run as reopens, `O37`, `O39` and `O44`
as gaps, and `O29`, `O30`, `O32`, `O38`, `O40` and `O42` are notes. A backend,
SDK or runtime claim is marked ✓ where this review read its source or ran it,
else not run.

- `OP-01/O27`: a reopen of OP-01/D28 and D34 against D12 (reader 1 1a,
  reader 2 1c, dossier). E7 includes "connect never completes"; D12 has a
  request fail "with the timeout category when connecting takes longer than
  T_connect"; D28 says "E7, E8, E10 and E12 surface as unavailable, E9 as
  timeout", and D34 gives E7 no builtin parent and E9 `TimeoutError`. So
  `except TimeoutError` catches a stalled connect under D12 and not under
  D34. The SDKs keep the two apart: botocore 1.43.117 `ConnectTimeoutError`
  against `EndpointConnectionError`, aiohttp 3.14.5 `ConnectionTimeoutError`
  against `ClientConnectorError` ✓. To decide with the maintainer: whether a
  stalled connect is timeout or unavailable, E7 split accordingly.
  Dependent: D12, D34's E7 and E9 rows, D38. Linked with `OP-01/O41`. Blocks
  the spec clause typing E7. Decided by OP-01/D56.
- `OP-01/O28`: a reopen of OP-01/D42 and D43's deviation category against
  D22 and D31 (reader 1 1b and 3a, reader 2 1b and 3b, dossier). D22: "If the
  backend can no longer serve that content, the read that needs it fails with
  the changed category"; D25 and D34's "E1 to E5 against a pinned stream |
  changed" repeat it with no exception. D39's option, D42 and D43 give a
  backend that cannot pin "no resume: fails unavailable (SFTP; an
  S3-compatible endpoint not declared to pin)"; D22's consistency check names
  the variation point, its Decision does not. There a replaced item surfaces
  as unavailable, which D38's table says "may succeed" on a new call: the
  outcome D31 rejected its option (b) for, "callers who retry unavailable
  restart the read, possibly getting the new content without noticing it
  changed". To decide with the maintainer: whether D22, D25 and D34 state the
  normal value only, and whether the deviation's category stays unavailable.
  Dependent: D22, D25, D34's changed row, D39. Blocks the spec clause for a
  lost pin. Decided by OP-01/D61.
- `OP-01/O29`: a note on OP-01/D9's option (a), not a reopen (reader 1 1c,
  reader 2 1a, dossier, primed). The option D9 adopts tells a folder from
  absence "with one listing request on the failure path only". D41 registers
  D9's listing under NP2, its rejected option (b) "rules out D9's listing
  folded into the success path", and its check says "NP2 leaves D9's extra
  listing free to happen on the failure path only or otherwise", so D41's "no
  row contradicts a decision" fails on the option's words. Azure with a
  hierarchical namespace tells a folder on the success path: a directory is a
  blob carrying `hdi_isfolder`, which 0.33's backend reads by a HEAD before
  streaming (`_azure.py:685` to `:697` ✓ read; the service not run). D9's
  Decision and its check ("a request count, AX-14's") agree with D41, so the
  spec writes from them. To decide with the maintainer: whether the option's
  wording is corrected. Decided by OP-01/D73. Amended by OP-01/D167. 0.33 citation dropped by OP-01/D167.
- `OP-01/O30`: a note on the Session section (reader 1 2a, reader 2 2a,
  dossier). "Formal models not read: no decision cites a clause they check."
  D4 cites KEY-001, KEY-013 and TREE-002, and D9's partition rests on
  KEY-001, KEY-013 and TREE-001. `KeyModel.lean` tags `@spec KEY-001` (l.273)
  and `@spec KEY-013` (l.631), and `StoreAxioms.als` and `StoreAxioms.lean`
  tag TREE-001 and TREE-002 ✓; spec 052's Tested against says `KeyModel.lean`
  "restates the sections and informs review; no bridge makes it govern". This
  review did not compare the models with D4 and D9. To decide with the
  maintainer: whether the line is corrected, and whether a reopen session
  reads the models. Decided by OP-01/D68.
- `OP-01/O31`: a reopen of OP-01/D25 against D41 and D43 (reader 1 1e and 2b,
  reader 2 2g and § 4, dossier). D25's dialog adopted "Same rights as O2",
  whose option reads "D21 to D23 apply unchanged"; the Decision adds "D23's
  real-time order is promised only for API mutations; when an O3 change
  becomes visible is the backend's", which no dialog asked. That claim's
  truth differs by backend, yet neither D43's table, which `OP-01/O13`
  counts as "four variation points", nor D41's register has a row for it,
  and AX-15's check fails on a backend that "deviates undeclared". To decide
  with the maintainer: whether O3's visibility is a variation point, a
  registered non-promise, or D23 applied to O3 as the option says.
  Dependent: D41, D43, `OP-01/O13`. Blocks the spec clause for when an O3
  change is seen. Decided by OP-01/D63.
- `OP-01/O32`: a note on the reasons of OP-01/D12 and D38, not a reopen
  (reader 1 3b, reader 2 3a, dossier). D12's check: "how many requests a
  retrying call makes is AX-13's, which therefore bounds the call's total";
  D38's: "a call's waiting is at most the attempt limit times the bound plus
  the backoff". D39 restarts the limit "once bytes have flowed since the last
  resume", so one stream `read(-1)` may resume any number of times and no
  attempt limit bounds its total. D12's Decision ("Under a quiet environment
  ... returns or fails") and D41's NP1 and NP2 stand, so the spec writes their
  clauses as they are. To decide with the maintainer: whether both checks are
  narrowed to one request. Decided by OP-01/D59.
- `OP-01/O33`: a reopen of OP-01/D34's changed row against D26 (reader 1 1f
  and § 4, reader 2 1d and § 4, dossier). D34's table, which its dialog did
  not ask (it asked key refused's builtin parent), has "E1 to E5 against a
  pinned stream | changed", E4 included. D26: under E4 "D21's single instant
  and D22's pinning are deliberately not promised", "the call may complete
  without failing", and it rejected option (b), detection "failing changed on
  a difference". E4 arises on Local and SFTP only (D43), where Local's
  descriptor never drops (D42's dialog) and SFTP does not resume (D42). To
  decide with the maintainer: whether E4 leaves the row. Dependent: D34's
  changed row. Linked with `OP-01/O15` and `OP-01/O28`, which reopen the same
  row. Blocks the spec clause typing changed. Decided by OP-01/D48.
- `OP-01/O34`: a reopen of OP-01/D40 against D2 (reader 1 1d, dossier). D2
  adopts "the file object of (a)", whose methods include "`readline` and line
  iteration"; D40 bounds an open stream to "a per-store buffer bound,
  independent of the item's size, plus what the caller's `read(n)` or chunk
  size asks for". A line asks for no size: on an `io.BufferedReader` over a
  raw stream of 10,000,000 bytes with no newline, `readline()` and the first
  line of iteration each return all 10,000,000 bytes (CPython 3.13.17 ✓). To
  decide with the maintainer: whether D40 bounds a line, `readline` takes a
  limit, or a line is registered beside NP8. Dependent: D2, D18's `readline`
  column. Linked with `OP-01/O25`. Blocks D40's spec clause. Decided by
  OP-01/D71.
- `OP-01/O35`: a reopen of OP-01/D22 against D9 (reader 1 1g, reader 2 1e).
  `OP-01/O20`'s history, a GET answering 404 and a listing after O3 created
  `d` and then `d/f`, meets a stream's open too: D22's "the open classifies at
  one instant t₀ inside the open call (D9's rows)". O20 reopens D21 only and
  blocks "D21's spec clause for a failed read". To decide with the
  maintainer: with O20, the same answer for the open. Dependent: D22, D35.
  Blocks D22's spec clause for a failed open. Decided by OP-01/D66.
- `OP-01/O36`: a reopen of OP-01/D18's Open row against D7 and D33 (reader 1
  § 4, reader 2 2c). D18's table, which its dialog did not ask (it asked the
  lifetime cell), says of an Open stream "a failure moves to Failed"; D16's
  dialog asked about "a transport failure". D7: "A non-positive size is a
  caller error"; D33 has caller errors raise `TypeError` or `ValueError` and
  `MemoryError` propagate "as raised". Read literally, `chunks(0)` leaves the
  stream Failed, every later read re-raising the `ValueError` (D16). To decide
  with the maintainer: which failures move Open to Failed. Dependent: D16,
  D18. Blocks the spec clause for the Failed transition. The Exhausted cell's
  `b''` for `readinto`, which returns 0 there (CPython 3.13.17 ✓), is
  `OP-01/O25`'s. Decided by OP-01/D51.
- `OP-01/O37`: a gap in OP-01/D38's caller-level table (reader 2 2d, readers
  1 and 2 § 4). "Caller-level retry of a surfaced failure, by a new call",
  which D38's dialog did not ask, has no row for a decode failure under strict
  (D32), caller errors and memory or descriptor failures as raised (D33), or
  a cancellation (D20), all of which D34's table lists. To decide with the
  maintainer: each row. Blocks the spec clause for caller-level retry.
  Decided by OP-01/D60.
- `OP-01/O38`: a note on `OP-01/O11`'s summary (reader 1 2d, reader 2 2i).
  O11 says D38 never retries "a classification, access or changed failure";
  D38's list also has key refused and not readable, which D30 places "not
  access". To decide with the maintainer: whether O11 carries D38's list when
  it lands. Decided by OP-01/D74.
- `OP-01/O39`: a gap in OP-01/D35's open-call list beyond `OP-01/O26`'s
  (reader 2 2e). D35: "Key refused, not found, not an item, permission
  denied, authentication failed and not readable surface from the open call";
  O26 adds unavailable and timeout. The list also lacks a cancelled open
  (D20: "A cancelled whole read or stream open hands out nothing"), a caller
  error raised before contact (D33) and E13's disk I/O, the base category
  (D33). To decide with the maintainer: with O26, the whole list. Dependent:
  D35. Blocks the spec clause for the open's failures. Decided by
  OP-01/D46.
- `OP-01/O40`: a note on the record's fidelity to its log (reader 1, outside
  the brief's four). Log f2ddab07's answer copies "each listed option's
  consequence and recommendation's reason verbatim from bcb85b21". D8's
  dialog option ends "on Local the third is NotADirectoryError, so one
  category hides that backend split. The distinction stays where it matters,
  for writes", which neither D8's option (a) nor its Recommendation carries
  (Local's `open('a.txt/b')` raises `NotADirectoryError`, errno 20, CPython
  3.13.17 ✓). D42's dialog option, "Honest: an SFTP stream that loses its
  connection is Failed; the caller restarts. Normal value stays 'resume
  pinned'", is not in D42's Recommendation. No sweep of the other decisions
  ran. To decide with the maintainer: whether both are restored. Decided by
  OP-01/D75.
- `OP-01/O41`: a reopen of OP-01/D12 and D13 against D14's reason and D1
  (primed). E7 includes "name resolution fails", and D12 bounds connecting by
  T_connect. D14's reason for Local's deviation: "the library cannot time out
  a blocked syscall without a helper thread", and its rejected option (b): "a
  timed-out syscall leaves a blocked thread behind". Name resolution is such a
  call: CPython 3.13.17's `socket.create_connection`, urllib3 2.8.0's
  `create_connection` and paramiko 5.1.0's `SSHClient.connect` call
  `getaddrinfo`, which takes no timeout, before any `settimeout` ✓. The sync
  form's bound on resolution is then the system resolver's own (not run).
  asyncio's `getaddrinfo` runs in an executor ✓, so the async form can time
  out the await, leaving the thread behind; D1's check binds both forms
  alike. To decide with the maintainer: whether T_connect covers resolution,
  at a helper thread's cost, or resolution joins D43's Termination row as a
  deviation. Dependent: D1, D13, D43's Termination row. Linked with
  `OP-01/O27`. Blocks the spec clause for T_connect. Decided by OP-01/D57.
- `OP-01/O42`: a note for the sessions `OP-01/O6` names (primed). D21 holds
  only if every API mutation is atomic as a reader sees it. SFTP's `RENAME`
  needs a target that does not exist (paramiko 5.1.0 `SFTPClient.rename`:
  newpath "must not exist already" ✓), so an atomic replace needs the
  `posix-rename@openssh.com` extension (`posix_rename` ✓). 0.33's SFTP backend
  falls back to displacing the target first whenever `posix_rename` fails for
  a reason `_probe_is_futile` does not recognise on a target that is not a
  directory, "so it is not confined to servers lacking the extension"
  (`_sftp.py:1071` to `:1080`, `:2285` to `:2296` and `:2322` to `:2329` ✓
  read): a window in which no item is at the key. To decide in those
  sessions: a declared deviation, or never falling back to a displacing
  rename, whether or not the extension is present. Amended by OP-01/D108. Amended by OP-01/D167. 0.33 citation dropped by OP-01/D167.
- `OP-01/O43`: a reopen of OP-01/D23 and D25 against Local on a network file
  system (primed; every backend claim not run). D14's scenario puts a Local
  store on an NFS mount. With two handles on two hosts over one export, the
  NFS client caches lookups and attributes, so after O2's rename on one host
  the other may open the old file until its cache expires, against D23's
  real-time order; and an `rm` from another host can fail the open
  descriptor's next read with `ESTALE`, against D25's "an open file
  descriptor keeps the old file". To decide with the maintainer: declared
  deviations in D43, or a network file system outside Local's profile.
  Dependent: D43. Linked with `OP-01/O19`. Blocks the spec clauses for
  Local's read-after-write and pinning. Decided by OP-01/D64.
- `OP-01/O44`: a gap in the Environment and OP-01/D22 (primed; the backend
  claim not run). No row covers O3 changing an item's metadata or properties
  without its content. On Azure every write to a blob, a metadata change
  included, gives it a new ETag, so a resume conditioned on the ETag fails
  changed while the content is still the open's. D22's condition is "If the
  backend can no longer serve that content", and D31's reason says "a new
  call reads the new content". To decide with the maintainer: an Environment
  row, and whether a pin token's change counts as the content's. Dependent:
  D19's table, D22, D31. Blocks the spec clause for changed on Azure.
  Decided by OP-01/D62.

`OP-01/O45` was found by the table check of RFC-0029 R1 on 2026-10-29 (BK-504,
`hatch run observe-tables` against `sdd/observe/tables/OP-01.toml`) and is a
note. Its other findings, five of D34's categories with no row in D38's
caller-level table, are `OP-01/O37`'s.

- `OP-01/O45`: a note on OP-01/D18's table, not a reopen. D18's table is "The
  stream's transitions, the open cells excepted", and D18 leaves cancellation
  out of it on purpose: "Cancellation is AX-7's." D20's check "adds a
  transition to D18's table: Open, any read, cancelled → Closed", which the
  table does not show. D20's Decision decides the behaviour in every state:
  "a cancelled stream read releases the stream, which is then Closed (D15)",
  so the spec writes a cancelled read's transition from D20. To decide with
  the maintainer: whether D18's table shows it, and whether D20's check, which
  names Open only, names every state as its Decision does. Decided by
  OP-01/D50.

`OP-01/O46` was raised by the reopen of 2026-10-30 (`OP-01/D65`).

- `OP-01/O46`: for the sessions of OP-03, OP-04, OP-10 and OP-12. OP-01/D65
  declares that on Local under Windows an API replace, delete or move of an
  item may fail while an OP-01 read holds it open. Each session decides the
  category that failure surfaces and whether its own clauses carry the
  deviation. Blocks nothing here. Amended by OP-01/D103.

`OP-01/O47` to `O64` were found by `/observe-review OP-01` on 2026-10-31,
the re-run after the reopen of 2026-10-30 that RFC-0029 R2 requires before a
freeze. Each names its source: reader 1 or 2 with the finding's label, the
primed review, or the table check (`hatch run observe-tables`). `O47` to
`O53`, `O55` and `O63` run as reopens, `O54`, `O58` and `O59` as gaps, and
`O56`, `O57`, `O60` to `O62` and `O64` are notes. A backend, SDK or runtime
claim is marked ✓ where this review read its source or ran it, else not run.

- `OP-01/O47`: a reopen of OP-01/D65 against D10, D20 and D24 (reader 1 1.3
  and § 4 D65, reader 2 1b, 1c and § 4 D65). D65: "under the deviation an
  open read can change the outcome of an overlapping mutation, never the
  state it leaves". A replace or delete that fails because a read holds the
  item leaves v1 or the item where O2's mutation alone would leave v2 or
  nothing, so the state left does change, against D10's "those the
  environment alone leaves" and D20's "changes nothing (D10)". D24's adopted
  option has each call "behaving as if issued alone"; under the deviation
  O2's call fails only because O1's overlaps it, and `OP-01/O8` is not
  amended. No dialog asked D65's D10 sentence: its option says "Readers keep
  their content". To decide with the maintainer: how D10 and D24 state the
  deviation. Dependent: D10, D20, D24, `OP-01/O8`. Linked with `OP-01/O63`.
  Blocks the spec clauses for D10's frame and D24 on Local under Windows.
  Decided by OP-01/D81.
- `OP-01/O48`: a reopen of OP-01/D64 against D43 (reader 1 1.4, reader 2
  1d). D43: "selected by the store's configuration where one backend serves
  services that differ"; D64 rejected option (c), "a configuration-selected
  declaration", and its check reads "Static per D43: no configuration
  selects it". Local on a local disk and on a network file system is one
  backend serving services that differ. With no probe (D43) and no
  configuration, a Local store declares eventual everywhere, against D64's
  reason "without penalising local disks", or strong, a deviation undeclared
  on NFS. `OP-01/O12` restates D43's rule unamended. To decide with the
  maintainer: whether configuration selects Local's Read-after-write
  declaration, or D43 admits a declaration conditioned on the file system.
  Dependent: D43, `OP-01/O12`. Linked with `OP-01/O63`. Blocks Local's
  Read-after-write row in OP-01's profile table. Decided by OP-01/D82.
- `OP-01/O49`: a reopen of OP-01/D62 against D47 and D61 (reader 1 1.5, 3.1
  and 3.2, reader 2 1e and 3b; every backend claim not run). E15 "Moves an
  item to a tier", E16 "Changes an item's metadata or system properties
  without its content": a tier is a system property, so one archive move
  fits both rows. D47 has a resume under E15 fail not readable, for the
  reason "a tier change keeps the ETag, not run"; D62: "a metadata-only
  change also fails a resume changed (Azure)", because "Azure's changes on
  any write". D61's recommendation, "changed claims a change the library
  cannot know of", and its check, "changed still means a change the backend
  proved", are false under D62's deviation, where changed fires on the
  content the open saw; D62 amends D31's reason, not D61's. To decide with
  the maintainer: which category a tier change surfaces where both rows
  hold, whether E15 and E16 are made disjoint, and D61's reason. Dependent:
  D47, D61, the Environment's E15 and E16. Blocks the spec clauses for
  changed and not readable on Azure. Decided by OP-01/D84 and OP-01/D85.
- `OP-01/O50`: a reopen of OP-01/D67 against D4, D34, D45 and D76 (reader 1
  1.8, 1.9, 2.1, 2.9 and § 4 D67, reader 2 1f, 1g, 2e, 2f and § 4 D67, the
  table check). D67 amends D9 and D19 only. D4: "every other pre-state is a
  failure", while under E17 D67 admits "the content of the object stored at
  exactly k". D34's check, "Every row of the Environment ... has a
  category", misses E17: its table has no E17 row (observe-tables: "row 17
  of Environment column 1 ('`OP-01/E17`') matches no member"). D67: E17 "is
  exposed at s2 and s3", its outcome one of "the content ..., not found, not
  an item", which D45 bars from a stream read; no dialog asked the exposure,
  or the scope "a key at or above k", where the option says "that key". E17
  arises on object stores only, the shape of D43's "O3 in-place
  modification" point, yet D76's five points name none for it. To decide
  with the maintainer: D4's and D34's rows for E17, what a stream read
  surfaces under it, and whether it is a variation point. Dependent: D4,
  D34, D45, D76, `OP-01/O13`. Blocks D9's and D34's spec clauses on object
  stores. Decided by OP-01/D88, OP-01/D89, OP-01/D90 and OP-01/D91.
- `OP-01/O51`: a reopen of OP-01/D80 against D15, D16, D18 and D52 (reader 1
  1.2 and § 4 D80, reader 2 1a and § 4 D80, primed). D80's adopted option:
  "Argument checks precede the state ... One rule for every state"; its
  Decision: "In every state but Closed, argument checks come first", which
  leaves a caller error on a Closed stream undecided. On a closed
  `io.BufferedReader`, `read('x')` raises `TypeError` and `read(2)`
  `ValueError` (CPython 3.13.17 ✓), so `io`, whose contract D70 adopts,
  checks arguments first there too. "A valid call on a Failed stream raises
  the failure again (D52)" also covers `close()`, which D18's close column
  and D16's option ("close works") have release, and `tell`, `readable` and
  `seekable` (D69, D70). D80 amends D51 only: D52's "Every read on a Failed
  stream raises" and D16's and D18's "the same failure again" are not
  narrowed to valid calls. To decide with the maintainer: the Closed state's
  order, and which calls a Failed stream re-raises on. Dependent: D15, D16,
  D18, D52. Blocks the spec clauses for argument checks on a Failed or
  Closed stream. Decided by OP-01/D92 and OP-01/D93.
- `OP-01/O52`: a reopen of OP-01/D35 and D46 against D79 (reader 1 1.1, 2.8
  and 3.4, reader 2 2d, primed). D35's "a whole read surfaces everything at
  its one call" stands: D45 and D46 amend D35, and D79 amends D46 only, with
  "every row of D34's table except changed and a closed stream's
  `ValueError`". D79's reason, that the closed-stream row "is a stream's",
  holds for D46's open-call list too, which keeps "every category of D34's
  table except changed and a decode failure". D46's check, "the two lists
  overlap only in what any backend request can meet", is false: both lists
  hold the caller errors, raised "before any backend contact" (D33), and a
  cancellation. To decide with the maintainer: D35's and D46's lists as D79
  leaves them. Dependent: D35, D46. Blocks the spec clauses for the open's
  and a whole read's failures. Decided by OP-01/D96.
- `OP-01/O53`: a reopen of OP-01/D59 against D12, D41 and D73 (reader 1 1.10
  and 3.3, reader 2 3a). D59: "a whole read's or an open's waiting is at
  most the attempt limit times the bound plus the backoff". D73 leaves how
  many requests a classification takes "not promised (NP2)", each with its
  own attempts (D38: "per backend request"), and T_idle fails a request only
  "when no byte arrives for T_idle" (D12), which a trickle never meets.
  D41's NP1 scope, "every call and stream read; D12 bounds waiting, not
  duration", is false for a stream read after D59's "a stream's total
  waiting is not bounded"; D59 does not amend D41. To decide with the
  maintainer: what bounds a whole read's or an open's waiting, if anything,
  and NP1's scope. Dependent: D12's and D38's checks, D41. Blocks the spec
  clauses for T_idle and NP1. Decided by OP-01/D97.
- `OP-01/O54`: a gap in the Environment (reader 1 2.2). No row covers O2
  changing an item's metadata through OP-20 ("Attach and read item metadata:
  user metadata and system properties", offer optional), whose reports D10
  names: E16 is O3's, and D62's changed row, "E1, E2, E3, E5, and E16 where
  the deviation is declared", has no O2 member, though on Azure the same
  change would fail a resume changed (not run). Nor does a row cover O3
  creating an item at an absent key: E3 "Replaces an item's content", E5
  "Deletes ... or puts a folder where an item was"; D63's scenario ("a sync
  tool PUTs `a`") and D66's ("O3 then PUTs objects") use it, and D63 covers
  "an O3 change of E3 or E5" only. To decide with the maintainer: both rows
  and their outcomes. Dependent: the Environment, D62's changed row, D63.
  Blocks the spec clauses for changed on Azure and for when an O3 creation
  is seen. Decided by OP-01/D86 and OP-01/D87.
- `OP-01/O55`: a reopen of OP-01/D60's caller-error row against D34 and D53
  (reader 1 3.5, primed). D60: "caller error | repeats it: a function of the
  arguments". D34's row is "caller error, closed stream", and D53 counts "a
  closed stream" among the caller errors; a closed stream's `ValueError`
  follows from the stream's state, and a new OP-01 call opens a new stream,
  which does not repeat it. To decide with the maintainer: the closed
  stream's row. Dependent: D60. Blocks the spec clause for caller-level
  retry. Decided by OP-01/D98.
- `OP-01/O56`: a note on `OP-01/O46`'s sessions (reader 1 2.6, primed). O46
  is "for the sessions of OP-03, OP-04, OP-10 and OP-12"; D65's deviation
  covers "an API replace, delete or move of an item", which OP-05, OP-11 (a
  folder tree holding the item) and OP-13 to OP-15 also make: O6 names "OP-03
  to OP-05 and OP-12 to OP-15" as the content-changing mutations. To decide
  with the maintainer: whether O46 names them. Blocks nothing here. Decided
  by OP-01/D103.
- `OP-01/O57`: a note on summaries and pointers the reopen left (reader 2 2g,
  2h and 3e). D74 amended `OP-01/O11` because "the summary is what that flow
  reads first"; the same holds of `OP-01/O4` (not D57's name resolution or
  D58's caller deadline), `OP-01/O7` ("across API mutations and reads", not

  D34, not D48, D53 to D56, D61 or D62) and `OP-01/O12` (D64). D43's
  "Decided by" column still reads "D12, D14", "D23" and "D39, D42", though
  D57, D62, D64 and D76 change those rows. `OP-01/O5` points to "OP-01/D18's
  last column", and D50 adds a column without saying where. To decide with
  the maintainer: whether they are amended. Blocks nothing here. Decided by
  OP-01/D104 and OP-01/D105.
- `OP-01/O58`: a gap in the method lists of OP-01/D18, D69 and D70 (reader 2
  2b and 2c, readers 1 and 2 § 4 D69, primed). D18's column "`read`,
  `readinto`, `readline`, chunks" lacks `read1`, which D70 adds and which
  "draws from the one position"; D70 amends D18's Exhausted cell only. D69
  makes `read`, `readinto`, `readline`, `read1` and `close` coroutines and
  `tell`, `readable` and `seekable` plain methods; `seek`, `truncate` and
  `write`, which D70 has raise `io.UnsupportedOperation`, are in neither
  list. No dialog asked D69's plain-methods sentence: its option reads "Same
  methods, awaited". To decide with the maintainer: `read1`'s place in
  D18's table and the async form of the other methods. Dependent: D18, D69,
  D70. Blocks the async stream's spec clauses. Decided by OP-01/D100 and
  OP-01/D101.
- `OP-01/O59`: a gap in reopen text no dialog asked (readers 1 and 2 § 4,
  reader 1 2.5, primed). D49: "The stream is Closed after a cancelled
  `close()` (D50)", and D50's column "a read or `close` is cancelled", where
  D50's option reads "a read is cancelled" and D49's dialog asked whether the
  cancellation propagates. D62: E16 "is exposed at s3 only, through a
  resume", while D22's dialog has a stream need a new request on "connection

  failure raised as is (D33), the new exception is of the original's type",
  where its option says "of the same category". To decide with the
  maintainer: each. Dependent: D49, D50, D52, D62. Blocks the spec clauses

- `OP-01/O60`: a note on E4 against E12 (reader 1 1.7). D28: "A short or
  corrupt body is never returned as content", E12 being "fewer bytes than
  announced". Under E4 on Local or SFTP a truncated file also yields fewer
  bytes than its size at open, which D26 ("the read ends at the end it
  meets") and D48 ("it may end short without failing") return as content.
  The record does not say whether such a truncation counts as E12. D26 and
  D28 stay decided, so the spec writes E12 from D28 and E4 from D26. To
  decide with the maintainer: whether D26's scope excludes E12 on
  file-system backends. Decided by OP-01/D99.
- `OP-01/O61`: a note on OP-01/D68's list (reader 1 2.7). D68: "though D4
  and D9 cite KEY-001, KEY-013, TREE-001 and TREE-002". D9 also cites
  KEY-002, KEY-004 and KEY-005, which `KeyModel.lean` tags (l.256, l.161,
  l.265 ✓), and D53, decided in the reopen, rests on KEY-008, tagged there
  too (l.46 ✓), while the Session's Reopen line says that reopen read "the
  TREE-001 and KEY-013 clauses". To decide with the maintainer: whether
  D68's list and the reopen's reading are completed. Blocks nothing here.
  Decided by OP-01/D106.
- `OP-01/O62`: a note on the record's fidelity to its log, beyond
  `OP-01/O40` (reader 2 § 4, unpaired dialogs). Log f2ddab07's answer, "copy
  each listed option's consequence and recommendation's reason verbatim
  from bcb85b21", is unfinished beyond D8 and D42 (D75). D41's dialog option
  ends "a test that asserts one is a defect", which D41's Recommendation
  lacks; D43's dialog option reads "(S3-compatible endpoints)" and "an
  unnamed non-AWS endpoint gets the deviations (eventual, no pinned
  resume)", which D43's option (a) drops. D75's dialog left a sweep to

  dialog claim by claim, and no word-by-word sweep ran. To decide with the

  here. Decided by OP-01/D107.
- `OP-01/O63`: a reopen of the scope of OP-01/D64 and D65 (primed; every
  backend claim not run). Both deviations name Local only. D14 declared "the
  server-side file system behind SFTP" for termination, since an SFTP
  server reads through its own OS. An SFTP server on Windows would then
  refuse a rename or remove of a file another of its sessions holds open, as
  D65 has Local do, and two SFTP servers over one network file system export
  would show D64's staleness across hosts, which D63 now brings under D23 as
  O3 changes. To decide with the maintainer: whether SFTP declares either

  OP-01's profile table. Decided by OP-01/D83.
- `OP-01/O64`: a note on the freeze (RFC-0029 R2). A record is frozen when
  each open item is decided, or is for another entry's session or tagged
  `shared:`, "each named with what it blocks". `OP-01/O3` to `O12` and `O42`
  name nothing they block (`O5` names what blocks it, OP-21's flow), so the
  record cannot be frozen even once `O47` to `O63` are decided. To decide

`OP-01/O65` to `O72` were found by `/observe-review OP-01` on 2026-11-01,
the re-run after the reopens of 2026-10-31 and 2026-11-01 that RFC-0029 R2
requires before a freeze. Each names its source: reader 1 or 2 with the
finding's label, the primed review, or the dialog-pairing report
(`hatch run observe-dialogs`). `O65` to `O68` run as reopens, `O71` as a gap,
and `O69`, `O70` and `O72` are notes. A backend, SDK or runtime claim is
marked ✓ where this review read its source or ran it, else not run.

- `OP-01/O65`: a reopen of OP-01/D101 and D108 against the maintainer's
  earlier answers to the same questions (the pairing report's unpaired
  dialogs, reader 1 4.14, reader 2 4i, primed). A worker restart delivered
  those answers into log `07bd66b7`, and a later session asked again. O58's
  async question was answered "All awaited (Recommended)" twice on
  2026-10-31 (`07bd66b7`, 09:33 and 12:07Z), its option ending "D69's
  plain-methods sentence withdrawn"; on 2026-11-01 (`fa0b580f`, 08:09Z) it
  was answered "I/O awaited, rest plain (Recommended)", which D101 adopts.
  O64 was answered "Blocks sentence each (Recommended)" on 2026-11-01
  (`07bd66b7`, 08:17Z), its option reading "a shared item its landing only,
  OP-01's spec stating the decision for OP-01"; at 10:21Z (`cbcf16f7`) it was
  answered "Per item, as listed (Recommended)", whose shared items hold back
  "OP-01's spec clauses for its own decisions", which D108 adopts. The
  Session names neither earlier answer, nor log `fa0b580f`. O52's and O58's
  `read1` questions were also answered twice, the options differing in form
  only: "Amend both" against D96's table, and "Into that column" and "One
  data-read column" against D100's citation of D93. To decide with the
  maintainer: which answer stands for the async methods, and for what a
  shared item blocks. Dependent: D69, D101, D108, D111, `OP-01/O3` to `O12`,
  `OP-01/O42`. Blocks the async stream's spec clauses and the freeze.
  Decided by OP-01/D114, OP-01/D115 and OP-01/D116.
- `OP-01/O66`: a reopen of OP-01/D92 and D93 against D15, D16, D18, D52 and
  D70 (reader 1 1.1 and 1.2, reader 2 1c and 1d). D80, as D92 amends it:
  "in every state, Closed included, argument checks come first: a caller
  error is raised and the state is unchanged". D93: on a Failed stream
  "`read`, `read1`, `readinto`, `readline`, line iteration and `chunks` raise
  the failure again (D52)"; D52's "Every read on a Failed stream raises" is
  narrowed by method, not to valid calls, and D16's "every later read raises
  the same category" and D18's Failed cell "the same failure again (D16)" are
  not amended. So `chunks(0)` on a Failed stream raises the failure under D52
  and D93 and a `ValueError` under D80. On a Closed stream D15's "reads raise
  `ValueError`", D18's Closed cell and D70's "on a Closed stream raises
  `ValueError` (D15)" stand, while D92 has `read('x')` raise `TypeError`.
  `OP-01/O51` named this ("not narrowed to valid calls"); its first dialog's
  answer, "Reads only" (`07bd66b7`, 2026-10-31 09:33Z), had "D16, D18, D52 and
  D80 narrowed to reads". To decide with the maintainer: whether D15, D16,
  D18, D52 and D70 read as D92 and D93. Dependent: D15, D16, D18, D52, D70.
  Blocks the spec clauses for a caller error on a Failed or Closed stream.
  Decided by OP-01/D117.
- `OP-01/O67`: a reopen of OP-01/D94 against D20 and D50 (reader 1 1.5,
  reader 2 1a). D50's column "a read or `close` is cancelled" reads "releases;
  Closed" for Open, Exhausted and Failed, and D20 "a cancelled stream read
  releases the stream". D94, as its adopted option reads: "leaves the stream
  Closed, its resources released or abandoned", and its check calls that
  consistent with "D50's column, as written ... so no amendment". An
  abandoned resource is not released, and D41's register has no row for when
  it is freed (NP4 covers an unclosed stream, D17). To decide with the
  maintainer: whether a cancelled `close()` releases, or D50's cell admits
  abandoning, with a register row. Dependent: D20, D41, D50. Blocks the spec
  clause for a cancelled close. Decided by OP-01/D118.
- `OP-01/O68`: a reopen of OP-01/D62's S3 premise (primed; the SDK's
  documentation read, the service not run). D62's reason: "S3 keeps the ETag
  on a metadata-only change (session input, Q4 Identity token)", so D43's
  Pinned resume deviation, "a metadata-only change also fails a resume
  changed", names Azure only. S3 changes an object's metadata by copying it
  onto itself, and botocore 1.43.117's S3 model ties the ETag to how an
  object was created: "If an object is created by either the Multipart Upload
  or Part Copy operation, the ETag is not an MD5 digest", while one created
  by "the PUT Object, POST Object, or Copy operation" and "encrypted by SSE-S3
  or plaintext" has "an MD5 digest of their object data" ✓ read. A metadata
  change on a multipart-uploaded object would then give it a new ETag over
  the same content, and a resume pinned by ETag (s3fs, session input) fail
  changed on S3 (not run), a deviation S3 does not declare (AX-15). The same model
  also says "The ETag reflects only changes to the contents of an object, not
  its metadata" ✓ read. E16, E18 through OP-20 (D86) and D84's tier move by
  copy meet it. To decide with the maintainer: whether S3 declares D62's
  deviation, conditioned or not, or pins by another token. Dependent: D43's
  Pinned resume row, D84, D86, D105's column. Blocks S3's Pinned resume row
  in OP-01's profile table. Decided by OP-01/D119.
- `OP-01/O69`: a note on OP-01/D105's Decided-by column (reader 1 2.5 and
  3.1, reader 2 2f, primed). D105's adopted option: "the column names every
  deciding decision per row". Pinned resume reads "D39, D42, D61, D62",
  without D77 ("where it declares no resume, a dropped stream fails
  unavailable under E15") or D84 (under E15, "where D62's deviation is
  declared, fails not readable or changed"), while D47, D86 and D102 also fix
  what a resume surfaces; Dual key reads "D67, D91", without D88 to D90,
  which fix E17's row, exposure and scope; O3 in-place modification reads
  "D26, D99", without D48 ("Under E4 a read never surfaces changed");
  Termination reads "D12, D14, D57", without D97, which D104's O4 counts.
  D110's check calls the column one "which lists every decision touching the
  row, restatements included", and its scenario has the Read-after-write row
  name "D76 and D105"; the row has no D105. To decide with the maintainer:
  each row's list, and D110's premise. Blocks nothing here. Decided by
  OP-01/D120.
- `OP-01/O70`: a note on four claims of extent later decisions break (reader
  1 2.1, 2.6, 2.7 and 2.9, reader 2 2a, 2c and 2d). D109's list is "every
  AX-10 decision (headings) and every decision whose Amends names one of D27
  to D34", and D109, headed "(AX-10, end check)", is not on it. D111: "D38's
  caller-level table, as D60 and D98 amend it", while D61 amends it too
  ("D38's unavailable row adds"). D88's check: "Every Environment row now has
  a row in D34's table", and D34's "Every row of the Environment ... has a
  category"; E4 has had none since D48. D41's check: "each names the decision
  whose limit it marks"; NP8's scope, "whole bytes and text", and that of
  the row D71 adds name none. To decide with the maintainer: each claim's wording.
  Blocks nothing here. Decided by OP-01/D121, OP-01/D122, OP-01/D123 and
  OP-01/D124.
- `OP-01/O71`: a gap in OP-01/D70, D93 and D101 (reader 1 2.8 and 3.2,
  reader 2 2g). D70 adopts "`io.BufferedIOBase`'s contract for every method
  it names", and `io.BufferedIOBase` has `readinto1` and `readlines`, both
  drawing from the position, and `writable`, `flush`, `fileno`, `isatty`,
  `writelines` and `detach` (CPython 3.13.17 ✓). D93's data-read list, the
  only one by D100, names neither of the first two, and D101's coroutine and
  plain-method lists name none of the eight. D100's reason, "one place
  enumerates the data reads, so the two cannot drift", meets D101, which
  enumerates them again for the async form. To decide with the maintainer:
  each method's value and async form, and where the data reads are listed.
  Dependent: D18 (through D93's list), D69. Blocks the file object's spec
  clauses for those methods. Decided by OP-01/D125, OP-01/D126 and
  OP-01/D127.
- `OP-01/O72`: a note on texts an amendment did not reach (reader 1 1.3, 3.3
  and 3.5, reader 2 1b, 1e, 3a, 3e and 3f). D97 rewrites "D59's check",
  while the text D59 wrote is D12's and D38's checks, "a whole read's or an
  open's waiting is at most the attempt limit times the bound plus the
  backoff", against D97's "No figure bounds a whole read's, an open's or a
  stream's total waiting"; neither D12's nor D38's Amended-by line names D97.
  D81 carries its exception into D10, D20, D24 and `OP-01/O8`, but not D58's
  "A cancelled whole read or open hands out nothing and changes nothing",
  D60's "a cancelled call leaves no effect (D20)", or the reasons of D10 ("it
  promises what every backend holds, success or failure") and D24 ("each call
  behaves as if issued alone"). D47's reason, "a tier change keeps the ETag,
  not run", stands beside D84, under which a tier move may fail changed
  where D62's deviation is declared. To decide with the maintainer: whether
  each text is amended. Blocks nothing here. Decided by OP-01/D128,

`OP-01/O73` to `O83` were found by `/observe-review OP-01` on 2026-11-02,
the re-run after the reopen of 2026-11-01 that walked `O65` to `O72` and
that RFC-0029 R2 requires before a freeze. Each names its source: reader A or
B with the finding's label, or the primed review. `O73` to `O76` run as
reopens, and `O77` to `O83` are notes. A backend, SDK or runtime claim is
marked ✓ where this review read its source or ran it (CPython 3.13.17 in the
hatch env), else not run.

- `OP-01/O73`: a reopen of OP-01/D126 against D78 and the `io` contract
  (reader A 1.1, reader B 1.1, primed). D126: `readinto1(b)` "writes 1 to
  min(len(b), r) bytes into b in at most one backend request", its option
  adding "as `read1`", and its check "consistent with ... D70 (`read1`'s
  one-request rule)". D78 withdrew that rule: "D70's "at most one backend
  request" is withdrawn. Retries and resumes apply as to `read` (D38, D39),
  and the request count is NP2's"; D41's NP2 leaves "The number of backend
  requests" unpromised for every call and stream. A reset inside
  `readinto1` resumed in the read (D39, D72) is the second request D126
  rules out and D78 allows. Separately, D126's `readlines(hint)` stops "once
  their total length reaches hint", and claims `io` semantics; `io` stops
  only once the total exceeds hint: `readlines(3)` over `b'ab\ncd\nef\n'`
  returns `[b'ab\n', b'cd\n']` ✓ run. To decide with the maintainer:
  `readinto1`'s wording beside D78's, and `readlines`' stopping rule.
  Dependent: D41's NP2, D70, D78, D93. Blocks the file object's spec clauses
  for `readinto1` and `readlines`. Decided by OP-01/D134 and OP-01/D135.
- `OP-01/O74`: a reopen of OP-01/D133 and NP9's scope as D126 sets it
  (reader A 2.3, reader B 2.3). D133: D40's bound "covers every read but those
  in NP9's scope: `readline` with a negative limit, `readlines` without a
  positive hint, and line iteration". By D126, `readlines(hint)` returns
  "`readline()` results", each a `readline()` with no limit, which NP9 (D71)
  has hold the whole line; `readlines(10)` over 1,000,000 bytes with no
  newline returns one line of 1,000,000 bytes ✓ run. So `readlines` with a
  positive hint is unbounded too, and D40 claims to bound it. To decide with
  the maintainer: whether NP9's scope takes `readlines` at any hint, or the
  hint bounds the line read. Dependent: D40, D41, D71, D126. Blocks D40's
  and NP9's spec clauses. Decided by OP-01/D136.
- `OP-01/O75`: a reopen of OP-01/D125 and D117 against `io` on a Closed
  stream (primed). D117: on a Closed stream "every valid call but `close()`
  raises `ValueError`"; D125, whose option adopts "`io`'s defaults",
  "`writable()` and `isatty()` return false" and "On a Closed stream each
  raises `ValueError` (D117)". On a closed `io.BufferedReader`, and on a
  closed `io.BufferedIOBase` subclass, `writable()` returns `False`;
  `isatty`, `flush`, `writelines`, `readable` and `tell` raise `ValueError`
  on `BufferedReader` ✓ run. One of D125's two promises breaks for
  `writable()`. To decide with the maintainer: whether a Closed stream's
  `writable()` returns false, as `io` does, or raises. Dependent: D15, D70,
  D93. Blocks the spec clauses for the six methods on a Closed stream.
  Decided by OP-01/D137.
- `OP-01/O76`: a reopen of OP-01/D118 on a backend whose SDK is synchronous
  (primed). D118: a cancelled `close()` "leaves the stream Closed with every
  local resource released, the connection aborted rather than returned to
  its pool", its option adding "so nothing waits for garbage collection", and
  its source "asyncio `Transport.abort()` closes at once". SFTP's SDK is
  paramiko (pyproject's `sftp` extra ✓ read), which is synchronous, so the
  async form runs its close in a worker thread, as
  `src/remote_store/aio/_sync_adapter.py` does with `asyncio.to_thread`.
  Cancelling an `asyncio.to_thread` call propagates the cancellation while
  the thread runs on: a 0.3 s close cancelled at 0.05 s had not finished when
  the `CancelledError` arrived, and finished later ✓ run. The close then
  completes, or returns the connection to its pool, after the caller is told
  the stream is Closed and released; an abort needs a call into the SDK's
  socket from the event loop's thread, not run. To decide with the
  maintainer: whether D118 holds on such a backend at that cost, or declares
  a deviation (release completes after the cancellation). Dependent: D20,
  D41's NP5, D50, D94. Blocks the spec clause for a cancelled close.
  Decided by OP-01/D138. Amended by OP-01/D167. 0.33 citation dropped by OP-01/D167.
- `OP-01/O77`: a note on two tier and metadata texts (reader A 1.2, reader B
  1.5, primed). D119's adopted option: S3 declares the deviation "and a check
  on S3 accepts either outcome"; its Decision, "a metadata-only change also
  fails a resume changed (Azure, S3; not run)", and D86's "a stream read
  fails changed where D62's deviation is declared", which admit no passing
  resume. On S3 a single-part plaintext or SSE-S3 object keeps an MD5 ETag
  across a copy (`OP-01/O68` ✓ read), so the resume may pass. D130's D47
  reason: "where D62's deviation is declared (Azure, S3 by D119), a tier

  Azure's tier move is Set Blob Tier (`BlobClient.set_standard_blob_tier`
  calls `blob.set_tier`, azure-storage-blob in the hatch env ✓ read), not a

  with the maintainer: each text's wording. Blocks nothing here. Decided by
  OP-01/D139 and OP-01/D140.

- `OP-01/O79`: a note on the Session's provenance lines (reader A 2.4, reader

- `OP-01/O80`: a note on the AX-13 row (reader A 2.5, reader B 2.5). D113: "a

`OP-01/O84` to `O91` were found by `/observe-review OP-01` on 2026-11-02,
the re-run after that day's reopen that walked `O73` to `O83` (D134 to
D150) and that RFC-0029 R2 requires before a freeze. Each names its source:
reader A or B with the finding's label, or the primed review. `O84` to `O87`
run as reopens or gaps, and `O88` to `O91` are notes. A backend, SDK or
runtime claim is marked ✓ where this review read its source or ran it
(CPython 3.13.17 in the hatch env), else not run.

- `OP-01/O84`: a reopen of OP-01/D150 against D93's list (reader A 1.1, 2.1,
  3.2 and 4.1, reader B 1.1 and 4.1). D93's Decision named the data reads:
  "On a Failed stream, `read`, `read1`, `readinto`, `readline`, line
  iteration and `chunks` raise the failure again (D52)", and D126 has
  `readinto1` and `readlines` "join D93's data-read list". D150's Decision
  rewrites D93's whole: "on a Failed stream, every data read (D93's list)
  raises the failure again (D52)", read "as D125 amends it", not as D126
  does. No Decision now enumerates the data reads, while D131 has them
  "enumerated in D93 only", and D100's column, D101's coroutine rule (D127,
  D147), D117's Failed rule and D148's reach all cite "D93's list". D150's
  dialog offered the unsupported family only ("the methods D70 and D125
  have raise io.UnsupportedOperation, as on an Open one"). D150's rewrite
  also drops D93's "D52's "every read" and D80's "a valid call on a Failed
  stream" mean those data reads", and leaves `writable`, `isatty` and
  `flush` to D125's own sentence, while its check says "D125 (its six answer
  on a Failed stream as on an Open one)". To decide with the maintainer:
  D93's Decision text, and where the eight data reads are enumerated.
  Dependent: D18, D100, D101, D117, D125, D126, D127, D131, D132, D147,
  D148. Blocks the Failed state's spec clauses and every clause that cites
  D93's list. Decided by OP-01/D151.
- `OP-01/O85`: a reopen of OP-01/D81's scope against D118 and D138 (reader
  B 1.2, reader A 3.5). D81: "where an open OP-01 read, whole or streamed,
  may make an overlapping API replace, delete or move of that item fail";
  D83 declares that deviation for "SFTP, where the server runs on Windows".
  D118 puts into NP5 "what a backend still holds on its side after a
  cancelled `close()` (an SFTP file handle until its session ends)", and
  D138 has SFTP's stream "Closed when the cancellation propagates", "the
  release completes when the worker's close returns". So on a Windows SFTP
  server a Closed stream's handle may make an O2 replace or delete fail:
  seen through an API call, not out of band (NP5), and from a stream that
  is no longer an open read (D81). Separately, D118's NP5 example rests on
  an aborted connection, while under D138's deviation the worker's close
  runs on to the end. Server behaviour not run. To decide with the
  maintainer: whether D81's exception reaches a Closed stream whose release
  is pending, and NP5's SFTP example. Dependent: D10, D24, D41's NP5, D83,
  D118, D138. Blocks D10's frame clause, NP5's and the cancelled close's
  spec clauses. Decided by OP-01/D152.
- `OP-01/O86`: a gap in D34's and D96's tables, and a reopen of OP-01/D117
  against `io` (reader A 2.7 and 2.8, reader B 2.4, primed). D34 lists "The
  categories OP-01 surfaces", its check "so none surfaces untyped", its row
  "caller error, closed stream | `TypeError`, `ValueError` | builtin itself".
  D70 and D125 have six methods raise `io.UnsupportedOperation`, a subclass
  of both `OSError` and `ValueError` ✓ run, which has no row in D34, D96 or
  D38's caller-level table as D60 amends it. D55 amends D34 but adds
  `LookupError` to D33's caller errors only. D117 has "every valid call but
  `close()`" (and `writable()`, D137) raise `ValueError` on a Closed stream,
  `tell` and `isatty` included, while D96 gives "a closed stream's
  `ValueError`" the Stream read phase only, and D93 has those calls no data
  reads. A closed `io.BufferedReader`'s `write(b'x')` raises
  `io.UnsupportedOperation`, not the `ValueError` its other calls raise ✓
  run, and D70 adopts `io`'s contract. To decide with the maintainer: the
  category and phase of `io.UnsupportedOperation`, `LookupError` in D34's
  row, the phase of a non-read call on a Closed stream, and what `write`
  raises on a Closed stream. Dependent: D33, D34, D38, D60, D70, D96, D125.
  Blocks the category table OP-01 gives `OP-01/O9` and the spec clauses for
  the unsupported methods. Decided by OP-01/D153 and OP-01/D154.
- `OP-01/O87`: a gap in OP-01/D78 and D134 for a size that is not positive
  (primed). D78: "`read1(n)` returns 1 to min(n, r) bytes as soon as at
  least one is available, ... and `b''` at the end"; D134: `readinto1(b)`
  "writes 1 to min(len(b), r) bytes into b ... and returns that count, 0 at
  the end". With r > 0, neither range holds a value for `read1(0)`,
  `read1(-1)` or an empty buffer, and D132's Exhausted cell reads 0 as the
  end. `io`, whose contract D70 adopts: `read1(0)` returns `b''`, `read1()`
  and `read1(-1)` return up to one buffer (8192 bytes from a
  `BufferedReader` with that buffer size, the whole rest from
  `io.BytesIO`), `readinto1(bytearray())` returns 0 ✓ run. To decide with
  the maintainer: what each returns, or whether each is a caller error.
  Dependent: D18, D70, D132. Blocks the spec clauses for `read1` and
  `readinto1`. Decided by OP-01/D155.
- `OP-01/O88`: a note on `io` as D135's and D137's reference (primed).
  D135's recommendation: "D70 and D126 adopt `io`'s contract, and code
  written against `io` sees the same list"; D137's: "a closed
  `io.BufferedReader` returns false". `io`'s classes differ:
  `readlines(3)` over `b'ab\ncd\nef\n'` returns two lines from
  `io.BufferedReader` but `[b'ab\n']` from `io.BytesIO` and from `_pyio`'s
  classes, and a closed `io.BytesIO`, a `BufferedIOBase` subclass, raises
  `ValueError` from `writable()` ✓ run; `io.IOBase.readlines`' docstring
  says "exceeds hint" ✓ read. Memory's sync read hands out an `io.BytesIO`
  (`src/remote_store/backends/_memory.py`, ✓ read), evidence that a
  backend's natural object gives the other values. D135's and D137's
  Decisions are unchanged by it. To decide with the maintainer: whether
  their reasons name `io.IOBase`'s documented contract, so that a check is
  not built on `io.BytesIO`. Blocks nothing here. Amended by OP-01/D156. Routed to OP-01's spec flow by OP-01/D156. Amended by OP-01/D167. 0.33 citation dropped by OP-01/D167.
- `OP-01/O89`: a note on the count of variation points (reader A 2.2,
  reader B 2.1). D76: "`OP-01/O13` and D63's check read: D43's five
  variation points"; D91: "`OP-01/O13` and D76 read: D43's six variation
  points". D138 adds a seventh, Cancelled close, and amends `OP-01/O13`
  only, so D63's check, D76 and D91 still read six. To decide with the
  maintainer: each text. Blocks nothing here. Amended by OP-01/D156. Routed to OP-01's spec flow by OP-01/D156.
- `OP-01/O90`: a note on D43's Decided-by cells (reader A 2.6 and 4.2,
  reader B 2.2, 2.3 and 4.2). D120's rule: each row lists "every decision
  that changes what its normal value or a deviation lets a caller observe".
  D138's Cancelled close row reads "D50, D94, D118, D138" without D49 ("The
  stream is Closed after a cancelled `close()`"; E14 "reaches s4"), which
  D138's check cites, and D146's check says "D138's new row lists its own
  deciding decisions"; that cell and "D120's column gains the row as
  listed" are in no dialog. D146's Pinned resume cell lists D47 for E15 but
  not D45, whose reason is "a resume is a new request, which meets E6 and
  E15 as an open does". To decide with the maintainer: whether the cells
  take D49 and D45. Blocks nothing here. Amended by OP-01/D156. Routed to OP-01's spec flow by OP-01/D156.
- `OP-01/O91`: a note on reasons later decisions make false (reader A 3.1,
  reader B 3.2). D145 has D14's recommendation read "network backends keep
  the normal value, name resolution aside (D57)", while D14's Decision
  declares "the server-side file system behind SFTP once the SFTP request is
  sent" a deviation. D63's recommendation: D23's order for O3 "holds
  natively on S3, Azure and Local", while D64 makes Local eventual where its
  file system is a network one shared across hosts, and D83 makes SFTP so
